### Red-pen key and current assessment

Tags describe the **specific statement or bounded capability**, not the whole platform. Source evidence is in section 1. “In progress” means partially implemented and currently preserved; this review does not restart implementation.

| Tag | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **[COMPLETE]** | Named work is finished at its stated evidence level: documented decision, inspected implementation, or recorded test. Never implies deployed/accepted |
| **[IN PROGRESS]** | Useful implementation exists; the named interaction still has gaps |
| **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** | Reconcile existing owners/contracts/presentation; avoid rebuilding |
| **[NEEDS BUILD]** | Required behavior is absent from the inspected implementation |
| **[NEEDS DECISION D#]** | Product choice remains unresolved; section 15 identifies its consequence and recommendation |
| **[VERIFY]** | Evidence is insufficient or stale; do not call it complete or missing |
| **[ANSWERED]** | Existing code or explicit user direction resolves the question |
| **[NOTE]** | Correction, boundary, or implementation trap |
| **[PROPOSED]** | Concrete recommendation, not accepted policy or implemented behavior |
| **[DEFERRED]** | Outside the current MVP unless explicitly included in the supported capability scope |

**Assessment:** substantial foundations exist. The main work is unifying capabilities and interactions, completing public intake/data views, and proving reusable capabilities and their connections. A universal schema, 110 components, or another canvas engine would increase scope without proving the MVP.

**[COMPLETE]** This source/reference planning reconciliation and the reference-pack checks are done. **[VERIFY]** The full MVP is not proven complete. Local implementation, hosted checks, deployment and live acceptance remain separate.

**[NOTE]** An untagged normative sentence describes intended behavior, not current implementation. Section 1 is the sole implementation inventory, including source evidence; section 15 holds verification receipts and the single decision queue. Other sections annotate requirements without creating another completion register.

## 2. Core model

> **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** These are semantic contracts, not instructions to create eleven new tables. Adapt current sources first; migrate only where an explicit invariant cannot be supported.
>
> **[NOTE]** Existing Particle graph storage uses organization-scoped workspace identity; do not assume every `workspaceId` already means an independently owned canvas. Verify that boundary before multi-workspace adapters.

A **Particle** presents a record, saved query, or composition through registered views.

> **[ANSWERED]** A Particle is the visible, interactive representation. Record identity owns content; placement owns geometry; membership owns plan context. A source reference, a visual duplicate and a copied record are different operations.
>
> **[NOTE]** This separation is the target contract. Today intrinsic Particle content and geometry share a record; documents/people/opportunities also have canonical external owners. Expose that distinction rather than pretending all sources already behave identically.

| Concept | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| Organization | Tenant and ownership boundary |
| Workspace | Authorized working surface |
| Objective | Desired outcome |
| Opportunity | Source-backed possibility informing a plan |
| Plan | Memberships and relationships serving an objective |
| Record | Canonical identity, kind, schema version, revision, data, provenance |
| Placement | Record reference, workspace/plan, geometry, view, lock |
| Membership | Plan context, accountable owner, contributors |
| Proposal | Unapplied commands against source/target revisions |
| Execution | Action attempt, idempotency key, status, receipt |
| Publication | Authorized audience, fields, assets, content revision |

Adapt existing storage to these contracts; avoid duplicate data owners.

> **[NOTE]** Current proposals store proposed plan contents with base revisions; they are not already a generic command log. Convert through existing validators/application handlers. Do not migrate proposal history merely to match the word “commands.”
>
> **[NOTE]** `graph-validation.ts` currently rejects a second individual-person placement on one board. Reusing a person through plan membership or view aliases must not bypass that invariant. General multiple placements need an explicit compatibility change, not a UI-only duplicate.

Reuse records across authorized plans. Shared edits update canonical content; placement and contextual responsibility stay local. Disclose shared-edit scope; provide explicit copy/fork.

> **[ANSWERED]** Keep one canonical content owner. Edit the source through its existing editor; show “Used in 2 plans” when relevant. Editing a title/summary that belongs only to a view must be a separately named override. A copy receives a new identity and provenance; a second placement does not.
>
> **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** Define the supported fields for each case before sharing editors. An unapplied proposal must retain proposed content/revisions without changing the accepted shared source. Accepting several source edits may cross transactions; do not imply one atomic graph save covers them.

Moving changes placement. Detaching removes membership. Source deletion is separate and shows reference impact. Removing a Particle must not delete its uploaded document.

Scope every operation by server-derived actor, organization, workspace, and target. Personal/team/organization visibility follows permissions; reuse grants no additional access.

### A1. Agentic UI orchestration: system boundary

**Architecture consolidation pass 1 — specified, not implemented.** Coach House is the product. Its agentic UI orchestration framework connects planning, typed capabilities, governed operations and Particle presentation. The Particle design system defines the visual and interaction language within that framework. These are cooperating responsibilities, not four new applications or independent persistence systems.

This contract is self-contained. External products inform implementation choices; their documentation cannot substitute for Coach House behavior or permission rules. Pass 2 B1–B5 selects constrained json-render presentation and AG-UI transport adapters, while deferring full CopilotKit. These are planning selections, not installed or qualified integrations. Selecting a package cannot expand MVP scope, authorize a provider, or change the ownership below.

### A2. Single-owner responsibility contract

“Owner” means the one authoritative decision or write boundary. Client previews may mirror it; adapters may call it; neither becomes a competing authority. The existing source anchors below are integration destinations from section 1/O1–O8, not claims that the universal framework is already wired.

| Responsibility | Authoritative owner / existing destination | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Request admission and run coordination | Coach House server harness; `workspace-ai` research run/application owners, H1–H10 | Derives actor/scope, enforces budgets, tracks lifecycle, sequences validated work and reconciles receipts. A library run callback cannot mark domain work applied. |
| Planning and content generation | Model/provider adapter invoked by that harness | Proposes goals, content, relationships and focused changes within available capabilities. Cannot grant rights, assign canonical IDs, fabricate evidence or declare writes successful. |
| Bounded model judgment | Optional judgment adapter under section 6 | Chooses among supplied candidates or scores a declared rubric only when enabled and qualified. Does not replace the planner, deterministic validation or approval. |
| Context and retained knowledge | Authorized source readers under K1–K9; explicit memory owner only if enabled | Resolves relevant organization records and provenance. Agent conversation/shared frontend state is not canonical organizational memory. |
| Capability definitions | Existing Particle registry extended through source-specific registrations, section 3/U9 | Owns supported input/result schemas, views, sizes, semantic constraints and command declarations. Produces filtered model/client catalogs from the same definitions. |
| Authorization and policy | Each server source/command owner, existing authz/RLS and proposal authority | Enforces current actor, organization, source/field rights and required review. Capability availability and browser visibility are hints, not access checks. |
| Review and approval | Existing proposal/review owner plus consequence-specific command policy | Stores the exact reviewed revision and actor decision. A frontend approval interaction collects intent; the server determines whether it authorizes the operation. |
| Canonical content writes | Existing graph, note, document, opportunity, people, form or provider writer, O1–O8 | Owns revision checks, persistence, retry identity and confirmed result. Orchestration invokes it rather than writing its tables through a generic patch handler. |
| Generated presentation | Application-owned presentation adapter and registered components; `ParticleFrame`/`WorkspaceNodeFrame` and source renderers | Converts validated configuration into U1–U12 views. The selected json-render adapter implements applicable generated composition; styling, allowed composition and actions remain Coach House contracts. |
| Agent/frontend transport | Replaceable event adapter around H8's application events | Delivers correlated lifecycle, preview and result updates. The selected AG-UI adapter covers this boundary; full CopilotKit is deferred; transport state never commits source changes or grants permissions. |
| Board geometry | Existing React Flow integration plus measured ELK/coordinator | React Flow owns interaction/placement; layout coordinator owns measured managed placement. The model can propose semantic relationships, never authoritative pixel positions. |
| Personal navigation and editor drafts | Existing focus/window/session and source-editor controllers | Owns selection, camera, return stack and unsaved input. Incoming agent state cannot overwrite newer user drafts or collaborator revisions. |
| Publication and external access | Existing publication owner, O7, plus explicit asset/intake/guest adapters | Builds permitted projections and checks audience/grants. Sharing cannot serialize arbitrary private renderer state. |
| Usage and accounting | Existing `ai-credits` ledger and server admission/settlement | Records usage and enforces approved limits. Provider metrics inform settlement; UI estimates and agent messages are not purchase or debit authority. |

### A3. Boundary flow: generation, application and rendering

~~~text
User objective / focused change
  → server admission: actor + scope + request identity + budget
  → authorized context + currently available capability catalog
  → model/tool loop: proposed content, relationships and references
  → application validation: structure + semantics + access + revisions
  → proposal preview through registered Particle renderers
  → required user/role review or existing authorized apply policy
  → application coordinator invokes each canonical source command
  → source receipt + current authorized readback
  → committed view projection → registered renderers → measured layout

Validated preview / lifecycle events → replaceable transport → interface
Manual editing → same canonical source command → receipt/readback → interface
~~~

The preview branch is deliberately separate from committed content. A rendered form is not published intake; an accepted plan is not executed outreach; a received event is not a saved record. Manual edits do not require a model call or an agent runtime to save.

Tools during planning use K5/K6's effect classes. Reads retrieve evidence; separately authorized draft/write effects still pass their own canonical command boundary. A tool return that contains UI never bypasses validation or source permissions.

H1–H10 remain the detailed lifecycle authority. H5 governs cross-source partial success and revisions; H6 governs cancellation and uncertain outcomes; H8 governs correlated events. This diagram does not invent a second state machine or require new database tables. L1–L7 now defines the concrete operation/event fields and transition cases using these owners.

### A4. Resolve overlapping mechanisms

1. **One capability authority, multiple derived representations.** Source schemas stay with their existing feature owners. The extended registry binds them to presentation/actions. A json-render catalog, model tool schema and client availability list are generated/adapted from those registrations; none becomes a separately maintained source of truth. Server handlers remain outside serializable catalogs.
2. **Domain proposals and renderer specifications are distinct.** Domain proposals describe intended record/membership changes. Presentation specifications describe a permitted view of drafts or committed records. Do not replace stored Particle/domain schemas with a library's generic element tree. Persist user-owned view configuration where required; treat generated render trees as derived unless an explicit versioned view owner is defined.
3. **Run state is not board state.** Stream snapshots/deltas update scoped run/preview state. They cannot be applied directly as JSON patches to the organization graph, database records or editor drafts. Canonical content changes only through source commands. Reconnect restores authorized run status and source readback; it does not replay every streamed patch as a write.
4. **Agent/UI shared state is not team collaboration.** It may synchronize permitted selections, draft context and run progress. Concurrent team edits still use existing revisions/conflict handling. Introducing a second collaboration store or CRDT system is not authorized by choosing a transport library.
5. **Renderer actions request commands.** A registered button/input resolves to a permitted command adapter. No model-defined arbitrary handler, endpoint or script. Streaming may reveal validated inert preview units; incomplete operations cannot become executable. Local renderer state is not durable-save proof.
6. **Human-in-the-loop UI is not approval authority.** An approval component must carry the correct proposal/operation and expected revision to the existing server owner. Recheck permissions and staleness there. A second participant seeing a green check does not acquire authority from it.
7. **Composition is not canvas layout.** AI may select approved semantic variants/sections and propose allowed ordering. The application validates nesting/order constraints and owns exact spacing, typography, controls and board coordinates. A library's free-form layout primitives stay unavailable unless our grammar explicitly permits them.
8. **Libraries are implementation details at the boundary.** Dependency-specific events/types are translated at adapters. A package upgrade cannot silently reinterpret saved records, permissions or approved actions. Migration and compatibility checks are required where persisted contracts change; no parallel framework or speculative plugin system is needed.
9. **Provider choice, transport and rendering are independent.** Adopting json-render does not select an inference provider or require Vercel AI SDK migration. Adopting AG-UI does not itself require all CopilotKit services. Any actual package/service dependencies and data-processing consequences must be recorded in pass 2 and D6 before adoption.
10. **Shared rules, distinct surfaces.** Board, editor and public renderers use approved source/view contracts; they need not receive identical payloads or mount identical controls. Public rendering begins with a server-approved projection, never the private board tree with fields hidden by CSS.

### A5. Ownership acceptance and next boundary

This pass is complete when all responsibilities above have a single authority, the preview/commit distinction is explicit, manual and AI changes converge on source commands, and library candidates have bounded replaceable roles. It defines ownership; it does not prove runtime integration, resolve D3/D4/D6 or approve the whole plan.

The completed dependency evaluation is recorded in B1–B5: selected adapters, exact evaluated versions, retained owners, custom work replaced, integration work introduced and remaining qualification. These decisions preserve A2/A4; installation and runtime proof remain implementation work after full-plan approval.

## 3. Feature contract

> **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** Extend the existing `particleRegistry`; it already has schemas, defaults and sizes. The missing work is the common binding/command/view contract, not a registry from zero.
>
> **[ANSWERED]** Start with explicit typed registrations for the supported product capabilities. No plugin loader, marketplace or universal configuration language is required.

One application-owned capability registry drives UI availability and AI capabilities. As specified in A2/A4, source schemas remain with their feature owners; model catalogs, renderer catalogs and client availability are derived bindings, not independent registries.

~~~text
FeatureManifest
  id, version
  recordKinds, configurationSchema
  views, sizes, relationshipRules
  bindings: source, querySchema, editableFields
  commands: inputSchema, resultSchema, handler, permission, effectClass
  states, revisionPolicy, retryPolicy
  publicProjection
  loading, empty, unavailable, error
~~~

Manual controls and accepted AI proposals invoke the same validated command handlers. Views share authorized bindings and canonical writers.

> **[NOTE]** Split public capability descriptions from server handler/authentication implementations. The browser/model receives command names, bounded schemas and availability—not callable server functions, credentials or executable handler text. UI availability is not authorization.
>
> **[ANSWERED]** Version the capability contract and recorded proposal. Revalidate a stored proposal after registry changes; reject or explicitly migrate incompatible commands. Persist supported view configuration, not arbitrary JSX.

Expose capabilities as available, proposal-only, or unavailable with a reason. AI emits validated configuration and commands, never executable React, SQL, or arbitrary scripts.

**Initial functions:** work/actions, decisions, documents/references, forms, people/assignments, and collections for results. Include editable document, email, and selected social draft variants. Sending/publishing requires an implemented action.

> **[NOTE]** Email/social drafts reuse registered text owners. Drafting does not imply platform delivery, audience synchronization or specialized composers; those require separately enabled action contracts. A sample plan cannot silently expand implementation scope.

### B1. Dependency decision — consolidation pass 2

**Planning selection, not installation or runtime qualification.** Use **json-render core/React through a constrained Coach House adapter** for generated presentation. Use **AG-UI through a run-scoped transport adapter** for agent/frontend events. **Do not adopt the full CopilotKit frontend/runtime for this MVP.** Keep the existing OpenAI research adapter, source writers, credit ledger, React Flow and ELK. Adapter selection is settled; the complete specification still requires final approval before application work.

This decision follows primary documentation, pinned source inspection and published package metadata checked October 29, 2026. It selects implementation dependencies at the specification level; it does not claim a successful install, benchmark, security audit or end-to-end integration. Future implementation must pass B5 before enabling the adapters. A failed gate requires a recorded dependency/adapter correction, not a silent weakening of the product contract.

| Package / option | Decision | Replaces | Integration still required |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `@json-render/core` + `@json-render/react` **0.21.3** | **ADAPT / selected** | Bespoke UI-spec catalog/registry plumbing, element rendering and applicable stream assembly utilities | Derive the catalog from Coach House capabilities; wrap existing components; restrict composition; enforce source/command validation; preserve manual editing and recovery |
| `@ag-ui/core`, `@ag-ui/client`, `@ag-ui/encoder` **1.0.3** | **ADAPT / selected** | A proprietary agent event vocabulary and generic HTTP event-client/encoding plumbing | Bridge the existing run lifecycle to protocol events; authenticated run subscription; application sequence/recovery envelopes; preview-state isolation and receipt reconciliation |
| `@copilotkit/react-core` + `@copilotkit/runtime` **1.83.0** | **DEFER / not selected** | Could provide frontend agent hooks, approval interactions and runtime integration | Existing Coach House composer, proposal authority, run ledger and provider path already own much of this. Adopting the broader stack adds integration/state ownership work without presently demonstrated savings. Reconsider only for a named gap AG-UI cannot economically cover |
| Prebuilt json-render shadcn catalog, generic layout/action catalog, code export, full-app generation | **NOT selected** | No required custom work identified | Coach House semantic components and U1–U12 govern composition; Apps SDK UI is the selected base catalog. No route generator or arbitrary model layout controls |
| Experimental Jev composer | **DEFER / optional later qualification** | May reduce some bounded component-selection work | Requires prepared candidates, qualified judgment/provider access and demonstrated quality/cost benefit; not necessary for initial typed generation |

json-render provides catalog/registry/spec mechanisms and progressive rendering; those are library capabilities, not source authorization. CopilotKit documents frontend tools and agent/UI integration; a browser tool is still a client function, not a trusted data mutation. These distinctions motivate the narrow selection above. [json-render catalog](https://json-render.dev/docs/catalog), [registry](https://json-render.dev/docs/registry), [CopilotKit frontend tools](https://docs.copilotkit.ai/frontend-tools).

**Approved UI foundation — October 30:** adopt the full `@openai/apps-sdk-ui` component catalog as the base for Particle content, tools and editors. Coach House supplies specialized compositions using existing feature behavior. This replaces the earlier decision to retain shadcn as the target control catalog for these surfaces; installed shadcn/Radix remains a migration baseline, not a parallel target design system. No unrelated application-wide rewrite.

**November2 scope correction:** the user retained reviewed SDK editors/cards and prohibited expanded migration, broad rollback and unrelated cosmetic changes. This supersedes older migration-completion language; remaining work is functional requirements and focused regression verification.

- **Library versus AI catalog:** the complete library is available to implementers. AI receives typed semantic components with approved props, slots, bindings and actions, not arbitrary library props or executable handlers. Manual and generated UI use the same components. Component availability does not enable new product capabilities or external effects.
- **Authority:** Apps SDK UI owns base control styling and foundational tokens. Coach House owns Particle anatomy, semantic statuses, supported sizes, canvas geometry, connectors, assignments, navigation and permissions. Map existing U3/U5 tokens through one shared adapter; library defaults govern base controls, while explicit Particle geometry and status meanings remain application contracts. No per-feature competing theme.
- **Execution (latest user scope):** retain the reviewed SDK integration and use existing shared components for required functional work. Do not migrate additional existing surfaces or rewrite cosmetics. S11 verifies affected behavior and fixes demonstrated regressions; no catalog rewrite or wrappers for unused exports.
- **Proof — October 30 local checkpoint:** `@openai/apps-sdk-ui` is installed and exactly pinned at **0.2.2**, with integrity in the lockfile. The existing objective/plan title editor uses SDK Input/Button; plan options use SDK Menu. Existing source writers, permissions, proposals and persistence remain authoritative. Eight final browser checks cover actual editor save/reload/cancel, keyboard/focus return, light/dark portaled menus, 390px fit, 44px controls, reduced-motion configuration and existing blank-board/composer behavior. Twenty-six unchanged revision/recovery cases also passed before the final CSS qualification. This is local fixture evidence, not authenticated full-app, complete catalog, visual acceptance or production qualification.
- **Shared foundation:** `src/components/ui/particle-ui` is the control import boundary and scoped foundation adapter. At build time it reads the pinned package's base/primitive/semantic/component/global CSS, scopes its tokens/resets to `.particle-ui` and marked SDK menu portals, and maps theme selection to the existing `.dark` owner. It does not copy token values into a second design system or replace application-wide Tailwind breakpoints, typography or utilities. Tailwind scans the installed SDK; package component CSS modules remain unchanged. The adapter runs before Tailwind in both Next and the existing fixture. Additional SDK primitives/portal families must join this boundary when used; importing a new control does not qualify it automatically. No wrapping of the whole catalog upfront.
- **Dependency compatibility:** React 19 and Tailwind 4 meet declared peers. SDK lodash is scoped to **4.18.1**; its rehype-katex 7.0.1 and micromark-extension-math 3.1.0 paths use **KaTeX 0.18.2**, resolving advisories found during installation. The SDK subtree has zero reported production advisories in the final audit; unrelated repository advisories remain. A direct **PostCSS 8.5.14** dev dependency supports the adapter. Actual Next PostCSS loader compilation, TypeScript, scoped ESLint, boundaries, raw-button/surface checks and fixture production bundling pass. Patched math dependencies render a formula and reject an untrusted JavaScript link; math remains unregistered/unqualified as a product feature. MIT license stays with the package. Next/RSC full-app rendering, qualification of retained controls, production bundle impact and authenticated appearance acceptance remain open under B5/S11. No provider, publication, billing or deployment change. Official source: https://github.com/openai/apps-sdk-ui.

### B3. json-render adaptation contract

The library operates **inside approved Particle presentation**, not as a replacement board or domain model. Existing registered cards can remain direct React renderers. Generated report sections and other approved compositions use the adapter where it removes custom composition work; do not wrap every static card just to increase library coverage.

| Boundary | Required Coach House adaptation |
| --- | --- |
| Catalog | Generate bounded component schemas from U9 capability registrations. Expose semantic components/sections, not unrestricted Container/Button/HTML/URL primitives. Reject unsupported types, slots, nesting, depth and sizes before renderer entry |
| Proposal → presentation | Continue generating domain content/change proposals against source schemas. Deterministically project fixed variants into render specs; allow model-composed sections only where U7 explicitly permits them. No extra model call merely to restate a fixed card layout |
| Data | Populate a minimal authorized display snapshot. Resolve binding paths against that snapshot only. An expression cannot reach the organization store, credentials, another run or another tenant. Repeated rows require stable identity and bounded counts |
| Editing | Local two-way bindings represent an editor draft. Saving calls the registered source command with revisions and retry identity; on success refresh from its receipt/readback. Neither setting renderer state nor emitting an action proves persistence |
| Actions | Bind approved semantic intents to application handlers. No arbitrary fetch target, handler text, automatic publish or source delete from generated props. Source writer rechecks authorization even when the UI catalog hid the forbidden action |
| Progressive output | SpecStream is a presentation assembly format, not a database patch format. Buffer incomplete units; validate completed units and render inert previews. Bound bytes/elements/patches and reject malformed or cross-scope paths; do not execute effects from arriving patches |
| Optional engine features | Automatic watchers/actions, arbitrary computed functions/directives and model-defined visibility are unavailable unless explicitly registered with a bounded purpose. Visibility never protects private data; omit it from the payload first |
| Saved format | Persist versioned domain content and approved view configuration. Do not make the library's mutable render-state tree the canonical graph, response store, document or approval record |

**Observed defaults to override:** the inspected renderer warns and returns nothing for an unknown component without a fallback; unknown imperative actions warn. Its structural validator is not a full domain/authz validator, and `autoFixSpec` permits lossy fixes by default. Coach House must reject unknown capabilities visibly, validate exact props/actions and composition independently, and never silently prune requested work to obtain a successful preview. Any bounded repair produces a new validated preview; no loss-changing automatic repair is accepted. [Pinned renderer](https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/blob/a0326b93c8e42ba33f4ad3d1dbf9990788de3b63/packages/react/src/renderer.tsx), [pinned validator](https://github.com/vercel-labs/json-render/blob/a0326b93c8e42ba33f4ad3d1dbf9990788de3b63/packages/core/src/spec-validator.ts).

The library documents JSONL patch streaming and state-path bindings. Our scoped snapshots, write controls and bounded composition above are additional application requirements. [Streaming](https://json-render.dev/docs/streaming), [data binding](https://json-render.dev/docs/data-binding).

### B4. AG-UI adaptation and why full CopilotKit waits

**October30 implementation checkpoint:** core/client/encoder are now installed and pinned together at **1.0.3**. The research-only progress bridge uses the SDK encoder and UTF-8/SSE parser behind Coach House strict envelopes, bounded input and existing saved-run readers. It does not use SDK state-patch application, untruncate repair, a second agent runtime or a hosted relay. The transport is server-opt-in (`WORKSPACE_AI_PROGRESS_STREAM=1`) and remains disabled by default pending full-app/hosted qualification. Existing JSON transport remains available. Local proof and open gates are recorded in S05; installation is not complete B5 qualification.

Use AG-UI for the external event representation of H8. Its client state is confined to one admitted run's permitted progress/preview. Coach House still owns the durable run, accepted content and recovery decisions. Implement the bridge at existing research run/provider boundaries; the current provider consumes OpenAI streaming internally and returns the final validated research result. It is not already a progressive frontend UI stream.

| Event family | Allowed destination / interpretation |
| --- | --- |
| Run lifecycle | Local run progress under admitted run identity. `RUN_FINISHED` ends inference/agent work; it does not mean the plan was accepted or all source writes succeeded |
| Tool calls/results | Sanitized permitted progress/result summaries; no raw tool arguments containing private data, credentials or hidden provider reasoning forwarded by default |
| State snapshot/delta | Validated run/preview namespace only. Never apply to `ParticleGraph`, editor drafts, grants, publications or organizational memory |
| Custom application events | Typed Coach House proposal/readback/receipt identifiers and status under H8; correlate run, operation, revision and sequence in the application envelope |
| Reconnect/cancel | Recover authorized server run state and receipts. Transport interruption is not cancellation; client abort is not rollback; replay cannot invoke a confirmed write twice |

AG-UI defines snapshots and incremental JSON patches; its documentation describes ordered application and resynchronization. It does not supply our source-write authorization or durable cross-source transaction. The json-render AG-UI guide is illustrative, so do not copy its example processor as production recovery code. [AG-UI state](https://docs.ag-ui.com/concepts/state), [events](https://docs.ag-ui.com/concepts/events), [integration caveat](https://json-render.dev/docs/ag-ui).

**CopilotKit tradeoff:** its inspected React/runtime manifests include substantially broader integrations and state/UI dependencies than the selected packages. That is not a measured bundle-size finding. Its useful shared-state/approval primitives overlap current source authority and composer behavior; adapting them would still require the same H5/H6/H8 contracts. The narrow AG-UI adapter avoids introducing a second runtime, approval store or chat shell while retaining protocol interoperability. No CopilotKit hosted Intelligence, memory/learning service, built-in agent or UI replacement is selected.

**Processing and telemetry:** selected library use does not authorize another inference endpoint, hosted relay, analytics destination or retained memory service. Use Coach House's authenticated transport and approved processing route. During implementation inspect installed dependencies and actual network traffic; exclude unapproved telemetry and keep private content out of diagnostics. CopilotKit manifests include Scarf/analytics-related dependencies, but manifest presence alone does not establish what data a deployed configuration sends. If reconsidered, evaluate install-time and runtime behavior explicitly. P3/D6 records the approved provider/funding behavior; actual tariff quantities and route qualification remain gates.

### B5. Integration work and qualification, within existing packets

| Existing packet | Concrete additional work from this selection |
| --- | --- |
| S01 | Add derived-catalog and source-command bindings to existing registrations; preserve manual rendering/editing without an active agent |
| S02/S03 | No replacement engine: keep existing navigation/layout/progress owners. Generated sections obey their measured bounds and deterministic status |
| S04/S05 | S04 supplies authorized context/tool results; S05 owns AG-UI transport integration, scope isolation, abort/reconnect, partial preview and durable result recovery using existing ledgers |
| S06–S09 | Existing publication/intake/table owners remain required. Library-rendered controls cannot substitute for their persistence or audience contracts |
| S10 | Wire permitted generated composition through json-render and the same canonical commands used manually; qualify incremental updates and focused revisions |
| S11 | Record actual dependency/build/bundle, browser/accessibility, provider-cost and recovery evidence; pin qualified versions |

**Required proof before enabling:** clean dependency resolution against the project lock; focused Next client/RSC integration; actual existing component rendering; exact-schema rejection; unknown renderer/action and lossy-repair rejection; malformed/fragmented/truncated streams; forbidden path/type/cycle/oversized composition; save/reload plus two-user conflict; reconnect without duplicate output; old run cannot overwrite a newer draft; tenant switch/revocation clears inaccessible output; public projection excludes private state; keyboard/touch/reduced-motion parity; measured first useful preview, render cost, bundle impact and model spend. Use deterministic fixtures before approved live processing. Do not claim performance savings from package descriptions. Record the concrete local machinery replaced or avoided and measured integration overhead; if no benefit is demonstrated, report that rather than claiming savings. S05 qualifies AG-UI before enabling its transport; S10 qualifies json-render before enabling its composition. S11 integrates existing evidence rather than discovering basic compatibility for the first time. A failed qualification returns a specific compatibility issue or bounded adaptation proposal; it does not silently substitute another framework.

**Pass 2 result:** generic rendering/catalog and event transport machinery are selected for adaptation; domain orchestration, source permissions/persistence, UI rules and product acceptance remain Coach House work. No app code or dependencies changed. Pass 3 is recorded in section 5 and pass 4 lifecycle bindings in L1–L7. P1–P6 records pass 5 approved defaults and remaining explicit policy details; section 9 now reconciles S01–S11 with all six consolidation passes.

### Component families

> **[NOTE]** This is a vocabulary of presentation families, not fourteen release requirements. A renderer can support only its registered variants. A conditional form, Gantt view or code editor is not implemented merely because its base family exists.

| Base | Design variants |
| --- | --- |
| Card | Summary, detail, status, preview |
| List | Checklist, sortable, grouped, nested |
| Collection | Table, grid, Kanban, feed |
| Form | Inline, multistep, conditional, public |
| Editor | Text, rich text, code, structured fields |
| Metric | Number, progress, comparison, target |
| Chart | Bar, line, pie, area, funnel |
| Timeline | Roadmap, Gantt, schedule, history |
| Calendar | Month, week, day, agenda |
| Decision | Options, branches, scoring, approval |
| People | Profile, avatars, assignments, directory |
| Media | Image, video, gallery, file preview |
| Action | Button, command, integration, execution |
| Container | Section, group, tabs, composite |

Register implemented variants first. Code editors display/edit content; they do not authorize execution. Every variant uses the same manifest, bindings, permissions, and bounded composition rules.

Finance, communications, and program categories classify work; they do not replace record kinds.

## 4. Relationships and state

> **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** Map display names to existing edge values. Preserve `related_resource` and explicit `feedback`; the shortened table below is not a replacement enum. A Produces edge is **[PROPOSED]**, not a required migration until the chosen output needs distinct semantics.

| Relationship | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Sequence | Suggested order |
| Dependency | Explicit prerequisite and satisfaction rule |
| Reference | Supporting information |
| Decision outcome | Authorized route selection |
| Produces | Identified output; requires compatible schema migration |

Preserve existing edge meanings. Distinguish exclusive alternatives, required parallel work, and optional support. Show blocked reasons. References and unselected branches do not inflate progress. Connections never execute actions implicitly.

Keep lifecycles separate:

- Work: not started → in progress/blocked → done.
- Proposal: pending → accepted/rejected; stale blocks application.
- Publication: draft → published → revoked; expiration checked separately.
- Execution: queued → running → succeeded/failed/canceled; unknown outcomes require reconciliation.
- Save: draft → saving → saved/conflict/failed.
- Data: loading → ready/empty/stale/unavailable/denied/error.

These summarize states, not exhaustive transition rules. Codex must define allowed transitions, actors, evidence, and recovery.

> **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** Reuse existing stored enums: work uses `backlog/in_progress/done/blocked`; pages use private/public with snapshot metadata. Map display labels instead of renaming persisted values to match these diagrams. “Stale proposal,” “revoked page” and “failed analysis” may be derived conditions, not new enum members.
>
> **[PROPOSED]** Each allowed transition gets actor, precondition, evidence, side effect and retry rule. Reopening work is explicit; a deadline passing cannot mark it done. Do not build an execution scheduler from the presence of an Execution type.

Assignee, contributor, approver, executor, contact, and member are distinct. Assignment does not grant access. Completion follows the configured work rule; ongoing plans need not define completion.

### Approved progress calculation and completion contract

**[ANSWERED D2 — latest user agreement]** Support Work, Time, Both and Ongoing. These are display/calculation modes, not new stored status enums. One deterministic selector feeds board cards, Objectives rows, summaries and public projections where permitted; AI never supplies the authoritative percentage.

| Rule | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Counted work | Accepted, selected, required actionable work in the declared plan/objective scope. Count each canonical work identity once within that scope, regardless of placements, memberships or number of assignees. Required work awaiting assignment still counts; assignment does not complete it |
| Exclusions | Documents, people, reference links and forms used as support; optional/unselected branches; pending/rejected proposals; duplicate placements; parent summaries of already-counted work. A separate explicit action concerning a form/document can count as work; opening its reference cannot |
| Calculation | Equal weights. `completed required work / total required work`; only canonical done status counts. In-progress and blocked contribute zero completed units. Use integer counts internally; render a whole percentage, reserving displayed 100% for all required units done |
| Empty work scope | Zero required units yields no percentage, not 0/0 or 100%. Show Not started unless the objective has explicitly been completed or uses Ongoing mode |
| Reopen / scope change | Reopening a required item or accepting additional unfinished required work recalculates progress. Removing or making work optional requires its authorized scope-edit command; never change the denominator just to improve the percentage. Keep the changed scope distinguishable from work completion |
| Time | Show elapsed/remaining time against a configured valid period; before start and after end are truthful time states. Missing dates remain unset. Passing the deadline does not complete work or the objective |
| Both | Show time and work separately; never average them into a single completion score |
| Ongoing | Show Ongoing and relevant work/status context without an overall percentage or forced finish line |
| Objective completion | Authorized user explicitly confirms the desired outcome. 100% work alone is not automatic objective completion. Keep outcome status distinct from its work/time indicators; changing scope after completion must expose that change rather than silently treating the old confirmation as evidence for new work |

Plan totals use that plan's accepted work; an objective spanning several accepted plans deduplicates the same underlying work once. Different actual work items require different canonical identities; multiple assignments are not multiple completions. Internal calculation may include restricted work, but a reader's projection must not reveal unauthorized counts, titles or status: show only a permitted aggregate or label visible-scope progress explicitly. Shared/public status is governed by O7's reviewed projection, not automatic access to live underlying tasks.

**Fixtures for S03:** two done of four required yields 50%; a duplicate reference changes nothing; adding one unfinished required item yields 40%; reopening one of the two done items yields 20%; optional/reference additions change nothing; zero required work has no percentage; elapsed time leaves work unchanged; Ongoing has no overall percentage; completing all required work still waits for an authorized outcome confirmation. Test changes with canonical readback, permissions and current revisions. Preserve existing stored work values and do not build weights, effort estimates or an execution scheduler.

## 5. Particle UI specification v1

**Scope:** this is the product's concrete UI contract for the existing board and approved MVP outputs. It replaces the earlier presentation sketch. It is a planning specification, not an implementation claim. Source observations below were checked October 29. New presentation choices are explicit v1 design decisions for review; they do not grant permissions, select providers or reopen the deferred catalog. No implementation begins until the complete plan is approved.

**Consolidation pass 3:** relevant Offloop prototype and Knowledge Canvas behavior is absorbed into the rules below. Selection remains explicit before expansion; inspection morphs in place; complex editors remain source-owned; optional suggestions, assignment controls, palette, saving, motion and return behavior have one contract. These are v1 specification choices for whole-plan review, not new claims about current UI or individually approved policy. The prototype's fictional scenario, people, price, simulated execution, localStorage persistence, original font asset and backend suggestions are excluded. No external prototype or reference folder is required to understand these requirements.

**Central rule:** AI proposes supported content, bindings and relationships. The application chooses registered renderers, resolves actual capabilities, lays out the board and enforces interactions. AI never authors arbitrary JSX/CSS, handler code, HTML embeds, SQL, permission grants or invented working controls. A human using Add/Edit gets the same result types and commands.

**Reference adaptation:** OpenAI's [plugin UI guidelines](https://developers.openai.com/plugins/concepts/ui-guidelines) inform bounded cards, direct edits, progressive disclosure, few actions, consistent styling and accessibility. Those guidelines describe ChatGPT-hosted surfaces; our board retains its own canvas, source owners and public publishing. Do not copy ChatGPT's host navigation. Adopt Apps SDK UI under B1 for base components and tokens; retain WorkspaceNodeFrame and existing source-owned behavior. The full library is the implementation foundation; U9 defines its bounded AI-facing compositions.

### U1. Presentation vocabulary and authority

| Term | Meaning | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Record | Canonical content and revision, whether in Coach House or an authorized external source | Existing source adapter/writer |
| Particle | Interactive representation of that record, dataset or permitted composition | Existing Particle registry plus registered presentation binding |
| Placement | Position, supported size and lock for a visible occurrence | React Flow integration and revision-checked placement writer |
| Membership | The record's relationship/responsibility within a plan | Existing objective/plan owner |
| Variant | Registered presentation capability, such as note, form or table | Application code; not a new database enum by default |
| Size | Small/Medium/Large/Extra-large board footprint | Existing supported-size registry |
| Mode | Board, Expanded, Editor or Public | Personal navigation state; source identity does not change |
| Section | A typed part inside an approved composition, such as report facts | That composition's schema and renderer |

A Large board card is still in Board mode. Expanding does not create a new record, publish anything, finish work or permanently resize a placement. A plan remains an objective's ordered membership/relationship projection; do not introduce a separate plan Particle merely for symmetry. Existing persisted type/widget values stay compatible.

Application authority is split deliberately: the shared shell owns anatomy/tokens; source renderers own content; source commands own writes; React Flow owns selection, handles, geometry and camera. The UI manifest extends `particleRegistry`; it does not replace these owners or duplicate the existing domain registry.

### U2. Shared anatomy

Every regular board card follows this order. Optional regions disappear completely when empty; do not leave spacer rows or blank badges.

~~~text
                       connection handles (outside clipping)
╭────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
│ type icon   title                         more menu     │ header
│ ╭────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮ │
│ │ one useful summary or approved content preview      │ │ body
│ │ optional facts / rows / image / evidence            │ │
│ ╰────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯ │
│ contextual status or count        primary / secondary  │ optional footer
╰────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
  accountable person   contributors +N                     assignment rail
~~~

| Region | Exact rule |
| --- | --- |
| Header | Type icon, title, one rounded-rectangle three-dot menu. Title is one line in Board mode, ellipsized visually; full title remains accessible and appears on focus/inspection. Do not manufacture a second title in the body. |
| Body | One inset content surface. Contains the registered preview, not a generic stack of nested cards. Child sections use spacing/dividers. No editor fields in a normal board preview. |
| Footer | Only meaningful work progress, source/saved-query summary or up to two contextual actions. At most one visually primary action. Omit footer when unused; keep the bottom shell gutter balanced. |
| Sources | Compact authorized reference pills inside the body/footer content area. Icon/avatar left, text right. Show a bounded subset plus “+N”; disclose more in Expanded/Editor. Never derive access from a pill. |
| Assignments | Outside and below the card, inside measured placement bounds. Show accountable person and up to two contributor avatars, then “+N”. One labeled control opens the existing person selector. Contacts and members are visually distinguished where access matters. |
| Handles | Real React Flow handles outside clipped/scrollable content, with existing stable semantic IDs. They are not decorative dots painted by the feature renderer. |
| Proposal review | Pending proposals receive a labeled treatment and owner review controls in the proposal surface; ordinary user-created records never receive generic accept/reject buttons. |

**Specialized image/event/document faces:** preserve their registered composition and shared shell ownership. Image title/caption strip may remain the existing 36px specialization; no forced icon/header duplication over the image. Event date layout may differ inside its body. A specialization declares its anatomy rather than adding an unrelated outer shell.

**Editor anatomy:** preserve `ParticleEditorHeader` and `WorkspaceWindowHeaderPortal`. Window-level Back/title/Save belong to the portal; the Particle's own header remains part of the card. Fields use the existing `editorFields` slot within that same card, not beside a duplicate preview. The model cannot move save controls or remove guards. No blanket status selector across every type.

### U3. Tokens, density and size

These are Particle-specific v1 constraints, centralized in the existing chrome/frame owners, not per-model styling instructions. Values marked current describe the migration baseline. B1's approved Apps SDK UI adoption governs base control styling and foundational tokens; map these once through shared owners while retaining explicit Particle geometry, semantic meaning and accessibility requirements.

| Token | V1 rule |
| --- | --- |
| Shell / inset radius | Current 28px shell, 25px inset. Child radius never exceeds parent. Use the shared chrome owner; no independent per-feature radius scale. |
| Outer gutter | Current 2px horizontal/bottom inset; header owns top spacing. Cards without footers keep the same balanced bottom inset. |
| Header | Current 32px desktop height, ≥44px coarse-pointer height; 10px horizontal padding, 8px gap. Grow for text scaling; never crop controls. |
| Body | 12px padding; 8px for containers ≤220px. Sections 16px apart; label/control or row content 8px apart; tightly associated icon/text 4–8px. |
| Footer | 12px horizontal / 8px vertical padding, 8px gap. Wrap actions without reducing touch targets. |
| Type | System font stack; card title 14px/20px semibold; body 14px/20px; supporting metadata 12px/16px. Editor/public input text 16px to remain readable on mobile. Larger metric values are a declared variant, not a new font scale per card. |
| Controls | Existing compact action styling: 28px desktop, ≥44px touch. Menu rows ≥36px desktop/44px touch. Prefer 44px where space permits; focus/hit regions must not overlap. Icon glyph 16px, header icon container 20px. |
| Frame colors | Shared frame semantics map to the scoped Particle palette below: outer frame, inset, text and border roles remain consistent. Do not change global theme values or add arbitrary per-card styles. Verify rendered contrast in both themes. |
| Focus / selection | Existing focus and selected-ring tokens. Selection uses a ring, not a work-status fill. Do not change status merely to indicate selection. |
| Shadows | No resting card shadow by default, matching current chrome. Temporary drag/overlay elevation comes from shared tokens. No glow/gradient generated per Particle. |
| Dividers / connectors | 1px content dividers; 2px semantic board/tree connectors with rounded joins. Decorative graph texture must not compete with content/edges. |

| Size | Existing regular footprint | Existing preview ceiling | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Small | 192×192 | 0 detail rows | Identity plus essential value/summary; open for detail |
| Medium | 400×192 | 2 rows | At most two relevant preview rows |
| Large | 400×400 | 6 rows | At most six preview rows |
| Extra-large | 816×400 | 12 rows | Approved collection/table/calendar layouts only |

The preview ceiling is a maximum, not a requirement to fill space. Reserve header/footer/assignment dimensions; reduce shown rows before content collides. Hidden content gets one “Show N more” or “Open” affordance. Never truncate saved content to fit its preview. Exact row counts may be lower for multiline content or text enlargement.

Regular size availability/defaults remain type-specific: objective Medium; resource Medium; calendar Large; people Medium; decision Medium; metric Small; finance Medium. Notes, form and event creation currently use Large. Images use width families 192/280/400/816px and natural aspect-ratio height, including the existing caption strip; do not force them into the regular 192px heights. Legacy custom widths remain readable until explicitly resized.

**Scroll ownership:** Board mode has bounded previews and no independent content scroller. Current `particle-sizing.module.css` uses `overflow:auto`; replacing that behavior is an identified implementation gap, not already done. Expanded mode is bounded to the usable viewport and moves substantial content to Editor. Editor has one main vertical scroll owner, with title/actions outside it. A wide data table may have one labeled horizontal scroll region inside Editor; this is not permission to add nested scrolling to board cards.

**Scoped Particle palette:** use the following v1 semantic token values for the planning surface in dark/light themes. These are Coach House targets derived from the desired contrast, not copied global prototype CSS. System theme chooses the corresponding palette. Apply through shared tokens, never through model props or a parallel stylesheet. Existing application colors outside this surface remain outside this specification.

| Role | Dark | Light | Use |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Canvas | `#16191d` | `#f4f5f7` | Quiet planning background |
| Particle frame | `#25282c` | `#ffffff` | Neutral visible card surface |
| Inset content | `#1e2125` | `#fafbfc` | Content plane inside shared frame |
| Primary text | `#f5f5f5` | `#191c20` | Titles and main content |
| Secondary text | `#a1a6ae` | `#5d6570` | Supporting text, never opacity-faded actionable content |
| Essential outline / connector | `#7b8492` | `#7b8492` | Required boundaries, neutral status and relationship lines |
| Progress | `#fb923c` | `#a14700` | Work in progress |
| Success | `#4ade80` | `#15803d` | Completed work |
| Proposal / selection | `#60a5fa` | `#1d4ed8` | Distinguish proposal by dashed treatment/label and selection by solid outline |
| Destructive / blocked | `#f87171` | `#b91c1c` | Labeled local error or blocked state; not interchangeable meaning |

Static sRGB contrast calculations across the three opaque surfaces give minimum text contrast 5.35:1 dark / 4.60:1 light for text and semantic status colors; essential outlines/connectors give 3.92:1 dark / 3.46:1 light. These calculations do not prove rendered accessibility with transparency, overlays or images. Filled status glyphs use the canvas color inside dark fills and white inside light fills; verify those pairs and focus indicators in implementation. Decorative dots/dividers may be quieter because they carry no required information. Never reduce meaningful text/icon opacity to create hierarchy. The system font and U3 dimensions remain; do not add the prototype's font asset or 32px pill sizing.

### U4. Modes and navigation

| Mode | Content | Editing / exit | Responsive behavior |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Board | Bounded preview using the selected size | Ordinary click selects. Explicit Open/Expand inspects; Edit invokes the registered editor. Card header/noninteractive shell can drag. | On a narrow viewport, provide the existing accessible list/focus route; do not squeeze four-column content into a tiny canvas card. |
| Expanded | Selected record's useful detail inside its existing shell, bounded sections and source previews | Short registered fields use the inline field contract below. Dismiss restores focus; Back restores prior navigation context. Preserve or explicitly resolve dirty drafts. | Width respects available viewport; if controls/detail cannot fit, use the existing full-width focused surface. |
| Editor | Full note/document/form/report/table configuration through its source owner | Existing Save/conflict/recovery behavior; Back respects dirty/pending guards. No surprise save on closing a modal unless that editor already has explicit autosave semantics. | Single column on narrow screens; long form labels wrap; title/actions remain reachable. |
| Public | Server-approved snapshot or explicitly supported interactive form | No private Edit/menu/assignments/traces. Public form Submit is its own authorized action. Authenticated guests use their scoped editing surface, not bearer-link edit buttons. | Organization header plus ordered full-width stack; public order independent of board coordinates. |

**Input precedence:** interactive controls/text selection take precedence over node drag; node drag over background pan. Keep `nodrag`/`nopan`/`nowheel` boundaries with the appropriate owner. Escape first dismisses the active popover/menu, then the surface; it must not close every layer at once. IME composition and multiline Enter never trigger accidental save. Keyboard users can perform every drag/reorder action through an equivalent command.

**Menu convention:** header three-dot button; actions grouped as Open/Edit, reuse/share, layout, destructive. Only render applicable actions. Use explicit labels distinguishing “Remove from plan”, “Remove from board”, “Make a copy” and “Delete source”. A source-deletion action requires its own authority and impact review; removal never implies file deletion. Share opens review, not immediate publication. Existing archive support appears only for sources with an actual archive command. No empty submenus or disabled placeholder features.

**Creation convention:** one searchable Add surface, reused by the composer, plan and board. Suggestions say “Form”, “Note”, “Document”, “Image”, etc., not “New …”. `@` searches accessible references; dropping/uploading resolves through the same source adapter. Missing required configuration opens that variant's existing editor, not a new wizard. The model cannot expose retired presets through another entrance.

**Assignment editing:** one owner plus contributors, not a row of separate management icons. Removal of a contributor does not revoke a separately granted permission; granting access is explicit and bounded by source policy. Expanded details list all authorized participants. Public display excludes identities unless selected and permitted in the publication projection.

**Exact activation contract:** ordinary card-body click/tap selects only; a focused, non-editing card uses Enter or its explicit Open action to expand. Selection, expansion and editing are separate states. For inspectable variants, the Open action occupies one permitted contextual action slot; it does not require a trip through a menu. Drag begins only from the declared drag region, and releasing a drag must not activate Open. Menus and text inputs consume their own events. Double-click is not a required hidden interaction. A plan-list title focuses the represented Particle; it does not silently open an editor.

**In-place inspection:** keep one expanded Particle per personal canvas view. Expand the selected card at its existing anchor, reserve its full measured bounds and show its registered detail inside the same shell. This is not a new inspector sidebar or automatic fullscreen takeover. Opening another resolves any dirty edit before replacing the expanded target. Substantial source editing still uses its existing focused editor. The same source, selection and navigation identity survive each mode change.

**Inline field contract:** each editable field declares `inline-commit`, `explicit-save` or `read-only` through its source binding. Simple title/summary and assignment edits target `inline-commit` only when their canonical writer supports revision-checked field updates and recovery. Enter commits a valid single-line edit; multiline Enter inserts a newline and Mod+Enter commits. Blur commits a valid changed inline field. IME composition suppresses commit shortcuts. Escape cancels the current uncommitted field draft and suppresses its blur commit; it never rolls back a confirmed write. An unchanged field sends no write. Show Saving, confirmed Saved or a local error; keep invalid/conflicted text available.

Fields without a qualified inline writer remain on the existing explicit-save editor path, with no pretend autosave. Complex note/form/report editors retain their registered save mode; whole-editor blur never publishes or saves a different source implicitly. Opening inspection focuses its heading/close control, not a text field, so mobile keyboards do not appear until editing is requested.

**Dismissal:** close/collapse handles dirty explicit-save drafts through the existing Keep editing/Discard guard. An inline save in flight remains owned by the source session; closing does not abort or report it saved. Preserve recoverable draft/error state and reconcile before another write. Escape closes only the innermost active menu/field/surface. On collapse restore focus to the initiating control, or the surviving card/canvas heading if it disappeared. Keep the current viewport unless explicit navigation changed scope; Back restores that recorded scope/camera when still valid.

**Assignment rail details:** place the rail 8px below the outer shell; use a 16px avatar with 4px avatar/name gap and U3 supporting-text style. The enclosing control retains a 28px desktop / 44px touch hit area. Measure the full hit area in layout, not just the avatar. Show the accountable person's name, then up to two contributor avatars and a remaining-count control. Expanded inspection keeps this rail as the single assignment entry; its popover provides owner/contributor editing and full authorized names without a second conflicting assignment row.

Unassigned work shows Assign when editable and Unassigned when read-only. Support-only records without plan responsibility omit the rail. Empty owner is allowed during planning; do not invent a person to fill it. Reusing a source resolves this rail from the active plan membership, not from an unrelated plan or source creator. Contacts are not workspace members; assignment never silently invites or grants access.

**Objectives/Opportunities surface:** preserve the shared dynamic island. In its expanded tab strip, center each title with its adjacent parenthesized count, while the Objectives progress mark stays at the leading edge. Use `(4)`, not `( 4 )`; unavailable counts are omitted, never invented as zero. Equal top/bottom tab-container inset is 4px; keep a 12px gap below tabs. Collapsed trigger uses symmetric horizontal padding with no flexible trailing spacer.

Only the feed/list body scrolls. The Add objective input remains in a separate fixed footer inside the island; tabs/header stay outside the scroll region. Switching feeds preserves their own drafts and scroll positions. Opportunity creation replaces/morphs the feed body inside the same container with explicit Back; required save/discard recovery survives tab changes. Empty feed offers Add opportunity. Authored cards retain the three-dot menu; pending recommendations alone receive applicable review controls. This surface follows U4/U6 save/focus rules and does not add a second editor shell.

### U5. Color, status and meaning

| Meaning | Presentation | Required distinction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Not started work | Dashed neutral 18px ring; “Not started” | Current source still renders a solid ring; dashed is the target, not verified behavior |
| Work in progress | Orange arc/ring; “In progress” or a verified work percentage | Never fake a percentage when no denominator exists |
| Work completed | Green circle/check; “Done” | Requires the configured work transition, not generated confidence |
| Blocked work | Red status mark plus “Blocked” and a readable reason on inspection | Not the same state as save failure or rejected proposal |
| Ongoing plan | Neutral ongoing indicator and “Ongoing” | No empty-completion percentage or implied finish line |
| Time progress | Time/clock label plus elapsed/period, where configured | Not work completion; when both modes are enabled, label both separately |
| Proposed change | Blue branch/outline and “Proposed” | Does not override the record's own work status; not a selected-state substitute |
| Save/conflict/error | Local control/field status and action to recover | Never color the whole plan “blocked” because a save failed |
| Publication | Explicit Draft/Published/Private label only in relevant sharing context | Never infer publication from a green check or saved state |

Bind these meanings to shared theme tokens (neutral, progress, success, destructive, proposal), with text/icons as redundant cues and verified contrast. The AI chooses neither color values nor status decoration. Color does not encode finance/communications/program categories; those use labels/tags/type icons. A record is not work just because it has a legacy `status` field. Do not display checkmarks/progress chrome on every note, contact, image or form.

**Plan-list rows:** status mark left only where work/progress semantics apply; title; status text aligned right; contextual three-dot action at the end. Use a type icon for support-only resources. Owner-only proposal check/X controls sit at the right of the proposal row and have accessible names. Ordinary authored items do not acquire them. Maintain the user's no-chevron/no-completion-fraction direction for plan rows; expansion has an explicit applicable control rather than an unexplained glyph.

### U6. State and persistence contract

| State | What users see | What must happen |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loading | Stable-size preview skeleton, concise loading label if needed | No fabricated content or editable empty replacement |
| Empty | One short explanation and one applicable Add/Create action | No sample content mistaken for user data |
| Draft / proposed | Draft or Proposed label in the relevant surface | Editing preview does not imply canonical save or acceptance |
| Saving | Local pending state; input/draft preserved | Prevent duplicate command dispatch; pending animation is not persistence proof |
| Saved | Brief confirmation or quiet settled state | Only after the canonical writer confirms; reload reflects the value |
| Stale | Current value plus concise freshness/conflict indication | Read current revision and review affected changes; do not blindly overwrite |
| Failed | Keep attempted input; show short cause and Retry where safe | Reconcile uncertain outcome before resend; retry identity is preserved |
| Denied/unavailable | Minimal truthful unavailable state | Remove inaccessible cached content; do not reveal private title/metadata to unauthorized actors |
| Unsupported | Explicit capability limit and supported alternative where available | No inert Save/Submit/Edit buttons or fake content widgets |

Form required markers and field errors stay adjacent to fields. Empty financial/metric values render as unavailable, not zero. Source loading/error is independent of the parent record's saved state. A model refusal is not a destroyed draft. Restore focus after valid saves/refetches; do not jump to a record the user has since left.

AI may propose multiple changes, but each source write retains its own receipt, conflict and recovery. Partial success identifies which artifacts saved and which remain drafts. Private drafts never appear on a public page through preview reuse.

### U7. Approved composition grammar

**Composition is bounded configuration, not a universal page builder.** Use the existing report and feature schemas. The following names describe renderer slots; they are not all new stored block types.

| Content element | Allowed use | Bound / fallback |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Summary text | Note, objective, work, report | Board summary ≤3 displayed lines; full source text remains in its owner |
| Facts | Report/event/record details | Label/value rows; long values wrap in Expanded/Editor; no numeric computation delegated to model |
| Cost breakdown | Existing report fact cost composition | Title/amount/fixed-or-variable rows and deterministic total; expandable inline; no accounting engine implied |
| Source pills | Authorized people/documents/events/resources/links | Board ≤3 visible pills then +N; keyboard/touch preview parity; no hover-only required action |
| Image | Image variant or existing report image block | Preserved aspect ratio, useful alt text; caption optional. Never an always-visible “Image heading” textarea |
| Work rows | Objective/work/legacy supported workflow preview | Size ceiling and one related action; status only for actual work |
| Form questions | Form definition/editor/public form | Existing six types plus the P4 familiar-field extensions; immutable published definition boundary; 50-question schema ceiling is not a target length; no respondent file upload |
| Data rows | Registered saved-query adapter | Authorized columns, bounded/paginated data; compact preview only; no arbitrary SQL or inline scripts |
| Decision options | Decision variant | Stable outcome IDs; long lists inspect in focused view; no automatic selected outcome |
| People | People record/assignment/source composition | Canonical references; no inferred invitation, permission or identity from a display name |

**Nesting:** shell → body sections → their declared fields/rows. No card-within-card-within-card, nested tabs, nested accordions or arbitrary recursive containers. A cost accordion may contain its row table; it cannot contain another accordion. A report can interleave its current text/facts/sources/image blocks, subject to its existing 12-block limit. Do not silently convert it into the 110-component future catalog.

**Choice of presentation:** use a summary for one record, a list for comparison, a table when column relationships matter, a form when collecting input and an editor for substantial writing. Prefer linking an existing artifact over cloning it. A requested layout that cannot fit the approved grammar becomes a specific supported alternative/limitation, not generated custom markup.

**Contextual suggestions are optional proposal views, not another Particle type.** Their data belongs to the existing proposal/suggestion owner and references an objective/plan/member plus the context revision. Display one short question, at most one relevant source/person pill and one plus action on a dashed support branch. Default visible budget: one per anchor, three per focused plan; additional suggestions stay available through that plan's Add/search surface. This is a display budget, never silent deletion of required plan content. Use existing type/text/status tokens; no permanent animation or extra marketing panel.

- A suggestion must identify a concrete supported addition and why it fits the current context. Explanation/evidence appears on accessible focus/click inspection; the visible question stays concise. No invented professional profile, availability, price or match score. Show price only from an authorized dated source, with currency and period. No fictional prototype person or $300/month fixture enters the product.
- Plus invokes the existing authorized add/proposal command. For an actor who cannot change the accepted plan, it submits a proposal and announces Proposed; it never claims Added or bypasses organization-owner review. Accessible action names distinguish Add from Propose. Disable repeat submission while pending; use stable suggestion/proposal identity for retry.
- Pending suggestions do not contribute to progress, block dependencies or reserve spending. Accepting optional support does not make it required work; that classification changes only through an explicit authorized plan edit. Do not fake a required dependency to obtain a branch line.
- Creating a task about consulting a person does not invite, book, message, pay or automatically assign that person. Assignment requires an explicit supported choice. A task's accountable owner is a person; an AI/tool executor is separately labeled run metadata.
- Hide is a personal presentation choice available in its menu; it does not reject someone else's proposal. Dismissal persists under personal view state for the same suggestion/context revision. A materially changed context may offer a new suggestion; ordinary re-render/reconnect must not restore a hidden one.
- After a confirmed addition, replace the suggestion projection with the accepted item or pending proposal in the same local region. Preserve camera and existing work; failure preserves the suggestion with Retry. Removing an accepted optional member detaches it according to normal rules and does not delete its source. No automatic suggestion regeneration or model call follows a removal.

### U8. Per-variant definitions

The identifiers V01–V14 are specification references, not new persisted Particle types. “Public” below means eligibility for explicit reviewed projection after its owner is implemented, never automatic access. All variants inherit U2–U6; unavailable source commands remain unavailable. Compact preview ceilings refer to U3.

#### V01. Objective and plan projection

- **Binding:** existing `objective` plus embedded plans/proposals; O1. Plan is not a duplicate root record.
- **Board:** title, one outcome summary and configured Time/Work/Both/Ongoing presentation. Show only relevant plan members up to the size ceiling; more opens the same plan projection. Default Medium; no Extra-large until the registry supports it.
- **Expanded:** accepted memberships and separate pending proposals, with sources/assignments where authorized. Right-side owner check/X applies only to pending proposals; review includes affected changes.
- **Edit:** short title/summary inline; plan membership/reorder through existing plan controls. Progress configuration and explicit outcome completion follow the section 4 D2 contract. Do not force the user into a separate editor merely to rename an item.
- **Actions:** “Add to plan”; optional “Open plan” if needed. More menu carries share and other authorized actions. Selecting a member focuses its existing placement.
- **Public / empty / proof:** explicit selected projection excludes hidden/pending/private memberships. Empty: “Add to plan”. Prove reorder/reuse/reload, owner/member review and ongoing-without-percentage behavior.

#### V02. Work and supporting resource

- **Binding:** existing `activity` without a specialized widget; use declared work versus support purpose, not a new unreviewed type enum.
- **Board:** title and brief description; applicable due/status metadata. A support resource has a type icon and source summary, not an empty task checkbox. Default Medium.
- **Expanded/edit:** short title/description inline, then registered work fields. Existing task checklist behavior stays where meaningful; do not call all plan members “steps”.
- **Actions:** work may expose its authorized completion control; resource exposes Open/source action. Never display completion for mere reference inclusion.
- **Public / empty / proof:** selected safe fields only. Empty offers Edit rather than fabricated content. Prove that adding a reference does not inflate work completion and that completion persists through its actual writer.

#### V03. Note and communication draft

- **Binding:** workspace note, eligible module reference or other explicitly writable text owner under O3. Email/social drafts are text purposes, not delivery tools.
- **Board:** title and bounded text excerpt, no toolbar; default existing note Large. Empty: “Write a note”.
- **Expanded/edit:** direct text editing only through the registered note editor; substantial rich text opens the existing focused editor. Keep source identity, formatting sanitation and save/recovery. Label read-only sources honestly.
- **Actions:** Edit/Open; Share only if the source has an eligible public projection. Never show Send/Schedule merely because the draft mentions an audience or date.
- **Public / proof:** sanitized selected snapshot; no hidden comments/evidence. Prove edit/reload, two-plan shared-source behavior, stale save recovery and no implicit sending.

#### V04. Document and file reference

- **Binding:** existing document source union/upload identity; O3. Source kind determines reader, editor and rights.
- **Board:** filename/title, format/source and one useful preview where supported. Prefer Open; default Medium for metadata, retain user-selected supported size. Unreadable/extraction-unsupported content is not a blank editable document.
- **Expanded/edit:** preview first; open the real source editor for writable sources. Uploaded Markdown's currently read-only file window must not acquire fake Save. External Drive editing follows its qualified adapter/provider path.
- **Actions:** Open; Edit only when a qualified writer exists. Download/export/share require their actual permissions.
