- **Public / empty / proof:** only supported canonical links or explicit asset grants; never persist signed private URLs. Empty offers Choose document. Prove same-source reuse, denied/revoked access and read-only absence of mutation controls.

#### V05. Form

- **Binding:** existing activity/form widget and O4 builder; O5 for real intake. Current thirteen manual question types: short answer, long answer, email, number, single choice, multiple choice, dropdown, date, time, linear scale, rating and both grids (S08 November1). Other remains unfinished. Native public intake works locally; lifecycle, Sheets and hosted/release acceptance remain open.
- **Board:** form title, bounded question preview and draft/published context only when relevant. Default Large. Board preview never accepts a real submission.
- **Expanded/editor:** existing single-card builder; question label/type/required/options inline within each question, with duplicate/delete and drag or keyboard reorder. Add option is inline; supported Other choices expose a text answer. Add question uses the P4 bounded type menu; reorder preserves question IDs and duplication creates new IDs. Destination/audience belongs in a compact configuration section, not repeated beside every field.
- **Actions:** Edit or Preview according to access; Share opens publication review. Preview distinctly rehearses. Only a published interactive form offers Submit.
- **Public / empty / proof:** approved version, collection notice and allowed controls; no private response count unless intentionally projected. Empty: “Add question”. Prove preview never writes, public submit saves to the selected destination with a receipt, and no respondent-edit link exists.

#### V06. Saved data table

- **Binding:** O6 saved view and a D3-authorized source adapter. Not an editable copy of rendered rows. Missing general binding remains a build gap.
- **Board:** title, source label, bounded first columns/rows and permitted total. Default Large; Extra-large only after supported registration. No editable micro-grid on the compact board face.
- **Expanded/editor:** focused table with saved filter/sort/columns; explicit permitted cell editing, stable row identity and current revision. Source location and read-only cells are clear. One horizontal scroller allowed in this focused table; page owns vertical scroll.
- **Actions:** Open table; Add row only if the source supports it. Authorized form creators/managers/editors can correct response cells, including name/email typos; respondents still cannot edit after submission.
- **Public / empty / proof:** private by default; selected safe snapshot requires an explicit projection. Empty differentiates zero matches from unavailable source. Prove canonical query/counts, permitted writes/reload and concurrent conflict; no arbitrary SQL or silent Google-to-local copy.

#### V07. Decision

- **Binding:** existing `decision`, outcomes and decision_outcome handles; O1 authority still applies.
- **Board:** question/title, selected outcome or “Decision needed”, bounded option summary. Default Medium; no Small per existing registry.
- **Expanded/edit:** criteria/evidence and options; explicit select/change through the qualified decision command. Editing labels cannot recreate or swap outcome IDs. Show resulting branch impact before an action requiring review.
- **Actions:** “Review options” then authorized choice. The model may recommend an outcome with reasons; it cannot treat recommendation as approval.
- **Public / empty / proof:** selected approved decision only; no pending/private criteria by association. Empty: “Add options”. Prove choice identity and branch meaning survive labels, revisions and reload; choosing a route does not execute it.

#### V08. Person and people collection

- **Binding:** existing people record/collection or authorized source pill; canonical contact IDs. Preserve the one-individual-person-placement invariant.
- **Board:** person avatar/name/role, or collection title with bounded people rows. Default Medium; type icon/avatar replaces work status.
- **Expanded/edit:** authorized contact details through their source owner; assignment is a separate contextual membership action. For multiple assignments, use U2's compact rail plus full list on inspection.
- **Actions:** Open person/people; Assign only when a work/membership target is present and allowed. Email icon must not imply sending is implemented.
- **Public / empty / proof:** explicit selected contact fields with permission; otherwise omit. Empty: “Add people”. Prove contact/member distinction, no assignment-based permission escalation and no duplicate identity created from reuse.

#### V09. Event

- **Binding:** existing activity/event widget or qualified calendar-source reference. Strings in the current attendee field are not automatically linked identities.
- **Board:** title, localized date/time and location; existing event composition, default Large. Unknown date says “Date not set”, never a fabricated date.
- **Expanded/edit:** existing date/time picker, timezone-aware values, place and source-supported attendees. Short fields edit locally; source-specific save remains authoritative.
- **Actions:** Edit/Open event; provider invitation only if separately implemented and authorized. Adding an event to a plan does not send invites.
- **Public / empty / proof:** selected event details without private attendee information. Empty offers Set date only when editable. Prove UTC/local round-trip, timezone/daylight-saving boundaries and no invitation side effect.

#### V10. Calendar and communication collection

- **Binding:** existing calendar record/entries; preserve draft/scheduled/published/canceled meaning and delivery owner.
- **Board:** title, relevant date range and bounded upcoming entries; default Large. A compact calendar or agenda renderer must be explicitly registered; AI cannot synthesize a new calendar UI.
- **Expanded/editor:** use existing calendar/content owners; entry click opens that entry, preserving return context. A saved draft labeled “scheduled” does not prove a provider delivery exists.
- **Actions:** Open calendar; Add entry when supported. No resurrected retired “message” creation shortcut; drafts use V03 until an enabled binding deliberately supports another path.
- **Public / empty / proof:** approved selected entries only. Empty: “Add entry”. Prove dates/order, source edit/reload and no automatic social/email delivery.

#### V11. Metric

- **Binding:** existing metric data/observations and units; future query-backed metrics only through an enabled adapter.
- **Board:** title, current value/unit and meaningful target comparison, with source/as-of metadata available. Default Small. Unknown is “No data”, not zero; demo data remains visibly labeled.
- **Expanded/edit:** observations and source definition; allow observation edits only through current authorized commands. Formatting/arithmetic is deterministic, not model-generated numbers.
- **Actions:** View details; Add value only for editable manual metrics. No invented chart renderer; chart variants require an explicit capability.
- **Public / proof:** reviewed value/snapshot with its date, no private drill-through. Prove units, missing values, revisions and source freshness.

#### V12. Finance summary and costs

- **Binding:** existing finance record or report cost rows; neither implies a ledger, payment or bank integration.
- **Board:** title, currency and a labeled amount such as target/committed; default Medium. Omit unsupported derived totals. Related cost details use the existing expandable breakdown in report facts.
- **Expanded/edit:** permitted amounts and fixed/variable cost rows; deterministic calculations and declared currency. Current report costs are USD-only, at most 20 rows, integer cents and nullable amounts; preserve that schema. A total excluding unpriced rows is labeled “Priced subtotal”, with the unpriced count, rather than presented as a complete budget. Keep original source values; do not sum currencies without an explicit conversion contract.
- **Actions:** View breakdown; Edit where writable. No Pay/Donate/Submit funding action until that action owner is implemented and approved.
- **Public / empty / proof:** explicit selected financial fields only. Empty: “Add amount” for writable source. Prove totals/rounding, permissions and no payment side effects; no accounting rewrite hidden in formatting.

#### V13. Opportunity report

- **Binding:** existing opportunity source via activity/opportunity widget, or the same report in the Opportunities feed. One canonical opportunity across both surfaces.
- **Board/feed:** title, concise summary, relevant timeline and bounded sources/facts; no universal match percentage without a real scored basis. Existing report block composition supplies detail. Default Medium for board summary; larger registered sizes show more without requiring scrolling.
- **Expanded/editor:** ordered text/facts/sources/image blocks, existing cost breakdown, source pill previews, image drop/replace and timeline picker. Preserve block IDs/order. An image has optional caption/alt controls in editing, not a compulsory heading textarea in normal viewing.
- **Actions:** authored opportunity uses the rounded-rectangle three-dot menu: Edit, Add to workspace, Archive, Delete, gated by actual source commands. Suggested opportunity acceptance/dismissal appears only in the recommendation context, never on every authored card.
- **Public / empty / proof:** selected safe report blocks only; private images require grants. Empty feed has Add opportunity; editor starts with useful empty sections, not fictional vendor facts. Prove inline change/save/reload, same source on board/feed, block reordering and safe image/source previews.

#### V14. Image

- **Binding:** existing activity/image or image-placeholder widget; authorized file ID, dimensions and alt text. A URL or model-generated image description is not a saved asset.
- **Board:** image at its preserved aspect ratio and supported width; optional caption strip. Keep existing crop/contain behavior explicit; no compulsory heading field or task progress. Empty placeholder offers Upload/Choose image.
- **Expanded/editor:** larger preview, optional caption/alt editing and Replace image through the existing upload owner. Drag/drop and file selection use identical authorization, validation and persistence. Keep the previous asset until replacement succeeds; do not delete a shared file when replacing one reference.
- **Actions:** Open image; Edit/Replace only when permitted. Upload progress and failure stay local to the affected image.
- **Public / proof:** explicit asset grant under S07 before public display; no persisted signed URL. Prove failed replacement preserves the old image, natural dimensions update measured geometry, alt text survives reload, and denied/revoked files cannot leak through previews.

**Compatibility-only variants:** current creation presets intentionally retire timer, workflow, split, sum, list, message and color. Persisted records keep their valid renderers/editors under existing owners; this specification does not re-enable creation or add them to AI suggestions. Referenceable existing workflow content stays readable. Any future activation is a separate registry/UX decision and qualification, not a model-generated extension.

### U9. Typed AI configuration and validation

A model-facing capability catalog is generated from approved application registrations and the actor's available operations. It contains bounded schemas, descriptions, current source handles, supported variants and allowed composition. Server functions, credentials and raw unauthorized schemas never enter that catalog. Tool-time authorization remains mandatory even if the catalog was filtered.

| Model may propose | Application determines |
| --- | --- |
| Supported variant and content draft | Whether the variant is implemented and available to this actor |
| A current authorized source handle or new-draft key | Canonical IDs, source owner, scope, revisions and identity resolution |
| Valid form fields, report blocks or query configuration | Runtime schema limits, allowed fields, data access, calculations and actual query execution |
| Relationships and semantic grouping | Allowed edge kinds, cycles, memberships, placement/handle geometry |
| Optional supported size preference | Actual registered size and responsive placement, preserving user locks |
| Suggested next actions | Actual visible controls, required approval, mutation route and receipts |

**Initial binding matrix:** these are precise adapters to existing owners, not permission to expose every stored field to the model. The IDs below are proposed registration names; saved type/widget enums remain unchanged. Registration is enabled only after its complete reader/renderer/editor/writer and recovery checks pass. Existing manual availability does not automatically qualify AI mutation.

| Proposed capability | Variants / schema owner | Allowed content proposal / command boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `plan.objective`, `plan.membership` | V01; `particle-schema.ts`, `particle-plan-schema.ts` | Objective text and bounded membership/relationship edits through O1/O2. Author, approval, revision, progress evidence and geometry remain server-owned. |
| `work.item` | V02; activity data/checklist schema | Title/description and declared work content through O1; completion is a separately authorized transition, not an arbitrary model-written status. |
| `text.workspace-note` | V03; notes widget and O3 note owner | Sanitized content draft/patch through `writeNote`; no generic file or personal-module-note writer. Other text source kinds require their own O3 registration. |
| `source.document-reference` | V04; `particleDocumentSourceSchema` / document widget | Reference an authorized existing source or attach a completed authorized upload; source-content editing only through the separately qualified source adapter. |
| `form.definition` | V05; organization `form-schema.ts` | P4 field menu, stable identity mapping, labels/options/required and reorder through O4/O1. Preserve current six-kind compatibility; add new types only after their manual renderer, editor, validator and writer qualify. Publish/destination/submit are separate O5/O7 operations; no response writes during rehearsal. |
| `data.saved-view` | V06; O6, still missing general schema/owner | Declared source handle, typed fields/filter/sort and bounded pagination. No model-written rows, arbitrary SQL or schema fallback. D3 adapter qualification precedes enabling this capability. Source row writes are separate commands. |
| `decision.definition` | V07; decision schema | Criteria and 2–12 stable outcomes through O1; recommendation separate from the selected-outcome command. Labels cannot change branch IDs. |
| `people.reference` | V08; people schema and authorized contact reader | Existing authorized person IDs/collection definition. No fabricated contacts or permissions; creating/updating a contact needs its own qualified writer. |
| `event.draft` | V09; activity/event widget | Date/time/location and supported attendee fields through O1. Provider calendar writes/invites are separate capabilities; attendee strings never silently become contacts. |
| `calendar.content-draft` | V10; calendar entry schema | Bounded draft entries through O1, preserving IDs/order. No model-supplied published timestamp or automatic delivery claim; schedule/publish requires its actual owner. |
| `metric.definition` | V11; metric data schema | Label/unit/target and authorized source binding. Observations require user-entered or source-grounded values through the qualified writer; unknown is null/unavailable, never invented zero. |
| `finance.summary` | V12; finance schema / `opportunityCostsSchema` | Typed supplied amounts, declared currency, cost rows and references through their respective graph/report owner. Do not route opportunity costs through a graph-only patch. No transaction or payment commands. |
| `opportunity.report` | V13; `opportunity-report.ts` and existing opportunity editor/save owner | At most 12 typed blocks, source handles and timeline through the opportunity source command; O1 only attaches the opportunity reference. No accept/reject/archive/delete hidden inside content edits. |
| `source.image-reference` | V14; image/placeholder widget | Authorized completed file handle and source-grounded dimensions/alt text; upload lifecycle belongs to its existing owner. Public asset grant remains separate. |

**Command rules:** create-draft, reference-existing and update-content are distinct discriminated inputs; update requires an authorized existing handle and captured revision, while create uses a model-local key. The adapter builds an explicit permitted-field patch, never spreads the model payload into a stored record. Undefined fields mean unchanged; clearing a nullable field is explicit. Reordering uses stable IDs and validates the permitted collection; omitted items are not implicitly deleted. Batch application follows H5's supported atomicity/partial-result rules, with per-output receipts and recovery. A new source cannot be referenced as saved until its creation receipt resolves. Runtime permission/revision checks still run after preview.

**Normative envelope design, not a shipped schema:** a versioned proposal contains bounded item drafts/references and typed relationships. Each item is discriminated by capability ID/version and validated by that capability's exact draft or patch schema. Do not use a generic unbounded `props: Record<string, unknown>` or arbitrary recursive UI tree. Model-local keys are resolved once to server IDs on application; they are never persisted as canonical identity or trusted as source IDs. Expected revisions come from the captured authorized source set and are rechecked at apply.

~~~json
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "items": [
    {
      "key": "form-a",
      "capability": "form.definition",
      "capabilityVersion": 1,
      "intent": "create-draft",
      "content": {
        "title": "Volunteer availability",
        "questions": [
          { "key": "q-a", "type": "email", "label": "Email", "required": true },
          { "key": "q-b", "type": "paragraph", "label": "Availability", "required": false }
        ]
      }
    }
  ],
  "relationships": []
}
~~~

This illustrates a proposed model envelope, not current wire compatibility or a release-defining scenario. The adapter maps local question keys to stable UUIDs for the existing form schema; on revision, it references existing IDs through authorized handles. Destination/audience still require configured policy. No Submit button, publication, storage claim or automatic assignment follows from this payload. The same application renderer produces the resulting form for manual and AI creation.

**Validation order:** decode/version → capability availability → exact schema/limits → authorized reference resolution → graph/semantic checks → source revisions → render sanitized preview → user/role-appropriate review → canonical source commands → independent readback. Do not stream partially parsed instructions into interactive controls. Unsupported capability/version preserves the result as an actionable error or explicitly unavailable proposal; do not replace it silently with another output.

**Bounds:** retain existing H7 result/budget and 250/500 graph limits, form 50-question limit, report 12-block limit and source-specific lengths. New configuration bounds may only tighten model-facing requests without corrupting saved legacy data. Display limits do not mutate storage limits. Server calculates counts, percentages, totals and status; model-provided confidence cannot approve a source or action.

### U10. Layout, connectors and transitions

- **Layout intent:** default to a compact readable plan projection, with related outputs near their membership/owner and explicit alternate branches. AI supplies semantic relationships; existing measured ELK/coordinator supplies coordinates. Re-layout only affected managed items; manual/locked positions survive. Initial visible group typically 4–8 elements, not a total plan cap.
- **Expansion:** measure the complete card plus assignment rail and reserve its bounds. Temporary managed-neighbor displacement remains personal; collapse restores only positions still eligible for restoration, never a collaborator's newer deliberate movement. Superseded measurement/layout results are discarded.
- **Board connectors:** 2px strokes, rounded bends/joins and registered handle anchors outside content clipping. Support relationship semantics explicitly: sequence, dependency, resource reference, feedback and decision outcomes. Produces is not added solely for visual convenience. Selected/highlighted relationship has a clear labeled inspect state; color alone does not explain it.
- **Objective-list tree connectors:** one computed 18px status/type-icon centerline and shared row geometry; connectors terminate at those anchors rather than guessed padding offsets. Continue the main rail to the next sibling objective; omit a tail after the last. Branch and last-child curves use shared geometry; remeasure after expansion/reorder. They are projected relationships, not an additional persisted graph.
- **Proposal branches:** show proposed membership/relationship changes distinctly and merge the accepted view only after canonical acceptance. Rejection removes the proposed projection, not shared source records. Invalid/stale acceptance keeps the proposal inspectable with a clear recovery path.
- **Motion:** state changes first, transitions second; use the exact motion defaults below, retaining 380ms for explicit camera navigation. No broad `transition-all` on node dimensions. New content appears in a stable reserved location; multiple arriving pieces do not repeatedly recenter the camera. Drag/text entry can interrupt animations. Reduced motion uses immediate state changes and restrained nonspatial feedback.
- **Feedback:** acknowledge local actions within 100ms target; show pending feedback by 400ms. These are measured targets, not a 400ms AI-generation promise. Separately measure saved readback, first useful validated preview and terminal completion.

**Expansion sizing and collision rule:** use the variant's registered content width, ordinarily 400px or 816px for an allowed wide detail. Expanded inspection targets at most 520px content height and must fit inside the free viewport with 16px clearance, including header, footer and assignment rail. Compute screen-space bounds through the current viewport transform. Reduce preview content before adding scrolling; when useful content/controls cannot fit, use the existing focused surface with its one scroll owner. Do not invent a new persisted size or force an image into a fixed-height box.

Keep the opening Particle's anchor stable where feasible and move only eligible managed neighbors. If locked/manual geometry leaves no safe room, use focused view instead of moving those items. Animate one measured layout result, not every input/token; a new user drag/selection supersedes pending work. Collapse restores only temporary personal offsets still based on the same eligible positions. An overlay closing or changing height may change free space but must not trigger whole-board fit automatically.

**Motion defaults:** card expansion/collapse 240ms; opacity/content reveal 160ms with no stagger; local reorder/neighbor displacement 200ms; explicit camera navigation 380ms. These supersede the prototype's 320ms open and reference's 500ms viewport motion. Use interruption-safe transforms with one motion owner per element; avoid scaling editable text throughout a long morph. Reduced motion sets spatial transitions to 0ms and retains immediate status feedback. Hover does not move cards or continuously animate connectors.

**Focus and disclosure:** search, source pills, plan-member focus and optional walkthrough all call the same personal navigation controller. Search/reveal clears only personal filters needed to show an authorized result and reports the change; it does not add membership or grant access. Keep the prior view for Back. A missing/denied target offers a surviving permitted context, not a broken empty canvas.

Walkthrough uses explicit ordered plan membership and labeled decision choices; dependency edges remain directionally truthful. Previous/Next/Exit never change work status, invite someone or execute a tool. Do not draw a synthetic narrative transition as a real dependency; use a separate labeled navigation treatment where needed. At a branch with no selected route, show a choice rather than implying that the first stored edge was approved.

When grouping hides detail, aggregate only authorized relationships of the same meaning and direction. Retain underlying IDs for inspection, suppress projection-induced self-edges, and label indirect/grouped connections rather than presenting them as direct facts. Outside-scope connectors reveal the permitted endpoint through the same navigation controller. Omitted/filtered content has an explicit count or narrow-scope route; required plan work is never silently clipped to a display budget.

### U11. Mobile, keyboard and public parity

Compact board geometry is not a mobile publication layout. In focused/list/public surfaces, use one column with 16px page gutters; no page-level horizontal overflow at a 320px CSS viewport. On desktop, optional report detail columns appear only when the container is ≥600px and the registered composition permits them; never let the AI choose arbitrary columns. Content zoom/text resizing wraps or enters focused view rather than hiding controls.

Keep controls ≥44px on touch; preserve the shared 28px desktop convention without overlapping hit targets. Focus order follows visible reading order. Menus/popovers support keyboard and touch; source previews open on focus/click as well as optional hover. Images have alt text, meaningful status has a text equivalent, and long unbroken filenames/URLs wrap safely. Announce save/error/proposal outcomes politely without reading every streamed token.

Public renderer receives only the approved projection, not the private graph with CSS-hidden fields. It excludes internal menus, unpublished changes, private sources, response datasets and assignment metadata unless explicitly approved for projection. Authenticated invited editing is a separate access mode using real source commands; viewing the public link never opens the private editor.

### U12. Concrete acceptance and design readiness

Each V01–V14 definition must have one reference fixture for empty, populated, loading, denied/read-only, dirty/saving, error/conflict and long-content states where applicable. Use current approved source schemas; intentionally malformed fixtures exercise rejection, not new variants. Preserve legacy compatibility fixtures separately. Fixture examples are not MVP scope or a single organizational journey.

| Check | Observable pass condition |
| --- | --- |
| U-shell | Header/body/footer/assignment order and token values come from shared owners; image specialization does not create a second independent shell; no-footer spacing balanced |
| U-preview | Every supported size contains its preview and controls with no nested Board scrolling; overflow opens preserved full content; long text never overwrites footer |
| U-edit | Inline Enter/blur commits once; IME and Escape do not accidentally save; complex editor retains its source save mode. Failure/conflict preserves draft; reload reads canonical state; no duplicate editor heading/actions |
| U-status | Dashed not-started, orange work progress, green done and explicit ongoing behavior; support-only resources have no fake task progress |
| U-actions | Applicable controls only; authored reports lack review buttons; owner review remains restricted; remove/detach/source-delete semantics differ correctly |
| U-reuse | Same source in another plan reflects content edits, while membership/view state stays contextual; no UI-only bypass of person-placement invariant |
| U-layout | Explicit Open expands in place; one expanded item per personal view; edges/tree rails meet anchors after reorder; full assignment hit area included; locked positions/newer collaborator movement survive; insufficient room uses focused view |
| U-ai | Manual and AI drafts use identical renderer/command binding; malformed/unsupported configuration never renders an executable substitute; server checks rights/revisions |
| U-responsive | Keyboard, touch, 320px focused/public viewport, text enlargement and reduced motion retain content/actions; no focus loss, automatic mobile keyboard or drag stealing text entry; actual themed contrast passes |
| U-public | Public payload contains only approved fields/assets; no private mutation controls; external editor grant does not expand unrelated source access |
| U-recovery | Pending, confirmed, stale and failed states are distinguishable; uncertain outcomes reconcile before retry; no false saved/published/submitted claims |
| U-suggestions | Add/propose follows actor authority; retry does not duplicate; pending/optional support never inflates required progress; hide survives reconnect; no contact/booking/payment effect; no fictional profile/price |
| U-assignment | One person owner plus contributors displays outside the shell; editing preserves contextual membership and source rights; no duplicate rail, implicit invite or assignment to an AI identity |
| U-island | Tabs have balanced inset and grouped counts; body scroll leaves input/header visible; tab/form transitions preserve drafts and scroll state; no review buttons on ordinary authored reports |

**Prototype evidence boundary:** read the current Offloop reconstruction README, system/style documents and `FlowNode`/`NodeEditor`/`ExpertSuggestion`/layout source. They establish a local simulation with inline node expansion, person rails and optional suggestions, not a production authorization or execution backend. Earlier style prose describes an inspector that the current prototype replaced; this specification follows the current in-place direction. Knowledge Canvas focus/projection rules informed the shared navigation and truthful connector requirements above. No source assets/code were imported, prototype services started or screenshot/runtime acceptance performed.

**Source observations versus target gaps:** `ParticleFrame` already wraps `WorkspaceNodeFrame`; `workspace-particle-chrome.ts` already supplies 28/25px radii and compact control tokens. `particle-size.ts` provides actual sizes/image exceptions; `particle-presets.ts` intentionally retires creation paths. `particle-sizing.module.css` currently scrolls sized bodies; `ParticleStatusMark` currently has a solid not-started ring/green progress, unlike U5 target. `opportunity-report.ts` already restricts block composition; `form-schema.ts` already restricts fields. This is source evidence only; no UI runtime acceptance was performed for this planning pass.

**Exact implementation owners to reuse:** `src/components/workspace/workspace-node-frame.tsx`, `workspace-particle-chrome.ts`; `F/workspace-particles/components/particle-frame.tsx`, `particle-card.tsx`, `particle-node.tsx`, `particle-editor-header.tsx`, `particle-sizing.module.css`; `F/workspace-particles/lib/particle-registry.ts`, `particle-size.ts`, `particle-presets.ts`, `particle-widget-schema.ts`, `particle-card-actions.ts`; `F/workspace-objectives/components/particle-status.tsx`; `F/workspace-finance/lib/opportunity-report.ts` and existing report/card editors; `F/organization-forms/lib/form-schema.ts`. Source paths identify existing project owners, not external design dependencies. They do not authorize forbidden cross-feature imports. Follow `docs/agent/workspace-node-frame-contract.md` for shell versus React Flow ownership.

**Before implementation approval:** use the section 15 consistency/coverage review and settled D2–D5/P3 policies. U9/O1–O8 defines the shared boundaries; source adapter qualification and measured tariff values remain recorded gates, not repeated product questions. Each enabled variant must meet U1–U12 and E1–E8 during implementation. The reference-fixture/measurement requirements become implementation acceptance; creating a specification is not passing them. No screenshot-review ceremony is required to define the contract, and no application build starts from this document without the user's complete-plan approval.

## 6. AI boundary

> **[IN PROGRESS]** Authorized research, strict output validation and proposal application exist. **[NEEDS BUILD]** A shared planner/UI-result boundary is specified below but not integrated. A dedicated Jev/Decisions adapter is required only for an enabled judgment-dependent capability. Do not equate a typed model response with valid facts, correct permissions or useful planning.

Coach House owns semantics, context access, validation, authorization, persistence and UI behavior. Section 2 A1–A5 assigns single owners across the agent, command orchestration, presentation and transport; the responsibility table below summarizes the reasoning boundary rather than creating another architecture.

### Responsibility contract

| Layer | Responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| Code | Known routing, permissions, arithmetic, constraints, state transitions |
| Optional bounded judgment | Predicates, choices or rubric scores only where an enabled capability requires model judgment; Decisions is one possible provider |
| Structured generation | Plans, drafts, targeted changes matching application schemas |
| Tools | Authorized reads and supported commands |
| Human review | Proposal acceptance and consequence-specific authorization |

Provider reference: [OpenAI Decisions](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/decisions), reviewed October 29: predicates, fixed choices and rubric scores are distinct from schema-based generation and function calling. Application-schema generation is required for generated plans. A dedicated judgment adapter is required only when an enabled capability explicitly depends on bounded judgment. Deterministic routing and ordinary structured generation must not depend on a second judgment call. **[VERIFY]** Confirm account/model eligibility and current API behavior at integration; documentation availability is not an activated Coach House integration.

~~~text
Request + selected scope
→ authorized context + available capabilities
→ bounded proposal
→ schema, reference, permission, revision, semantic validation
→ visible preview
→ authorized canonical commands
→ persisted receipt + readback
~~~

Adapters declare operation/model capabilities, schemas, tools, streaming, authentication, funding source, limits, and processing policy. Normalize answered, uncertain, refused, unavailable, and failed results. Preserve provider-specific metrics; confidence never grants authority.

Enable bounded judgment only where evaluation demonstrates a benefit and the capability declares the dependency. Candidate uses include: routing, relevance, reuse, missing requirements, component fit, relationship meaning, actionability, support, or change scope. Batch independent questions; stage dependent ones. Bound calls, repairs, retries, duration, and total spend.

> **[ANSWERED]** Type safety belongs at the application boundary. Deterministic code handles known choices; a bounded model judgment is optional where ambiguity remains. A second model call is not required for every request or registry lookup.
>
> **[NEEDS DECISION D6]** Provider/funding/processing policy remains open. First compare the existing structured-generation path with any added judgment route on the same fixtures. Require a demonstrated usefulness, latency or cost improvement; do not make provider interchangeability a prerequisite for the first usable planner.

Retrieve relevant authorized records with identity, revision, provenance, extraction scope, and truncation. Distinguish empty, incomplete and unavailable results; expose denied-versus-absent detail only when the actor may already know the source exists (K3). Treat retrieved content as evidence, not instructions. Exclude private names, identifiers, document excerpts, and organizational details from public web-search queries unless explicitly authorized and necessary.

Generate useful partial structure with editable unknowns. Never fabricate facts, capabilities, sources, or completion.

> **[PROPOSED]** Separate a useful unknown from a blocking unknown. Missing budget/date can remain editable; missing tenant, target identity, authority or unsafe execution destination blocks the affected operation. Prefer one material clarification over a questionnaire.
>
> **[NOTE]** “Use organization context” does not mean send the whole organization. Select authorized metadata, then read relevant bodies on demand. Cache identity/revision/access scope; invalidate on changes and recheck current authority at private-data return, disclosure and mutation boundaries (K2). Source excerpts are evidence, not model instructions.

Use stable run/operation IDs. Preserve user drafts and confirmed writes during cancellation; reconcile in-flight outcomes. Current research returns a completed result to the UI. Stream validated sections only after the event/recovery contract below is implemented; otherwise reveal the complete validated proposal.

### Planning harness and run lifecycle — pass 3

This contract governs the AI bar and O1–O8. It is required target behavior, not a claim that the current research flow implements a general planner. Reuse the existing run ledger, authorization, accounting and output writers. Start with one bounded model/tool loop; deterministic code controls admission, validation and mutations. No multi-agent framework, autonomous scheduler or provider migration is a prerequisite.

#### H1. Request admission and intent

| Boundary | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Request identity | A client-generated request ID identifies one submitted request; the server binds it to verified actor, organization, canvas, normalized payload and processing/funding policy. Same identity and payload recovers the same run; changed payload with the same identity conflicts. Changed intent is a new request linked to its predecessor, not a retry. |
| Target | Distinguish `new_objective`, `existing_objective`, `existing_plan` and `existing_output` in the application request schema. Existing targets carry source identity/revision; a new objective has no fabricated persisted ID. Server resolves scope and capabilities before provider access. These are proposed contract variants, not current stored enum names. |
| Intent | Separate create/propose, revise, research and inspect/explain. Existing UI context supplies deterministic intent where known. Only ambiguous requests need model interpretation; ask one material question when target or consequential scope cannot be safely inferred. |
| Unknowns | Missing optional date/budget/content remains an editable unknown. Missing identity, access, funding authorization, required execution destination or unsupported mandatory capability blocks the affected operation. Never invent facts to complete a schema. |
| Authorization | Resolve current identity and organization/canvas access on the server. Snapshot relevant target/source revisions and capability versions; the snapshot is not a permanent grant. A model cannot supply an authoritative actor, permission, billing route or storage location. |
| Admission | Validate input and capability availability; bind approved provider/model, funding source and budget; reserve and claim once before provider/tool spending. Pre-admission failure creates no model call. Concurrent requests still pass existing account/org caps. |
| Non-AI work | Known deterministic UI commands, exact authorized reads and navigation need no model call. Manual edits remain usable during model downtime. Do not send every click through the harness. |

**October30 entry-path implementation:** `researchTargetSchema` now accepts an explicit `mode: new_objective` target with organization, named/default canvas and graph revision; it forbids objective/plan IDs. Existing saved-objective/plan request shapes remain compatible. `loadResearchContext` resolves the current editable canvas without creating anything. The existing provider returns a run-only preview. Add proposal authorizes one exact-revision graph write containing the server-created objective, supported members and pending plan. Owner acceptance remains separate. The workspace composer exposes Plan with AI; it opens the existing bounded research surface. Primary-composer objective text now hands off to the same preview with the exact request and credit estimate; Generate plan remains the explicit spending boundary. Slash search, manual commands and explicitly selected document assistance retain precedence. Bounded user-authored preview copy editing is implemented locally (S05 October31); structural/partial preview changes, general output variants and live/full-app qualification remain open. No hidden placeholder, replacement ledger or database enum change.

#### H2. Four independent states

| State owner | Current implementation / required meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Generation run | Existing ledger uses `reserved`, `running`, `reconciling`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `canceled`. Preserve these values. `succeeded` means a valid saved generation result, potentially clarification-only; it does not mean accepted plan, published output or completed work. |
| Visible phase | Resolving scope, gathering context, generating, validating and recovering are phase/status descriptions, not mandatory new database enum members. A phase must reflect actual work. No simulated percentage or fabricated activity feed. |
| Proposal review | Existing plan proposals use `pending`, `accepted`, `rejected`. Stale/unsupported are derived applicability conditions. Only the organization owner can review plan proposals under current rules. Generation completion never supplies review authority. |
| Application / effect | Track each canonical operation's identity, expected revision and outcome: not attempted, in flight, confirmed, rejected/conflicted, or unknown. These are semantic outcomes to map onto existing receipts, not a demand for a universal table. Partial completion across writers remains visible. |

**Current staging behavior:** `researchPlanOperations` adds new Particles and a pending proposal to the graph; it can also reveal existing hidden members. Accepted plan contents remain unchanged. Therefore “Add proposal” is an authorized graph write, not merely opening a preview. The shared contract must distinguish a run-only preview, saved proposed artifacts and owner-accepted work. Proposed artifacts must not count as accepted progress or become public automatically. Rejecting a proposal does not authorize deleting records that have acquired other references; cleanup requires its own ownership-aware command.

#### H3. Allowed lifecycle

| Transition | Guard and resulting behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Submit → admitted | Validate H1; bind immutable request identity and policy snapshot; reserve/claim once. Replays read existing state. |
| Admitted → context gathering | Discover only enabled, authorized capabilities and relevant source summaries. Read needed detail on demand; retain evidence identity/revision/completeness. Existing work is considered before proposing duplicates. |
| Context → generation | Supply the objective, explicit constraints, selected scope, relevant evidence, allowed output schemas and tools. Model may use authorized discovery tools within budget. A preset chain of model calls is not mandatory. |
| Generation → validation | Parse typed output; check capability/schema version, references, local keys, relationship meaning, scope preservation, limits and evidence binding. No invalid fragment enters canonical storage. |
| Invalid → repair or failure | Allow at most one model repair using bounded validation errors, only if the configured round/time/spend budget has capacity. Do not perform a fresh search loop or exceed the reservation to repair. If repair is unavailable or fails, preserve user input and return a recoverable failure. October30 local implementation adds this policy to the existing research provider: one tool-free repair inside the current reservation, bounded fixed validation feedback, fresh source/cancellation/deadline checks and complete revalidation. Refused/incomplete/failed provider responses, uncertain usage and server evidence-metadata errors do not trigger repair. General planner/live quality qualification remains open. |
| Valid → saved run result | Persist the result/terminal receipt before reporting it ready. Settle/reconcile usage through existing accounting. A result may be a useful proposal, authorized read result, clarification, or explicit unsupported capability; do not force every request into a plan. |
| Ready → preview/revision | Show supported outputs and scoped changes. Follow-up editing uses the selected result/target version; it does not overwrite accepted work. If a new paid run is needed, it receives a new identity and approved budget. |
| Preview → save proposal | Current research saves proposed Particles and pending plan proposal via O2/O1. New-objective and cross-output paths must explicitly map which artifacts this operation writes. Persist only authorized reviewed content; no implied publication or execution. |
| Pending → accept/reject | Owner review uses the latest pending proposal and current source/base revisions. Editing changes the reviewed content/version. Stale/conflicting content requires a new preview before acceptance. Saving or generating a proposal does not accept it. |
| Authorized output change → apply | Bind authorization to the exact output/command payload and revisions. Call its canonical writer; permissions and availability are rechecked immediately before the effect. A user's explicit supported edit request can authorize that scoped edit; do not add redundant confirmation unless the command requires review. |
| Apply → confirm/reconcile | Confirm canonical result/receipt and return new source revisions. On uncertain outcome, read/reconcile before retrying the same operation. Never substitute a regenerated plan, duplicate object or new request identity to bypass uncertainty. |

The model may decide which permitted information to inspect and how to propose a plan. It may not decide that approval happened, invent a tool, bypass a guard, or declare work complete. Skills supply bounded guidance; they cannot change these transitions.

#### H4. Plan and change semantics

- **Useful plan:** identify the intended outcome, constraints, necessary outputs, dependencies and unresolved decisions. Add people, dates, risks or resources only when relevant. Prefer a small coherent structure over an exhaustive checklist; honor D2 time/work/both/ongoing modes without inventing a required completion metric.
- **Reuse:** cite existing authorized records by canonical identity. Newly proposed items use local keys; the application resolves them to stable IDs within the confirmed operation/receipt. Generation cannot choose storage IDs or alias a denied record.
- **Focused change:** enumerate affected targets/fields/memberships/links and preserve unmentioned work. Distinguish removing a membership, hiding a placement and deleting a source. A request to remove something from a plan does not authorize global deletion.
- **Existing relationship replacement:** current research replaces incoming unconditional links for mentioned items and outgoing conditional links for declared decisions. A generic “unmentioned work remains” promise is insufficient: show the resulting link diff and explicitly retain required links in those replacement sets. Do not silently drop a prerequisite because it was omitted from a generated fragment.
- **Capability limits:** expose only implemented actions for an output. A proposed unsupported future component is labeled unavailable/proposal-only and cannot render a pretend working form/table/button. An enabled capability has a versioned reader, renderer and authorized writer where needed.
- **Validation order:** structural schema → target/reference identity → source access/revisions → capability support → graph/domain semantics → consequence-specific authorization → canonical writer → result/readback. Repeat time-sensitive checks at application; generation-time validation cannot replace them.
- **Source grounding:** bind claims to evidence; distinguish authored goals, assumptions, inferred recommendations and confirmed facts. Strict JSON makes structure predictable, not statements true. Missing/truncated sources cannot be represented as fully read.
- **No automatic execution:** acceptance of a plan defines work; it does not send messages, submit forms, spend money, publish or mark tasks done. Those effects require their own enabled tool contract and authorization.

#### H5. Revision, concurrency and multi-output application

A run records its original scope and revision baseline. A new selection does not retarget that run. Concurrent read/generation work may coexist within quotas, but changes to the same source are serialized or rejected through its existing revision contract.

Before applying a result, resolve current actor/access and compare affected sources, target and capability versions. A changed permission fails closed. A changed target/source produces a conflict with the draft preserved. Refresh/rebase must produce a new validated preview; never silently rewrite an old reviewed command. If a source writer supports safe nonoverlapping reconciliation, preserve that owner-specific behavior and prove it independently; do not introduce a generic merge bypass.

For a request involving several owners:

1. Identify operation dependencies and exact payloads before starting writes.
2. Use one existing transaction only where that owner actually guarantees atomicity.
3. Apply dependent operations only after their prerequisites have confirmed identities/revisions.
4. Persist/recover each effect's receipt; report partial success precisely.
5. Retry only unconfirmed operations after reconciliation. Do not recreate confirmed outputs.
6. Do not delete or roll back successful shared work automatically. Compensation is a separately authorized, revision-aware command.

Generating a new version of a proposal does not alter the accepted plan or erase earlier review provenance. Do not allow an old stream/result to overwrite a newer draft or clear another operation's pending state.

#### H6. Stop, disconnect and resume

| Event | Required behavior / existing limit |
| --- | --- |
| User selects Stop | Request cancellation for that run; stop dispatching new model/tool work. Show “Stopping” until terminal state or reconciliation is known. Cancellation is best effort for already dispatched provider work; it cannot promise zero cost. Current provider checks cancellation between rounds and custom calls, not continuous in-stream provider abortion. |
| Network disconnect or hidden view | Does not prove cancellation or failure. Preserve a scoped run identifier and user draft. On reconnect/open, read authorized server state; do not automatically start another provider request. Hidden views do not need an idle polling loop. |
| Provider timeout / lost terminal response | Record or reconcile the known provider/run identity. Unknown cost remains unconfirmed, not measured zero. Existing accounting recovery must remain usable when generation is disabled. |
| Resume | First recover terminal result, in-progress status, receipts and applicability. Existing request-bound research has no proven arbitrary mid-loop durable continuation. If generation cannot resume, settle/reconcile it, then offer a new authorized run rather than pretending the old call can continue. |
| Clarification | Keep the result and context references; wait without holding an active provider loop. Answered clarification starts or resumes work only through a defined admission path. Do not reserve spend indefinitely while waiting for human input. |
| Account/org/canvas change | Detach obsolete UI results, clear inaccessible content and cancel local subscriptions. Server work remains associated with its original identity; never replay under the new actor. Access is rechecked before restoring results. |
| Cancel during application | Stop starting subsequent effects. A committed write remains committed; a dispatched write may be unknown until readback. Cancellation is not rollback. Preserve confirmed outputs and pending reconciliation. |

Run recovery is not organizational memory. Provider-managed conversation state is not canonical Coach House data or proof of current permission. K1–K9 defines context and memory policy separately; L1–L7 defines operation/event recovery.

#### H7. Bounds and accounting

The following are **current research-path observations**, not universal product pricing or approved limits for every provider/output:

| Bound | Inspected baseline | Shared harness rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Generation loop | `research-config.ts`: 3 provider rounds; `research-provider.ts`: shared 90-second abort deadline and SDK retries disabled | Every run binds finite rounds, calls and wall time. Repair consumes the same budget. No unbounded reflection or recursive delegation. |
| Discovery | 8 custom tool calls; 2 configured web searches | Account for all tool calls and prices. External lookup requires its processing/disclosure policy, not merely a prompt instruction. |
| Context/output | 20,000-byte assembled-input bound; 1,500 output tokens per provider response; request prompt max 2,000 characters | Budget source content before calls. Typed truncation indicators preserve meaning; do not silently clip required plan members or review targets. Larger limits require measured changes to validation/configuration. |
| Result shape | Up to 12 generated items and 40 sources; strict local-key/reference/branch checks | Advertise actual capability limits. Return an explicit partial/blocked result if the request cannot fit; no automatic paid continuation or hidden truncation. |
| Cost | Tariff-bound reservation; configured ceiling checks of 125 credits and 250,000 provider microdollars; actual quote computed from configuration | Preserve approved cap/funding route. Check remaining budget before new work; no silent top-up, new provider or paid fallback. D6 determines enabled product policy. |
| Completion | Terminal result receipt precedes settlement; failed/canceled current runs charge members zero while provider costs still matter | Keep generation result, member charge and provider-cost certainty separate. Preserve existing policy until D6 explicitly changes it; do not infer billing from UI status. |

**Capacity qualification — S05, reused by S10:** the observed 1,500 output tokens, 20,000 input bytes and 12 generated items are research-path baselines, not general planner defaults. Before advertising combined outputs, test plan-only and plan-plus-form-plus-draft requests against their complete runtime schemas, using sparse/dense authorized context and focused revisions. Record complete usable output rate, truncation/repair outcomes, input/output usage, latency and actual provider spend. Count required schema overhead and all repair/tool rounds. Select finite versioned per-capability budgets from this evidence; do not raise every limit globally or quietly shrink required outputs to fit. S10 repeats only cases affected by richer composition. A request outside qualified capacity receives an explicit bounded limitation or user-selected split; no hidden paid continuation. Qualification is implementation evidence, not a claim made by this specification.

**October31 local capacity measurements — not provider qualification:** reproducible synthetic specimens live in `tests/acceptance/fixtures/research-capacity-fixtures.ts`; registered runner: `tests/acceptance/workspace-research-capacity.test.ts`. They call the actual request builder, admission check, output schema, evidence binder and merged-plan operation validator. Configuration uses the real research limits with a synthetic model name/rate card; no live calls, token estimates presented as measurements, or fabricated model scores. The values below describe this revision and fixture model name; a different model identifier, prompt/schema or tool contract changes serialized overhead.

| Complete request specimen | UTF-8 bytes | Local admission at20,000 bytes |
|---|---:|---|
| Sparse objective |8,123|Accepted;11,877 bytes remain|
| Selected12-member plan, short titles and all11 links |11,344|Accepted;8,656 bytes remain|
| Selected40-member plan,240-character titles and all39 links |27,985|Rejected; no selected members or links are clipped|
| Selected12-member plan,240-character titles,2,000-character CJK prompt |19,987|Accepted; only13 bytes remain|
| Same CJK request after one function call and empty tool result |20,203|Rejected before the next dispatch|
| Sparse objective plus6,000 ASCII tool-content characters and call/result envelope |14,345|Accepted|
| Same tool return with6,000 CJK characters |26,345|Rejected|

Instructions, schemas and request options contribute7,465 bytes outside the input array in these first-round specimens. This is transport size, not provider input-token usage. A request fitting initially does **not** qualify its whole tool loop. Tool evidence is currently character-bounded; byte-aware evidence packing needs qualification against the remaining complete-request budget. Any later improvement must preserve authorization, coverage/truncation metadata and complete selected-plan context; never silently drop required members or branches, extend a reservation, or turn an oversized continuation into a successful plan.

Four complete output specimens validate through evidence binding and merged-plan operation construction: four concise items1,009 bytes; twelve concise items2,715 bytes; twelve maximum-description items23,523 bytes; a two-route decision948 bytes. A focused sourced revision also validates without mutating the input graph. These are schema/semantic specimens, **not generated plans or evidence that1,500 output tokens suffice**. The thirteenth item and explicit unsupported `form`/`draft` output types/fields fail validation. These original tests do not detect a model substituting a note for a requested form. The v11 follow-up below adds an explicit generation scope and required model-reported omissions; exhaustive request/output completeness remains unqualified. Combined plan/form/draft qualification waits for those actual enabled output contracts.

**Output-scope follow-up — `research-v11-output-scope`:** the required omission field and explicit capability instructions add981 bytes to these complete-request specimens. Remeasurement: sparse9,104; twelve short members12,325; forty dense members28,966; twelve dense members plus2,000 CJK characters20,968 (now rejected);6,000-character ASCII/CJK tool returns15,326/27,326. Fixed envelope outside input is8,217 bytes. The original table above is the retained **v10 baseline**, not the current admission result. A dynamically selected near-limit CJK prompt still demonstrates initial admission followed by rejection of an empty-tool continuation (20,212 bytes). Sixteen capacity/compatibility cases now cover the actual v11 builder, legacy result parsing, omission retention and real editable-note materialization. Limits remain20,000 input bytes/1,500 output tokens/12 items; no budget was raised to accommodate the new contract. Provider quality and token measurements remain unqualified.

**Still required before capacity is qualified:** approved model/rate-card measurements over sparse/dense/multilingual objectives and focused revisions; actual input/output/reasoning usage, end-to-end latency, total provider cost across tools and repair, incomplete/refusal/repair outcomes, complete usable output rate and preserved-work correctness. Record provider/schema/prompt/budget versions. Choose finite per-capability budgets only from those results. No universal model-capacity, minimum success-rate, latency or spend claim is established by these offline checks. Existing failed/incomplete/canceled settlement evidence remains separate and unchanged.


Budget exhaustion may produce a validated useful result with explicit missing parts, or a recoverable failure. It must not turn a partial result into a claim that the objective is fully planned. Detailed tool timeouts, per-source retrieval limits and memory bounds belong to pass 4; quality thresholds belong to pass 5.

#### H8. Visible behavior and event identity

Show the board/draft/diff first. Status uses at most one concise sentence, with an actionable error when needed. No narrated internal reasoning, repeated summary of visible cards or mandatory follow-up questionnaire. Necessary assumptions, source uncertainty, accessibility labels and consequence warnings remain available.

Current research streams provider events internally but returns the final JSON result to the UI. This does **not** prove incremental, resumable board streaming. Initially, immediate acknowledgement and truthful phase updates followed by one validated preview are sufficient. Progressive output is enabled only when its validation, ordering and recovery contracts work.

Progress/section events follow L5's exact application envelope, AG-UI mapping and sequence/snapshot rules. No partial JSON is executable. A section must be schema-valid and explicit about unresolved cross-section references before display; apply requires a complete valid dependency set. L4 owns canonical operation receipts; generation-terminal events cannot stand in for them.

Preview geometry is transient. Reuse existing measured layout/coordinator and reduced-motion behavior; new output should appear in the requested scope without repeatedly moving the user's camera or manual placements. A completed background run offers its result without taking over newer focus. Showing a draft never means saved, accepted or published.

#### H9. Traceability and acceptance boundary

Capture permitted structured events: admission/claim, provider call identifiers, tool names/status/duration, evidence references/revisions, validation outcomes, result identity, review/application receipts, cancellation/reconciliation, usage and cost certainty. Version prompts/skills, output schemas and capability bindings so a regression has a reproducible configuration. Redact secrets and apply data-retention/access policy; do not collect hidden chain-of-thought or indiscriminately retain full private tool bodies.

Required lifecycle cases for pass 5's fixtures:

- New objective from a blank board; existing objective research; focused plan/output revision; read-only inspection.
- Duplicate request identity with same versus changed payload; repeat application without duplicated artifacts.
- Missing useful data versus blocking access/identity; unsupported capability; schema-valid but semantically invalid links.
- Source or permission changes between retrieval, preview, proposal save and acceptance.
- Stop before dispatch, during provider work and after an effect; disconnect before terminal receipt and after commit.
- Old run completes after user changes scope or edits the draft; partial cross-owner commit recovers without recreating successful outputs.
- Limit exhaustion, refusal, no valid result, unconfirmed usage and disabled generation with recovery still available.

Assess these across varied objectives and output combinations. They define behavioral correctness, not a named organizational scenario. Source mapping below is inspected implementation; test cases above are requirements, not recorded passes.

#### H10. Existing implementation anchors and remaining work

`F/` is `src/features/`; `W/` is `src/app/(dashboard)/my-organization/_lib/`. These pointers identify owners, not permission to bypass feature import boundaries.

| Current anchor | Reuse / gap |
| --- | --- |
| `F/workspace-ai/research-types.ts`, `server/research-context.ts` | Reuse strict target/request validation and authorized selected-objective lookup. New-objective/output targeting and shared capability versioning remain missing. |
| `F/workspace-ai/server/research-run.ts`, `F/ai-credits/server/accounting.ts` | Reuse reservation, claim, correlation, terminal result and settlement/reconciliation. Extend compatible envelopes; do not introduce a second billing ledger. |
| `F/workspace-ai/server/research-provider.ts`, `research-config.ts`, `knowledge-tools.ts` | Reuse bounded loop, scoped discovery/read and strict generation. General output-capability selection, explicit disclosure enforcement and shared repair/phase behavior remain to be connected; exact tool contracts are pass 4. |
| `F/workspace-ai/lib/research-contract.ts`, `F/workspace-particles/lib/research-plan.ts` | Reuse local-key/evidence/decision semantics and run-based proposal deduplication. Preserve existing relationship replacement behavior explicitly. General source-content edits must use O3/O6 writers, not this membership-only revision path. |
| `F/workspace-ai/server/research-application.ts`, `W/workspace-research-actions.ts` | Reuse saved-run provenance, source rechecks, canvas binding and exact-revision graph application. Separate proposal staging from owner approval and canonical cross-output effects. |
| `F/workspace-ai/components/workspace-research.tsx`, `F/workspace-particles/hooks/use-particle-research.ts` | Reuse run-scoped recovery and late-result guards. Current sessionStorage pointer is not durable cross-device resume; no universal board event stream is established. |
| `F/ai-credits/server/recovery.ts`; `supabase/migrations/20261022120000_workspace_ai_metering.sql`, `supabase/migrations/20261022123000_workspace_ai_durable_recovery.sql` | Source evidence for stored run states, receipt-backed recovery and unconfirmed cost handling. Inspection is not proof of deployed scheduler/SQL behavior; preserve release verification boundaries. |

**Pass 3 checkpoint:** lifecycle contracts are defined; implementation is not complete. No database enum migration, provider adoption, spending increase or autonomous execution is approved here. D2/D5 product direction is answered; remaining D3/D4/D6 details are in section 15. Context, memory and registered tool contracts follow in K1–K9. Product/evaluation acceptance is defined in E1–E8.

Design basis, official guidance reviewed October 29: [Anthropic — simple composable agents](https://www.anthropic.com/research/building-effective-agents), [OpenAI — structured generation versus Decisions/tool calling](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/decisions), [OpenAI — agent safety](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agent-builder-safety), [OpenAI — trace-based evaluation](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agent-evals). These support the design principles; Coach House contracts, permissions and code remain authoritative. No hosted framework is selected by citing its guidance.

### Operation and event bindings — consolidation pass 4

The following L1–L7 contracts make H1–H10/K5 executable design requirements. They are target adapter shapes, not new persisted enums, shipped endpoints or a second orchestration system. Existing owners keep their stores and schemas. Runtime schemas must be strict discriminated unions with inferred TypeScript types; symbolic types below refer to the registered source/capability schema, never unrestricted JSON.

#### L1. Identity and admission envelope

| Field / identity | Exact boundary |
| --- | --- |
| `contractVersion` | Literal `1` for the new application envelope. Unknown versions reject before spending or dispatch; legacy research has an explicit adapter, not a guessed default |
| `requestId` | Client-generated UUID for one submitted intent. Server binds normalized payload, actor, organization and resolved canvas. Same identity/same payload recovers; changed payload conflicts |
| Requested scope | Existing organization/canvas selectors identify requested scope only. Server authenticates and resolves the actual actor, organization, canvas and source access; model output cannot override them |
| `intent` | `create`, `revise`, `research` or `inspect`; deterministic UI choice wins. Ambiguous interpretation may produce clarification, never an unreviewed consequential action |
| `target` | Discriminated `new_objective` with no stored ID, or `existing_objective`, `existing_plan`, `existing_output` with an authorized reference and expected source revision. Existing-plan target also binds its owning objective |
| `prompt` / `contextRefs` | Prompt retains the current 2,000-character bound. At most 40 explicitly selected source handles, each bounded by its registered reference schema; duplicate handles are removed. A selected plan is one reference whose permitted contents are retrieved on demand. Additional material uses bounded retrieval, not silently clipped input |
| `parentResult` | Optional authorized run/result identity plus result revision for a follow-up. A changed request has a new request ID; an answer to clarification is a follow-up, not a mutation of a completed run |
| Funding selection | Existing tariff/max-credit input remains subject to server policy and reservation. The model cannot choose payer, allowance or provider. D6 still owns commercial values; existing caps are not new approved pricing |
| `runId` | Server-issued durable run identity after admission. The receipt binds request, resolved scope, schema/capability versions and authorized status-read/cancel operations |

Admission returns either `admitted` with the resolved run identity/status, or `rejected` with a bounded typed reason and safe next action. No admitted receipt means no inference may begin. Navigation/manual edits bypass AI admission and use their existing command paths.

IDs and revisions retain source semantics: graph/Particle revisions are integers; a provider ETag or opaque revision remains source-tagged text. Never compare unlike revision kinds numerically. A source without a usable concurrency token cannot pretend to support compare-and-swap; its adapter must declare an alternative safe policy or stay read-only.

#### L2. Validated result and operation descriptors

A saved generation result is a discriminated union: `proposal`, `findings`, `clarification` or `unsupported`. Common fields are result ID, owning run ID, result revision, schema version and bounded source/evidence handles. Proposal payloads use U9's capability-specific schemas; findings use bounded evidence-backed blocks; clarification uses one material question at a time; unsupported identifies the unavailable capability and a permitted alternative where one exists. None of these variants means source content has been applied.

For a proposal, the server prepares the operation set below from validated model content. The model cannot manufacture an authoritative operation manifest.

| Operation descriptor | Requirement |
| --- | --- |
| Identity | Stable `operationId` UUID; optional originating run/result; exact capability ID/version and command name |
| Target | Authorized existing source handle, or a declared create key mapped to an eventual source identity. Create/reference/update are distinct operations |
| Payload | Exact registered command input; server-derived field patch and sanitized content. No `Record<string, unknown>`, broad object spread or arbitrary graph patch from the model |
| Preconditions | Captured target/source revisions, capability version and any required exact graph revision. New-target absence is explicit and maps to the source's existing create semantics |
| Dependency list | Other operation IDs needed for confirmed source identities/results. Acyclic and bounded by H7/source batch limits; reference only declared operations |
| Effect class | Internal read, disclosed external read, draft/proposal write, canonical edit, publication or external effect, as defined in K5 |
| Payload fingerprint | Server computes a deterministic digest over normalized command, scope, payload, target, preconditions and relevant destination. Excludes volatile display timestamps. Existing owner fingerprint formats remain compatible through adapters |
| Approval binding | None when existing user intent/role already authorizes the exact command; otherwise a server-verified reference to the required review/policy and exact fingerprint/revisions |
| Reconciliation binding | Existing writer receipt/readback mechanism, declared retry policy and known replay horizon. Not an arbitrary client-supplied URL |

Persist or deterministically recover the same descriptor identities before dispatching effects. Retrying an unresolved operation keeps its ID and exact payload. Editing, rebasing or changing destination invalidates the prior review and produces a newly prepared descriptor; it is not a retry. Persisting these bindings extends existing run/application owners only where necessary; this is not permission to build a universal command-log database.

Apply remains disabled while the originating run lacks the terminal/applicability conditions required by its source owner, including the current research requirement for `succeeded`. The apply request carries prepared result/operation identifiers and reviewed revision, not a browser copy of allegedly approved content. Manual edits supply typed input to the source command; their command adapter applies the same fingerprint/revision rules without requiring a generation result.

#### L3. Operation matrix: reader, guard, visible state and durable result

| Operation | Owner / input | Guard / command | Visible result and recovery |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Generate / revise | Harness; L1 request and authorized context | H1 admission, budget and capability checks; model/tool loop; validate and persist L2 result | Show truthful progress then durable result preview. Recover by admitted run ID; never resubmit because a stream dropped |
| Inspect / research | Authorized readers and harness only where synthesis is requested | Current source access/disclosure checks; bounded evidence. Exact reads need no model | Findings/unknown coverage. No created Particle or accepted-plan edit merely to display an answer |
| Save proposal | O2/O1; saved run/result and captured target | Current research application validates saved ledger content and source/base revisions, then graph writer stages proposed artifacts | Saved pending proposal, distinct from accepted plan. Lost response reconciles existing run/proposal identity before another batch |
| Accept / reject proposal | Organization-owner proposal authority; proposal ID/revision and decision | Current role, pending status, reviewed content and source/base revisions; bind server review metadata | Accepted/rejected receipt and refreshed plan. Concurrent/stale review leaves content inspectable; no cleanup of shared records implied |
| Create / edit source | Registered O1/O3/O4/O6 or opportunity/contact source writer; exact prepared payload | Source/field permission, current revision, schema, fingerprint; explicit create identity mapping | Source-specific confirmation/readback. Conflict preserves draft; uncertain write reconciles rather than creates a replacement record |
| Membership / reorder / detach | O1 plan/graph owner; IDs, order, expected revisions | Plan authority and membership semantics; preserve other plans/source content | Updated plan projection; failed reorder restores confirmed order while preserving pending intent. Detach never means delete source |
| Placement / navigation | Existing placement writer for deliberate persisted moves; personal controller for selection/focus | Geometry revisions for persisted moves; no source write for view navigation or temporary expansion | Saved move or personal view change. Temporary layout is not posted as collaborator movement |
| Share / publish / invite | O7 publication/grant owner; reviewed selection, audience and revision | Separate audience/source/asset checks and exact consequence authorization | Actual publication/grant receipt. Failure cannot show Published or expose a preview as a live public link |
| Public submission | O5 respondent endpoint; immutable form/version, bounded answers and request ID | Publication access, exact answer schema, destination policy, duplicate detection | Confirmed canonical intake receipt only after destination persistence; unknown remote outcome remains unconfirmed, never queued success. Not an AI tool, no model call and no fabricated answers |
| Save view / edit row | O6 source adapter; view query versus row patch as distinct inputs | Query/source/field access; row revision for edits; D3 adapter contract | Saved configuration or source row receipt, respectively. Authorized form collaborators can correct answer cells; technical identity and published question meaning remain protected. No copied display dataset becomes canonical |
| Cancel / reconcile | Existing run/effect owners; scoped IDs | Recheck actor access. Stop new dispatches; reconcile already dispatched work | Stopping/recovering then actual outcomes. Confirmed effects remain; cancellation is not rollback |

Read-only, unsupported and unauthorized are separate typed cases internally; public error detail must not reveal a denied record's existence. The UI never offers an effect merely because a generic library can render a button.

#### L4. Receipts, replay and partial completion

Each effect returns a typed outcome: `confirmed`, `rejected`, `unknown` or `not_started`. Running local state is `dispatching`, never optimistic confirmation. `not_started` includes canceled-before-dispatch and dependency-blocked operations, with distinct reason codes.

- **Confirmed:** receipt/operation identity, payload fingerprint, actual affected source IDs and resulting revisions, confirmation timestamp and the writer's permitted readback projection. Creation receipts resolve local keys once. A replay returns the original effect identity and marks replay; it never creates a second output.
- **Rejected:** stable code (`invalid`, `conflict`, `scope`, `locked`, `unsupported`, `unavailable` mapped from existing owners), safe message and permitted recovery action. Declare “no effect” only when the owner proves rejection occurred before commit.
- **Unknown:** operation identity and reconciliation status, no claimed new revision or automatic success. A timeout/network/server error after dispatch is unknown unless the owner can prove no effect. Do not label every existing `unavailable` response safe to retry.
- **Not started:** retains the descriptor for supported later action; has no fabricated receipt, provider call or effect timestamp.

**Inspected graph receipt limits:** `operation-schema.ts` uses batch UUID, base revision, optional exact-revision requirement, per-record expected revisions and a SHA-256 fingerprint. `graph-operations.ts` retains 64 batch receipts and a receipt floor; `ParticleCommit` returns graph/inverse/replayed. This is useful bounded replay protection, not indefinite exactly-once execution. Preserve it. Generic recovery must use the owner receipt/run binding and current readback; if proof has expired, show unresolved recovery and require a reconciled new intent rather than blindly replaying with a fresh ID.
