Current research application can create a fresh graph batch ID on invocation; deduplication also depends on the saved research-run/proposal mapping. Preserve that mapping rather than claiming a universal operation ID already exists. New cross-output descriptors must keep stable identity across retries even when an owner uses its own receipt ID.

For multiple source owners, prepare the dependency set first. Dispatch source writes sequentially for the initial implementation; use one transaction only within an owner that guarantees it. Confirm prerequisites before dependents. On rejection/unknown, stop undispatched operations, retain confirmed results and mark blocked/not-started items. Readbacks may run independently where permitted. Resume only the reconciled remaining set. Do not automatically compensate by deleting confirmed shared work. Display exact confirmed/failed/pending output counts, not “Everything saved”.

For Google Sheets intake, keep the approved destination as sole canonical response store. A Coach House recovery receipt can identify an in-flight attempt, but “Submitted” requires canonical persistence confirmation/readback; show the distinction. P5 now requires immediate confirmed submission without a durable delivery buffer; an admission/recovery receipt alone is not a successful submission. No permanent local backup is introduced by this receipt contract.

#### L5. Ordered frontend events and recovery snapshots

AG-UI is the wire vocabulary, not a replacement for H2's independent states. All generated-preview events use this application envelope at the adapter boundary:

~~~text
version: 1
streamId: server-issued identity for this run's event stream
runId, requestId: admitted identities
scope: resolved organizationId + canvasId
sequence: positive integer, strictly increasing within streamId
emittedAt: server UTC timestamp
resultRevision: integer when the event concerns a result
kind: discriminant from the table below
payload: exact schema for that kind
~~~

Server authenticates the subscription and rechecks current access before returning snapshots/private results. Scope fields correlate the stream; they do not authorize it. Derive wire extensions through a typed AG-UI CUSTOM payload or approved protocol metadata, with one adapter implementation; do not depend on every standard AG-UI event carrying these fields natively.

| Application event | AG-UI mapping / rule |
| --- | --- |
| `run.started` | RUN_STARTED; admitted identifiers. No dispatch based solely on a client-supplied start event |
| `phase.changed` | STEP lifecycle or typed CUSTOM; truthful phase and concise status, no fake percent or private reasoning |
| `tool.updated` | Permitted tool-call/result events; sanitize payload and validate fully before any invocation. Partial streamed arguments are never executed |
| `preview.item` | Typed CUSTOM, revision0; one complete schema-valid item reduced to key/type/title/description. Append within the current attempt; at most12 distinct keys. References, sources, bindings and connections are withheld pending complete validation |
| `preview.cleared` | Typed CUSTOM, revision0; discard current attempt's transient items before validation, repair, refusal or interruption. Never alters saved work or permits another generation |
| `preview.updated` | Typed CUSTOM or isolated STATE snapshot/delta; carries validated result revision and complete preview units. Unresolved references remain inert/explicit |
| `result.ready` | Typed CUSTOM with durable result ID/revision and applicability. Ready is emitted only after result persistence. It does not accept/apply the result |
| `run.snapshot` | Typed CUSTOM; complete authorized durable run plus its sequence watermark, under the same stream/run/scope envelope. Terminal snapshots may replace a redundant status read only after consistent terminal completion and clean stream closure. This never applies a proposal |
| `run.terminal` | RUN_FINISHED or RUN_ERROR mapped to existing succeeded/failed/canceled state. Reconciling is nonterminal; cancellation semantics are explicit. Provider completion alone is not an application-terminal event |

After generation is terminal, later proposal reviews/source writes return their own L4 command receipts and authorized readback. They are not appended as fake generation work or a new paid run. A source subscription may deliver updated records through its existing owner; it must not reuse the run sequence as a domain revision.

**Client reducer:** accept only the active permitted stream/scope; ignore only exact duplicates of accepted envelopes. Reusing a sequence with changed content, timestamp or event kind requires snapshot recovery, including a restarted start event under an old stream ID. On any gap discard the out-of-order delta, stop applying further deltas and request one authorized snapshot; do not create an unbounded reorder buffer or concurrent snapshot requests. Never merge a new stream into an old one by timestamp. A snapshot contains stream ID, sequence watermark, run status, latest durable result/revision and current applicability; apply it atomically, then resume strictly after its watermark. If replay is unavailable, snapshot recovery remains sufficient. A producer restart issues a new stream ID and authorized snapshot; never restart sequence numbers under an old stream ID. An incomplete in-memory preview lost on restart may reset to the last durable result or an explicit incomplete state, without regeneration or a false ready claim. Persist a durable result/checkpoint before advertising its ready event; do not require retaining every token event.

**Reconnect behavior:** on network return or visible-tab activation, a recoverable current run receives one authorized same-run read. Coalesce simultaneous return/focus events while that read is pending. Detach any old generation response/subscription, clear transient units, and fence its late callbacks/finalization before consuming the saved state. This ends presentation only, not the server run. Succeeded results reopen normal review; running/reconciling results offer Check status; denied/invalid results remain withheld and do not automatically retry. Preserve private drafts against the exact saved base. No polling, new generation, invented watermark, auto-application or browser content cache. Ordinary focus retains existing saved-result revalidation; hidden tabs do not fetch.

**Incremental item disclosure:** frame only fully closed root-level item objects from the selected provider text part, with a32KiB buffer ceiling and strict existing item validation. Never repair incomplete JSON, render raw tokens, execute partial arguments or infer missing relationships. Before each nonempty batch, recheck the current actor/context, every observed source and run cancellation; reject unavailable evidence. Display only inert text under “Unconfirmed items. Connections pending.” Duplicate/overflow keys or a changed provider output part invalidate that attempt's presentation. Clear transient items when the provider stream ends, including failure; normal complete-plan validation, metering, persistence and authorized saved readback remain independent. Presentation failure cannot erase measured usage or create another paid call. Keep the existing server-side stream qualification flag disabled until live/full-app acceptance. No layout movement or new animation is required for text previews.

**Bounded subscription:** stop consuming progress after30 seconds without a new accepted event or120 seconds total. Duplicate sequences, incomplete frames and arbitrary bytes cannot extend the idle limit. These bound presentation only; they do not cancel execution, retry generation, settle usage or claim failure. On timeout or producer-identity discontinuity, clear the transient preview before one authorized same-run read. A saved result opens normal review; running/reconciling and denied reads retain explicit recovery without polling. Release timers, subscriptions and the response body on completion, error, timeout or unmount. A terminal event without clean closure still requires saved readback. The current single-result implementation uses snapshot-only recovery: reconnect/open returns an authorized durable run, not a resumed event stream. Old stream identity/sequence state is discarded, never persisted or merged into that read. Durable event replay and persisted cursors are not required for this path; any later resumed transport must obey the epoch/watermark rules above.

The transport parser must handle UTF-8 and event frames split across network chunks; one network chunk is not one JSON message. Enforce H7/source bounds before parsing/patch traversal; reject unknown versions, malformed paths, array overflow and structural cycles. Selected library transport helpers do not remove these acceptance cases. An active preview remains explicitly unsaved. Once its stream fails or becomes incomplete, clear transient content while rechecking saved authority; never promote it to a saved result.

Local editor drafts have their own identity/base revision. Incoming result revisions cannot overwrite them. Switching organization/canvas detaches the old subscription and clears inaccessible content; a late completion updates only its saved run and may offer a non-stealing result notification when still authorized. No automatic camera move or refocus on stream arrival.

**Private preview autosave:** after1.5 seconds without an edit, checkpoint a recoverable changed research draft through its existing actor-private revisioned writer. One save may be in flight; continued typing stays local and schedules a later checkpoint after acknowledgement. Empty/unfinished copy is recoverable; unsafe topology is not. Do not save while verification, proposal application, departure confirmation, staleness, conflict or uncertain/error recovery is active. Unmount cancels pending timers. Failed/uncertain saves stop automatically; explicit retry preserves the original edit identity. An acknowledgement marks only the exact saved version, never newer typing. Display private-save semantics and pending/saved/error state. Retain manual Save draft, Save and leave and discard. No canonical application, AI call, credits, browser text cache, unload write or retry loop. Recovery covers acknowledged checkpoints; a crash before the debounce/acknowledgement can still lose the latest edits. This supersedes earlier explicit-only private-checkpoint behavior, not other editors' save contracts.


#### L6. Approval and failure recovery rules

Approval binds the actor, required role/policy, exact target/destination, prepared payload fingerprint, result/proposal revision and relevant source revisions. The server reads authoritative content; a client `approved: true`, generated checkmark or generic human-in-the-loop event is insufficient. Recheck current rights at dispatch. Editing after approval invalidates that approval. A supported routine edit already authorized by the user's request does not acquire an extra confirmation screen.

| Failure boundary | Required visible action |
| --- | --- |
| Before admission | Preserve objective/input; explain the blocked capability/access/limit. No provider charge claimed |
| Invalid generation | One bounded repair only under H3; otherwise preserve input and last valid preview with a failure state. No lossy automatic “success” |
| Source changed before apply | Keep proposed/draft content, show conflict, refresh relevant source and prepare a newly reviewed diff. Never silently rebase approved effects |
| Commit response lost | Show Checking save; reconcile receipt/readback under the same operation. Do not generate/recreate the output |
| Access revoked | Stop exposing source/result content; retain only permitted recovery metadata. Do not leak cached content through errors, previews or search |
| Partial multi-output completion | Show confirmed outputs and exact remaining states; retry only the reconciled set. Do not erase already completed work |
| Stop after dispatch | Report confirmed versus unknown outcomes. Keep recovery available even when new AI generation is disabled or credits are exhausted |
| Refund/usage uncertainty | Existing accounting reconciliation remains authoritative; do not equate source-save success, model success and billable usage |

#### L7. Contract fixtures and source bindings

Before enabling the lifecycle, focused fixtures must prove: same request/same payload recovers; same ID/different payload rejects; create → dependent reference resolves once; two source edits conflict safely; owner review rejects changed content/role; lost commit response does not duplicate; receipt expiry does not authorize blind replay; partial multi-owner work resumes without touching confirmed outputs; fragmented/out-of-order/duplicate events recover via snapshot; a new user draft survives a late old result; account switch/revocation prevents cached disclosure; no-op manual navigation makes no model call; canceled/clarification-only runs do not complete work; a public form retry preserves its one receipt and actual destination status. Cases vary capabilities/domains; no single example defines MVP.

Reuse current `F/workspace-ai/research-types.ts`, research run/application owners, `F/workspace-particles/lib/operation-schema.ts`, `graph-operations.ts`, `authorized-mutation.ts`, `types.ts`, and `W/workspace-particle-write.ts` / `workspace-research-actions.ts` (`W` remains the existing `_lib` owner). Exact source APIs stay canonical. Extend adapters and receipts only where the required behavior is missing; no source code was changed or fixture executed in this planning pass.

**Pass 4 complete:** A owners, B dependencies, U presentation, H/K lifecycle/tool rules and O output contracts now connect through L1–L7. P1–P6 records approved D3/D4/D6 product behavior; measured commercial quantities and implementation qualification remain pending; section 9 now contains the reconciled S01–S11 packets. These are specification-level contracts; runtime schemas, wire transport and integration proof are still implementation work after complete-plan approval.

### Context, memory and tool contracts — original planning pass 4

These contracts bind H1–H10 to actual data owners. They define application behavior, not provider promises or an already integrated memory system. `F/` means `src/features/`. Tool names below marked target are contract names, not newly implemented endpoints. No skill, source document, connector or model output grants access.

#### K1. Separate knowledge from memory and recovery

| Store / context | Authority and lifecycle |
| --- | --- |
| Canonical workspace knowledge | Organization records, people, plans, documents, decisions and connected sources remain in their existing owners. Retrieve authorized current versions; do not copy the organization into a second AI database. Existing source editing remains the way to correct facts. |
| Run working context | Current request, selected scope, permitted evidence, tool results and draft outputs. Bounded to the run; cached copies do not become independent facts or permissions. Derived summaries retain source identity and limitations. |
| Run recovery | Saved request/result IDs, relevant versions, operation receipts and usage state support H6. Current ledger/session pointers are foundations. Recovery means restoring authorized work, not automatically remembering everything for future requests. |
| Personal retained preferences | Optional explicit user preferences owned by that person, scoped to permitted contexts. They cannot reveal another organization, override team policy, or become shared knowledge merely because their owner joins a workspace. |
| Shared retained guidance | Optional organization/workspace preferences or authored guidance with an accountable owner, source, revision and approved access. The model can propose an entry; it cannot silently promote an inference or private personal note into organization policy. |
| Retrieval index/cache | Derived acceleration, never authority. Each result retains canonical source identity/revision and current access checks. Expired/deleted/revoked items are removed or suppressed; stale index matches never authorize content retrieval. |

**MVP boundary:** canonical knowledge plus scoped run context/recovery are required. A general long-term memory store is not a prerequisite to planning. Persistent preference/guidance writes remain disabled until their access, retention and correction policy and real writer exist. This is an explicit capability limit, not permission to build hidden memory first. No actual user memory is written by this planning pass.

#### K2. Context assembly and efficiency

1. Resolve server-derived actor, organization/canvas, target, requested operation and processing policy. Determine the registered output/tool subset relevant to the request.
2. Load the selected target's minimal authoritative baseline: identity, revision, necessary membership/relationships and the fields needed to preserve existing work. Load approved organization profile fields relevant to the task; not the full profile or every contact.
3. Search permitted sources for missing information or reusable work. Return compact matches, source kind, revision and availability—not whole documents by default. Explicitly selected known references may be read directly after authorization; they need not be rediscovered through search.
4. Read the useful detail with bounded field/section extraction and provenance. Separate “not found in this bounded search” from “does not exist.” De-duplicate source references and repeated tool calls within the run.
5. Assemble evidence with extraction scope, missing parts and uncertainty. Treat selected context as useful evidence, not permission to transmit it anywhere or follow embedded instructions.
6. Before another call, check remaining H7 budgets. Compact expendable intermediate detail while preserving target identity, required relationships, permission boundaries, pending operations, unresolved questions and source references. Reload current sources as needed; a model summary never replaces an authorization/revision check.

**Preservation rule:** current `research-prompt.ts` includes a selected plan's membership/links completely or rejects oversize input. Keep that protection. For general requests, bounded summaries must declare omitted coverage; do not silently make “first four plans/eight members” look like the whole workspace. Required affected dependencies cannot be removed to fit a prompt. Scope/narrow or return an explicit incomplete result instead.

**Search rule:** start with current source adapters and deterministic search. Neither a vector database nor embedding every organization is required. Add indexing only for demonstrated retrieval/latency failures, with the same source/access/deletion rules. Search, read and evidence freshness need their own evaluation in pass 5.

**Caching rule:** current caches are request-local. Coalesce identical reads within that scope, but do not let a cache bypass access checks before returning private content, disclosing it externally or applying changes. A later shared cache must include tenant, actor/effective access, source revision, selected fields, extraction version and processing scope in its key/invalidation contract. A tenant ID alone is insufficient. Do not claim revocation can recall data already lawfully returned to an earlier request.

#### K3. Evidence envelope and availability

The shared contract is a strict schema with TypeScript types inferred from the same source. Reuse `KnowledgeRecord` / `ResearchSource` fields where compatible; extend versioned boundaries rather than silently changing stored historical evidence.

| Field group | Required meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Identity | Canonical source owner/kind/ID, organization and applicable workspace scope; stable references, not model-generated titles as lookup keys. Server controls authority-bearing fields. |
| Version / freshness | Source revision and checked/read timestamps; source-specific freshness policy where needed. A shared profile timestamp is a coarse invalidation revision, not a claim of per-contact versioning. |
| Locator | Allowed field/section/range or external URL identifying the evidence. Never include private storage paths, credentials, signed URLs or account hints merely for citation. |
| Content | Task-relevant permitted fields/excerpt, content kind, extraction scope and availability. Mark metadata-only, truncated, partially searched and unsupported extraction explicitly. Character/byte limits use their actual units. |
| Basis | Source observation, explicit user-authored statement or inference. An inference cites its supporting evidence but is not relabeled verified. “Checked at” is not “confirmed true.” |
| Disclosure | Server-derived processing/destination policy and sensitivity category applicable to those fields. A model cannot downgrade sensitivity or grant an outbound exception. |
| Recovery | Machine-readable result status and permitted next action; no raw database exceptions or private identifiers in user-facing errors. |

Required adapter outcomes: available, metadata-only, truncated, changed, unavailable, unsupported-format, or exhausted-budget, mapped consistently from source errors. An explicit denied/not-found distinction may be returned only when the actor is already entitled to know that identity exists. Otherwise use a non-enumerating unavailable result. Private existence cannot leak through titles, counts, timing-sensitive detail, connector labels or error wording.

Unknown, unreadable and empty are not interchangeable. An empty verified table is different from a failed query; a filename match is not document evidence; a contact listed in an old file is not a verified current contact route. No auto-generated confidence percentage makes those claims true.

#### K4. Current source coverage and concrete gaps

These observations come from `F/workspace-ai/server/knowledge-records.ts`, `knowledge-file.ts`, `knowledge-docx.ts`, `knowledge-tools.ts`, `research-prompt.ts` and `research-types.ts`, inspected October 29.

| Source | Implemented retrieval | Contract gap / boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Organization | Actor-scoped profile query; allowlisted string fields such as name, mission, location and service area | No claim that every structured organization field is available. Select relevant fields; avoid a second org profile owned by AI. |
| People | Active profile contacts including role, email, phone, notes, details and provenance; profile revision | Fine-grained task/field minimization needs an explicit adapter contract. Search permission is not permission to expose all contact details to tools or public outputs. Contacts, members, assignees and approvers remain distinct. |
| Particles / plans | Current scoped graph; selected-plan metadata seeds discovery; descriptions, workspace notes and some planning/decision content | Preserve canvas identity. Target-revision checks do not cover every referenced canonical source. Some string bounds currently truncate without complete shared evidence metadata; unify that contract. |
| Core documents | Authorized non-Drive roadmap content through existing document-storage entitlement | Body text is bounded/plain-text extraction. No assumption of complete rich structure or provider-linked body coverage. Core source corrections use O3 writers. |
| Uploaded file search | Metadata query, filename filter, at most 20 matches; metadata is explicitly not content | MIME/name/size recognition does not prove extraction support. A 15 MiB placement-metadata ceiling is not an AI parsing limit. Search is not full-corpus semantic search. |
| Uploaded text read | Private library download for TXT, Markdown, CSV and JSON MIME types, up to 256,000 bytes; UTF-8 validation; rechecks metadata/revision/access after reading | Extracted content capped at 20,000 characters; tool returns share a 6,000-character detail budget per read call. CSV/JSON here are text evidence, not a typed editable dataset or query engine. |
| DOCX read | Up to 2 MiB compressed input; bounded ZIP/XML parsing through existing parser; body text only | Preserves decompression/XML limits. Images/scans, headers/footnotes and omitted formatting are not fully understood by this extraction. No arbitrary external file/URL access. |
| PDF, legacy DOC, images/OCR, Drive bodies | Metadata can identify several formats, but the inspected file-reading adapter does not extract these bodies | Return unsupported/metadata-only. Do not claim the platform has no other readers; qualify and register any additional adapter before advertising it to this harness. |
| Calendar | Up to 20 matching internal events by title, with dates/descriptions/revision | Not a complete external-calendar search or permission to schedule/change events. |
| Opportunities | Up to 20 title-matched non-dismissed opportunity records with report/status metadata | Matching does not establish eligibility, availability or acceptance. Read rights do not authorize provider edits/submission. |
| Public web | October30 S04 update: disabled in private-context research; only internal workspace readers are offered | An isolated approved-public-brief or mediated query boundary remains unimplemented. Public search must remain unavailable until K7 qualification; no prompt-only exception. |

Current `search_workspace` returns up to eight ranked matches across selected kinds; `read_workspace` accepts up to four known workspace references, including selected/discovered IDs; current authorized readers resolve exact IDs without requiring a search. Request-local maps/cache are not long-term user memory. Existing source access and post-read file checks are foundations, not evidence of universal field-level policy or global immediate revocation.

#### K5. Tool registration contract

Extend the existing capability manifest; do not create a competing tool registry. Every enabled tool has a server implementation plus a minimal model-visible description/schema. Tool discovery is itself scoped; it cannot reveal inaccessible integrations or private record names.

| Contract field | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Identity / version | Stable tool/capability ID and contract version; bind results and stored proposals to that version. Incompatible changes require revalidation or explicit migration. |
| Purpose / examples | Clear action-oriented description, when to use/not use, input/output examples and known limits. Avoid many near-identical tools whose distinction is unclear. |
| Input / output | Strict runtime schemas and inferred static types; bound strings, arrays, page size, bytes and nesting. Parse provider/tool responses too. No arbitrary SQL, code, executable UI, filesystem path or unrestricted endpoint. |
| Authority | Server derives actor and grants; resolves source references and field access; verifies provider connection ownership and current scopes. Caller/model arguments never supply authority. |
| Effect / disclosure | Distinguish internal read, external read with disclosure, draft/proposal write, canonical edit, publication and external effect. “Read-only” can still transmit sensitive data. |
| Revision / retry | Expected source revisions for mutations; stable operation identity; declared replay window and receipt/readback path. Non-idempotent tools cannot be automatically retried after uncertain delivery. |
| Approval | Reference the exact authorized intent/payload/revision/destination and its policy. Do not ask again for routine internal reads or edits already explicitly authorized. Owner plan approval, public release and external effects preserve their independent rules. |
| Limits / cancellation | Explicit time, payload, result, concurrency and spend bounds drawn from H7 and source constraints. Propagate abort when supported; cancellation cannot prove an already dispatched effect did not happen. |
| Errors / observability | Typed non-enumerating failures, retryability, correlation/receipt ID and safe next action. Log permitted metadata; not secrets or indiscriminate content dumps. |

L1–L7 supplies the concrete request, operation, receipt and event bindings for this tool contract. Mandatory call sequence: decode/validate arguments → authenticate/authorize and resolve references → check policy, revisions and budget → dispatch through canonical owner → validate response → persist/reconcile required receipt → return bounded typed result. Recheck after long-running reads when source ownership requires it. None of these checks may exist solely in a prompt.

#### K6. Initial tool surface and execution permissions

Names marked **target** are illustrative application contracts to bind to existing public feature APIs, not endpoints that already exist.

| Tool/capability | Input / result | Implementation and execution rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `search_workspace` | Bounded query + registered source kinds → authorized compact matches, coverage and availability | Existing `knowledgeSearchSchema`, `createKnowledgeTools`, `knowledgeLoader`. Keep current bounds; add missing shared evidence/field-minimization semantics. No write or outbound search implied. |
| `read_workspace` | Up to four explicit/discovered references → permitted detail with revision/extraction state | Existing `knowledgeReadSchema` and `readKnowledgeFile`. Known explicit references can be resolved through a bounded authorized adapter; do not require a fake search or accept guessed IDs as access grants. |
| List available capabilities — target | Current intent/scope → allowed view/tool descriptors and limits | Bind existing registry plus actor/source permission checks. Loading a description does not grant execution. Only relevant enabled tools enter model context. |
| Prepare/validate proposal — target | Typed proposed outputs/changes → preview, validation issues and applicability | Bind existing generation schemas, `bindResearch`, graph/domain validators and source checks. May persist a run result, but does not write accepted business content. No duplicate general-purpose planner database. |
| Save plan proposal | Saved run/target IDs → O2/O1 commit receipt | Existing research application; authorized staging write creates proposed artifacts. Do not label it read-only. Owner acceptance remains a separate typed command. |
| Edit source / save view — target bindings | Registered source ID, approved field patch, expected revision, operation ID → canonical receipt | O1/O3/O6 owners. Model may propose a command; dispatch only within current user authorization and source policy. Unsupported writers are unavailable, not executable placeholders. |
| Review/publish page | Draft/page ID, revision and reviewed selection → O7 receipt | Existing page actions. AI may prepare a draft; publication follows its exact review/authorization contract. No arbitrary public JSON or recursive disclosure. |
| Public form submission | Immutable form/version binding, permitted answers and request ID → O5 receipt | Endpoint remains missing. It is a respondent-facing action, not a default planning-agent tool for fabricating or submitting answers. |
| External research — target mediated boundary | Approved search brief/query and destination policy → public source excerpts | Apply K7 before dispatch. Existing model-managed web search is not by itself this boundary. |
| Retained memory read/write — optional target | Scoped preference/guidance reference or proposed change → versioned result | Disabled until K8 policy/store/writer exists. It is not an alias for unrestricted filesystem operations. |
| Send, purchase, schedule, destructive external operations | Tool-specific payload and explicit destination/effect authorization → provider receipt | Not granted by “plan anything,” inferred intent, proposal acceptance, an MCP connection or a skill. Expose only separately implemented, enabled and evaluated capabilities. |

Parallelize independent bounded reads when useful. Dependent reads wait for their references; writes follow H5 dependency/revision rules. A model-selected tool is a request to execute, never proof that execution is permitted. Connected apps must use the approved server-side credential/session boundary, not credentials embedded in prompts, schemas or logs.

#### K7. Outbound disclosure and untrusted content

There are separate disclosure boundaries: sending organization context to the selected model, sending a query/payload to an external tool, and publishing content to other people. Authorization for one does not authorize the others. D6 governs approved processing/funding routes; O7/D5 govern public audiences.

For external research, the shared harness must use a mediated query tool whose proposed payload the application can inspect and authorize before dispatch. A provider's built-in search may remain only in an isolated call supplied with an approved research brief and no unrestricted private context. Do not claim prompt instructions or a generic string sanitizer prove confidentiality. If the application cannot establish an allowed destination/payload, withhold that outbound action, offer a public/generalized query or request the specific missing authorization.

Define field-level minimum payload and approved destinations per connector. Keep personal contact information, nonpublic organization details, private documents, tokens, hidden source IDs and signed URLs out of public search by default. User approval must name the actual information/destination or an explicitly scoped standing policy; “use my data” is not blanket permission for arbitrary transmission. Pure internal retrieval under the existing approved scope requires no new modal per call.

Treat external pages, tool outputs, imported documents, remembered text and cited instructions as data. They cannot amend system policy, request secret access, authorize another tool or introduce executable code. Schemas constrain channels but do not eliminate prompt injection inside strings. Gate side effects and disclosure in deterministic handlers even when the model misinterprets evidence. Generated “next action” links are not automatically trusted instructions.

If a custom URL reader is later enabled, it needs explicit URL-scheme/destination checks, redirect revalidation, private-network/metadata-service blocking, bounded payload/decompression/time and content handling. Do not introduce an arbitrary fetch endpoint as a shortcut to file extraction. This is conditional protection for that tool, not a requirement to build a web crawler for MVP.

Revoking a connector disables future tool access and invalidates derived caches/proposals as applicable; it does not erase already transmitted data from another service. Keep that distinction in user-facing status and retention policy. Provider `store:false` is a request setting, not proof of account-wide zero retention or deletion.

#### K8. Optional retained-memory contract

A future retained-memory entry carries: stable ID, schema version, scope/owner, kind, concise value, basis, source references/revisions, author/confirmation, created/updated times, revision, access policy and configured expiry/retention. Keep personal preferences separate from organization guidance and source-derived summaries. Reuse canonical fields instead of storing a competing factual profile.

- **Write:** explicit “remember” requests or reviewed model suggestions invoke a scoped typed writer. Automatic inference alone cannot commit shared facts/preferences. If capability is disabled, say it was not saved; retain only ordinary run context as permitted.
- **Read:** authorize scope and underlying sources at retrieval. Relevant memories are bounded context, not privileged instructions. Never reuse personal memory from organization A in organization B merely because the actor is the same.
- **Conflict:** a current canonical fact supersedes an older derived summary for operational use. A preference cannot override policy. Preserve unresolved contradictions explicitly; do not silently merge opposing instructions into a fabricated consensus.
- **Correction:** authorized users can inspect, edit and remove retained entries through the owning interface. Changes use expected revisions. Model confidence or repeated mentions cannot replace confirmation.
- **Deletion/revocation:** stop retrieval and purge/invalidate derived caches/index entries under the configured retention policy. Avoid resurrecting deleted preferences from old summaries. Required audit records remain restricted and are not fed back as active memory; external-provider deletion guarantees are separate.
- **Portability:** switching providers does not grant them the whole memory store. Retrieve the minimum approved entries for that run and route. Provider-native memory tools adapt to these application contracts, not a shared filesystem namespace spanning tenants.
- **Enablement:** define access, retention/expiry and management UI before enabling writes. Until then, read current organization data and recover runs without pretending long-term memory exists. This remains outside the critical path to a useful planner.

#### K9. Skills, capability discovery and operational proof

Trusted skills are versioned task guidance and examples using registered tools/output schemas. Discover relevant descriptions first; load detailed instructions only when needed. Organization-authored instructions remain scoped user guidance; arbitrary retrieved text cannot become a trusted skill. A skill's requested tool must still be enabled, authorized and budgeted. No skill can provision a connector, change its access, install code or bypass owner review merely by naming it.

Required pass-5 evidence:

- Allowed versus denied source/field/tenant searches, including counts, titles and non-enumerating errors.
- Revocation/source change during read, cache reuse and resume; exact identity cannot be replaced by a matching name.
- Metadata-only, truncated, malformed/oversized and unsupported files remain visibly incomplete; extraction does not execute instructions or external reads.
- Sparse/zero-result retrieval is distinguished from unavailable sources; repeated equivalent calls do not waste budget.
- Malicious document/tool/memory instructions cannot cause secret reads, unauthorized writes, provider switching or outbound disclosure.
- Approved internal context cannot flow into public search/publication without its separate contract.
- Duplicate/unknown-outcome tool writes reconcile; disabled/unavailable tools never appear as successful effects.
- Retained memory, if enabled, has scoped read/write/correction/deletion and stale-summary tests; absence of that optional capability does not block ordinary planning.

Existing focused test anchors: `tests/acceptance/workspace-research-knowledge.test.ts`, `workspace-research-files.test.ts`, `workspace-research-context.test.ts`, `workspace-research-revisions.test.ts`. Their existence is not proof of all K1–K9 requirements. Use actual source fixtures and server/DB assertions; later model evaluation scores usefulness independently of authorization.

**Pass 4 checkpoint:** context, memory boundaries, tool registration and disclosure contracts are defined. Existing retrieval coverage is corrected; a general memory store, unified tool dispatcher and deterministic external-query boundary are not claimed implemented. D2–D6 remain explicit. Product behavior and measurable acceptance for O1–O8, H1–H10 and K1–K9 are defined in section 10 E1–E8.

Design basis: [Anthropic — context engineering](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/effective-context-engineering-for-ai-agents), [Anthropic — tool design](https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/writing-tools-for-agents), [Anthropic — application-owned memory tool operations](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/memory-tool), and [OpenAI — agent safety](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agent-builder-safety). Official guidance informs the contracts; no provider SDK, memory store or hosted framework is selected by these citations. Memory/tool documentation checked October 29; prior context/safety guidance reused from this planning review.

## 7. Data, outputs, and access

> **[NEEDS BUILD]** The proposed general saved-query/table binding has no completed owner established by this audit. Existing report tables are useful presentation foundations, not proof of an editable cross-source dataset system.
>
> **[PARTIALLY ANSWERED D3]** Table access depends on the acting user, source, creator/manager, assignments and explicit permissions. Cover Coach House records, uploaded/imported tables and linked Google Sheets; departments/job titles do not automatically grant access. Default inheritance is answered: new workspace-owned tables inherit workspace access; imports and Google connections require an explicit audience check. Exact source-specific writable fields remain adapter work. Editing a table must target its canonical source, not a copied display row.
>
> **[ANSWERED permission defaults; adapter details require qualification]** Simple View/Edit/Manage presets with separate sensitive actions for delete, export, sharing and connections; organization policy plus the resource grant plus source capabilities determine effective access. Assignment offers an explicit access grant when needed, never a silent one. **[ANSWERED default]** New workspace-owned tables inherit workspace access; imports and Google connections require an explicit audience check. The user approved View/Edit/Manage defaults; latest P4 direction gives form creators/managers/editors response access without a second reviewer setup. Responses remain private to authorized form collaborators; ordinary workspace membership or assignment alone never grants access. Source linking and copying are distinct actions. Provider storage and permissions must not be bypassed by a broader Coach House grant.

Saved views contain authorized source, columns, typed filters, stable sort, pagination, and presentation. Execute trusted queries; persist definitions rather than copied datasets. Edits use allowed fields and expected revisions. Counts identify their scope and must not reveal inaccessible records. Formula fields require a bounded, typed, allowlisted expression engine with complexity/execution limits; otherwise mark them unsupported. Never use eval or model-authored JavaScript/SQL for formulas.

Use explicit currencies and exact arithmetic. Distinguish instants, time zones, and date-only values.

> **[NOTE]** Some existing finance payloads use JavaScript numbers; this line is a target, not evidence of a universal exact-money implementation. Define units and conversions at the selected finance adapter. **[DEFERRED]** A formula engine and accounting rewrite are not prerequisites to an ordinary table.

Generated UI returns a plan proposal, change preview, saved view, document draft, clarification, source result, execution receipt, or recoverable error. Include target, permitted next actions, provenance where relevant, and truthful save state.

Generated copy uses the fewest words preserving facts, uncertainty, and required instructions. Do not repeat visible content in chat. Preserve exact identifiers; rewrite user content only when requested. Sanitize HTML/Markdown and URLs through existing field-specific renderers; reject scripts, event handlers, unsafe URL schemes, and arbitrary embeds. Apply the same policy to AI, imported, user-authored, and public content.

> **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** The concise-writing rule is accepted product direction; universal enforcement is not implemented. Put it in shared generation contracts and fixtures, not just this document. Preserve accessibility labels and task-critical instructions even when visible copy is minimal.

Separate app sign-in, inference permission, provider, and payment source. Users may select among organization-approved processing/funding routes; a preference cannot expand data access or spending authority.

**[ANSWERED — D6 integration boundary, October 29]** The user submitted Sign in with ChatGPT requests and has no approval yet. Treat it as a future integration, never an MVP dependency. Keep the existing application sign-in and authorized API path. User-reported application status is not evidence of partner eligibility. Identity and ChatGPT-plan inference grants remain distinct if approved later.

**[ANSWERED — D6 user setup]** Do not build customer BYOK. Users must not obtain, paste, manage or rotate provider API keys. The user rejected this integration because setup would be too difficult for the intended audience. It is excluded, not an optional launch requirement or scheduled follow-up. ChatGPT sign-in remains a separate deferred integration.

**[ANSWERED direction — D6 allowance]** Included AI usage can be increased through explicit purchases of additional credits; this is intended product behavior, not proof of a working checkout. Purchases increase available usage, not access rights, per-run safety limits or provider capacity. Amounts, prices and expiry/reset still need final policy. Organization-owner purchases and no automatic recharge are approved for this release. Reuse existing payment/credit ownership and require verified payment, idempotent credit grants and independent balance readback before claiming paid top-ups work. No payment or live activation is authorized by this documentation update.

**[ANSWERED launch direction; optional infrastructure remains conditional — D6 platform operation]** Coach House manages provider access; separate funding policy from request routing:

| Layer | Proposed MVP treatment | Constraint |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Platform-funded API | Included usage plus optional purchased credits through existing Coach House credit/reservation machinery and organization caps | Managed OpenAI and organization-owner purchases approved as product policy; exact allowance/terms and live activation remain separate; this document authorizes no spending |
| Gateway | Optional routing/observability infrastructure underneath an approved funding route | Not a third payer. Must preserve tenant isolation, source/tool authority, model capability checks, usage receipts and explicit processing/funding choices |
| SDK | Retain the inspected native OpenAI implementation as baseline; evaluate Vercel AI SDK as an adapter option | No SDK migration is required by this funding decision. Adopt only after proving structured results, tool behavior, cancellation, recovery and usage accounting for the enabled routes |
| ChatGPT sign-in / plan use | Deferred until Coach House receives the required eligibility/approval and qualifies the integration | No subscription sharing, teammate funding or unattended usage assumptions |

**[NOTE — internal routing]** Any gateway is an internal implementation choice, not another setup option for users. Preserve organization limits, usage accounting and explicit provider/funding policy. No silent paid fallback. Existing provider credentials remain server-managed; do not add customer key settings, key onboarding or subscription-credit claiming flows.

D6 now selects platform-managed OpenAI, organization-owner purchases and no automatic recharge. Exact allowance/pricing/terms and processing configuration qualification remain open. Customer BYOK is excluded; ChatGPT sign-in is deferred. These choices do not make three providers, a gateway or a new SDK prerequisites to S01 or the first qualified planner.

When an enabled capability requires bounded judgment, use a tested adapter on the selected approved route. A separately funded/provider route requires explicit authorization. If no authorized route supports the required judgment, mark that capability unavailable and explain the limitation; keep unaffected generation, editing and manual work available. A provider lacking Decisions does not, by itself, require an adapter. Never silently switch provider, processing destination, or billing source. Reuse existing reservations, caps, settlement, and reconciliation.

### P1. Policy authority — consolidation pass 5

**[USER DECISIONS RECORDED, October 29; updated by latest clarification]** Approved: View/Edit/Manage defaults; private responses accessible to form creators/managers/editors without a separate reviewer setup; direct staff corrections; immediate submission without a durable delivery buffer; familiar form controls with no respondent uploads; 30-day explicit organization closure; Coach House-managed OpenAI with organization-owner credit purchases and no automatic recharge. The latest form direction replaces the earlier explicit-reviewer/annotation-only proposals. Commercial amounts/terms, actual provider/source qualification and infrastructure retention targets remain distinct. No runtime implementation, payment, provider activation or permission mutation follows from these planning decisions.

Policy evaluation is deterministic and server-owned. Each effect evaluates the current authenticated actor, organization/resource scope, requested action, field restrictions, source capability and current policy revision. AI may explain an unavailable action; it cannot infer a grant from a job title, assignment, connection credential, prior preview or organization ownership. Existing organization-owner proposal approval and `canEditOrganization` publication rules remain distinct.

### P2. View / Edit / Manage and source adapters

**[ANSWERED D3 permission defaults; source adapters still require qualification]** Use three familiar presets backed by explicit action capabilities, not three new global organization roles. Resolve them through existing authorization where possible. Grants are resource-scoped and non-transitive across links. An external editor cannot inspect unrelated workspace records, search the organization, or assign new people merely because one item is shared.

| Preset / action | Allowed scope | Does not imply |
| --- | --- | --- |
| View | Read permitted fields and use permitted saved filters/sorts; personal presentation changes | Source edits, bulk export, other records, private responses or AI disclosure |
| Edit | View plus registered writable fields and explicitly supported create/update commands | Share, connect/rebind a source, change permissions/schema, permanently delete records or approve plan proposals |
| Manage | Edit plus scoped audience/configuration management within the actor's delegable authority; form management includes P4 response access/correction | Organization administration, provider ownership, billing, unrelated private datasets or bypassing source restrictions |
| Export / delete / publish / source connection | Separate named command capability; management UI may expose it only when authorized | A role label alone is not authority; each effect still uses its source owner's command and review/recovery contract |

Resource contracts refine these presets: form Edit includes question authoring and P4 response corrections; form Manage includes that same response access plus permitted configuration. A generic workspace/table grant does not imply access to every form.

Do not display a large permission editor by default: View/Edit/Manage first; show the relevant sensitive action controls when configuring sharing, export, deletion or a connection. Users cannot grant more authority than they can delegate. Show who can access the selected resource before confirmation; changing a saved view's audience cannot widen its source audience.

| Canonical source | Audience rule | Writable MVP fields / behavior | Required evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Existing Coach House record | Existing owner/authz plus explicit resource scope | Only fields the current source schema and writer expose; no generic unrestricted row patch | Two-user allow/deny, stale revision, reload readback |
| New workspace-owned table | Approved workspace inheritance; narrower grant when configured | Declared text, number, boolean, date and approved selection columns; identity/audit/derived fields read-only; schema changes separate from cell edits | Typed field validation, revision guard, scoped query/count and persistence |
| Imported local table | Explicit copy/import and audience review | After validated import, Coach House owns the new dataset; original file is unchanged; mapped editable columns only | Import receipt/provenance, duplicate retry, invalid-row handling without silent loss |
| Linked Google Sheet | Explicit audience check plus live source authority for the execution identity | Declared ranges/columns only; protected/formula/identity cells read-only. Enable cell writes only when adapter qualification proves its conflict strategy; otherwise show read-only with Open source | Current source capabilities, protected range handling, reauth/revocation, concurrent edits, unknown-result reconciliation |
| Coach House form responses | Form creator and authorized form editors/managers can read and correct; optional explicitly granted read-only reviewers; no public/workspace-wide inheritance | Direct answer-cell corrections through the source writer; technical identity/version fields read-only; deletion separate | Immutable published question meaning, stable response identity, revision checks and actor/time/changed-field metadata |
| Google Sheets form responses | Same form-collaborator default plus actual Google source authority; do not lend owner credentials | Direct permitted answer-cell corrections in the canonical Sheet; protected/formula/identity cells remain read-only; show source changes truthfully | Stable submission identity/readback, schema drift and concurrency checks; no platform answer backup or invented Google history |

**Connection identity is not user authority.** For interactive linked-source access, check the acting user's current provider access; an owner's token must not become a general proxy for teammates. If an adapter cannot verify that identity/access, use Open source or deny the operation. A published form is different: its manager explicitly authorizes narrow service delivery to one destination/range; respondents receive no provider token or source read access. This delegation cannot be reused for arbitrary table reads or edits. No new Google permission is created as a side effect of adding a Particle.

Google documents per-user file capabilities separately from ACL role labels. Qualification must inspect the appropriate current capabilities and underlying source restrictions; a UI role cannot replace these checks. [Google Drive sharing and capabilities](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-sharing)

Assignment may offer a separate scoped access grant when authorized; it never silently changes access. Removing a placement removes its membership/view, not the canonical source. Removing source access invalidates cached rows, counts, preview references and search results at their existing authorized boundaries. No platform owner backdoor into private provider files.

### P3. AI processing, purchasing and accounting

**[ANSWERED D6 launch policy; implementation and qualification pending]** Start with the existing Coach House-managed OpenAI route, one qualified default model configuration, organization-owner credit purchases, and no automatic recharge. No BYOK or ChatGPT-plan dependency. Keep provider adapters replaceable; do not build a provider picker with unavailable choices. Adding a provider/model requires the same structured-output, tool, privacy, usage and quality qualification; a registry entry alone does not qualify it.

- **Use:** authorized organization members consume that organization's available allowance within server-enforced limits. External invitees receive no AI-spending authority by default. Owning or editing a Particle does not grant billing rights. Existing user-level balances must not be silently pooled or transferred; reconcile current accounting ownership before an organization allowance is implemented.
- **Buy:** the organization owner initiates an explicit purchase showing price, credits, beneficiary organization and applicable terms. A model cannot buy credits. Payment redirect success cannot grant them; verify the paid provider object and process it idempotently, then independently read the balance. Duplicate events and distinct events for the same payment must not grant twice. Refunds/disputes use linked adjustments, not deleted ledger history or an automatic new charge.
- **Reserve:** show the run's maximum credit authorization before admission where confirmation is required; atomically reserve against remaining available balance. Concurrent runs cannot overspend. Existing tariff ceilings remain until deliberately revised; buying credits does not raise safety caps. No full-balance reservation for a small run.
- **Settle:** preserve the currently documented failed/canceled-run member-charge policy. Separate provider cost, member charge and source-write success. Release proven-unused reservations; unknown provider outcomes stay in bounded reconciliation rather than being replayed or silently charged twice. A recovered result/receipt is not a new inference purchase.
- **Explain minimally:** show active provider/model, available usage and a concise run receipt. On exhaustion: retain work, offer an authorized purchase or ask the owner; keep manual editing available. Never silently select a paid fallback.
- **Process:** retrieve only authorized task context; exclude raw submissions from default organization-wide retrieval. A reviewer using AI on responses needs response access and an approved processing route. Disclose processing independently from public sharing. Raw prompts/answers do not belong in analytics or payment logs.
- **Retain:** propose `store:false` for eligible foreground Responses calls; the application owns its saved results. This is not a zero-retention promise. Record the selected model/endpoint features, provider retention behavior and account configuration before activation. Hosted tools, uploaded files and third-party services require their own retention/deletion treatment.

OpenAI distinguishes API application-state retention, abuse monitoring and feature-specific exceptions; `store:false` does not eliminate every retention category. [OpenAI data controls](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/your-data)

Stripe documents duplicate and unordered webhook delivery. The credit writer must reconcile payment-object identity as well as processed event IDs. [Stripe webhook handling](https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks)

**[ANSWERED D6 behavior — latest user agreement]** One organization-wide included allowance refreshes monthly with no included-balance rollover. Consume included allowance first, then purchased credits. Purchased credits carry forward without expiration while the organization remains active. Start with a single **provisional $10 top-up** offered to the organization owner; no automatic recharge and no new pricing-tier redesign. Preserve saved work, manual editing, response intake/review and existing access when AI allowance is exhausted. AI credits meter inference, not ordinary use of the workspace.

**Settlement and closure:** failed/canceled runs charge users zero; provider costs remain recorded separately. Duplicate customer charges are reversed, with payment/credit adjustments linked to the original transaction. On explicit organization closure, refund unused purchased balance; included/promotional allowance has no cash refund value. Consumed purchased usage is not an unused-balance refund. Reconcile in-flight reservations before calculating a refund; do not drop unresolved liabilities when closure purges content. Preserve the minimum required billing records under their existing owner and actual retention requirements, without raw form answers. Payment/provider eligibility failures produce a visible unresolved refund requiring resolution, not a false success or silent forfeiture.

**Provider-loss containment — required S05 safety invariant:** customer charge and actual service expense are separate accounting dimensions. All incurred provider/tool spending counts against finite organization and platform spending limits, including failed, canceled, repaired and retried runs, even when the customer is charged zero. Customer credit release/refund never erases incurred expense or replenishes a service-spend window. Before dispatch, atomically reserve a conservative maximum exposure against both limits; bound concurrent work and check remaining exposure before every paid call. Reconcile usage exactly once under the existing run/accounting identities. Unknown cost retains conservative exposure until reconciled or explicitly accounted for by an authorized operator; a timeout or retry must not turn uncertainty into zero. Limits and windows are explicit server configuration, qualified before activation; no numeric values are invented here. At a limit, stop new paid dispatch, preserve manual work and recovery, and show a concise availability state. Credit purchases cannot bypass these safety limits. Repeated fail/cancel and concurrent cross-organization fixtures must prove bounded exposure and zero customer charge together. No second billing ledger or new customer setting is required.

**Accounting contract:** monthly periods have one server-owned anchor and immutable period identity; retries cannot refill twice. A run reservation identifies its included/purchased allocation and tariff version; monthly reset cannot erase an in-flight reservation or recreate last month's allowance when it releases. A later tariff or monthly boundary does not change that run's agreed charge. Track purchased lots and consumption/refunds so the remaining monetary value comes from the original purchase rather than today's conversion. Existing personal credit balances must not be silently transferred into organization balances. Use current accounting/payment owners and narrowly migrate only missing invariants; no second ledger.

**Still to measure, not invented prices:** included quantity per existing entitlement, credits granted by the provisional $10 pack, credit-to-operation conversion and final sustainable tariff. Before customer billing, S05 produces one versioned tariff from representative qualified cost data and customer value feedback; owner approves final amounts. Approval of the $10 candidate does not claim a profitable pack or authorize sales. Show balance and a bounded usage estimate before unusually expensive work; use the registered operation budget to determine when confirmation is needed, not a model's self-reported cost. Do not promise a fixed number of plans when their work varies.

**Bounded tariff/qualification deliverable inside S05:** use E4's varied generation/revision/retrieval/output cases and existing mocked accounting checks first. For an authorized capped non-production provider evaluation, record actual model/tool usage, successful/failed/canceled/retried runs, p50/p95 cost and latency, provider-cost certainty and delivered-output quality. Compare proposed allowance/top-up economics against the existing subscription entitlement and ordinary customer workload; do not derive retail value from token cost alone. Deliver a tariff candidate, explicit assumptions and a maximum evaluation budget for approval before paid tests or sales. No new provider benchmark platform, broad market study or live spending is authorized by this document.

**P3 proof in S05/E2:** concurrent organization reservations; one monthly refill despite retries; in-flight work across reset; included-before-purchased consumption; purchased carryover; failed/canceled charge zero; duplicate payment and refund events applied once; exhausted credits leave manual work/intake usable; account closure refunds the correctly reconciled unused purchased amount without exposing private responses. S10 reuses this accounting path.

## 8. Collaboration, sharing, and recovery

> **[IN PROGRESS]** Revision checking and public-page commands already exist. **[NEEDS BUILD]** Real public form intake and approved uploaded-asset publication remain gaps. **[NEEDS BUILD/VERIFY]** User-approved external viewer/editor sharing requires a scoped access/invitation contract and implementation verification beyond existing public/private pages.

Validate revisions and permissions at application time. Prevent silent overwrites. Undo applies a validated inverse against current state.

Assign operation IDs before requests; retry with the same identity. Scope deduplication to tenant, actor, command, and payload; reject mismatched reuse. Retain durable receipts for at least the supported retry/replay window. An expired receipt is not proof an effect never occurred: reconcile or reject ambiguous replay before executing again. Deduplicate effects and reconcile unknown outcomes. Cross-store operations require durable recovery; do not imply atomicity beyond the transaction boundary.

> **[NOTE]** Current graph receipts are bounded to 64 and the page service retains its latest-operation receipt. These are different supported guarantees, not a universal permanent ledger. Extend only for the selected effect/retry contract. Provider charges and remote execution require their own reconciliation.

Read authorized data and apply explicit manual edits directly. Keep AI changes reviewable. Publishing, sending, charging, inviting, and source deletion use separate authorized commands; proposal acceptance alone does not authorize them.

> **[ANSWERED]** Proposal approval belongs to the organization owner. Publishing currently uses `canEditOrganization`; those are distinct policies. Do not accidentally impose owner-only publication or let proposal acceptance grant source-edit/execution access.
>
> **[ANSWERED D5]** Include invited external viewers and editors alongside selected public sharing. Authorized users choose and revoke access. External editing uses authenticated, resource-scoped grants; a public bearer link never grants editing. Reuse existing roles where sufficient, but the current public/private page foundation is not proof that external collaboration already works. Source data and organization membership do not become accessible merely because a resource is shared.

Publish selected fields/assets beneath an organization card in a mobile stack. Default to reviewed snapshots; live bindings require explicit support. Private edits do not republish automatically. Public links are revocable and optionally expiring. When a link grants access, use a cryptographically random, unguessable token scoped to the publication/audience; possession never grants edit rights. Check expiry/revocation on each access path.

> **[COMPLETE] Source-confirmed foundation:** draft/review/publish/make-private service and snapshot-only public reader. **[VERIFY]** Connected release acceptance is still open. The inspected page schema has no expiry field; optional expiry is a target, not a shipped feature. Avoid a token-system replacement merely because the current public link is a UUID.

Public forms bind responses to immutable published versions. Validate submissions server-side, limit abuse, deduplicate retries, store responses privately, and return receipts.

> **[NEEDS BUILD]** Persist the published version and submission, enforce access/revocation/body limits/abuse controls, and expose private authorized results. The existing preview stores rehearsal answers locally; it must never display a real submission receipt.
>
> **[ANSWERED D4 product direction; implementation unproven]** Users choose Coach House or a linked Google Sheet for response storage. The form definition, published version, destination binding, permissions and delivery receipts still require a platform owner. **[ANSWERED]** The selected destination is the sole canonical response store, with only minimal delivery/recovery records in Coach House for Google storage; no retained platform backup of response bodies. Respondents cannot edit submitted answers in this release. Retention and the simplified form-collaborator/direct-correction behavior are approved below. Respondent access remains separate from private response access. Never silently substitute another destination.
>
> **[ANSWERED destination authority; proposed mechanics]** One canonical response destination per published form version; a destination change affects future submissions unless a separate migration is requested. Show pending/delivered/failed truthfully, use stable submission IDs and reconcile uncertain remote writes before retrying. Google delivery is not a database transaction with our server: do not claim exactly-once external writes without proving deduplication/concurrency behavior. Preserve version meaning; if responses can be edited directly in Google, define detection/history rather than claiming our database makes those cells immutable.

### Form defaults and retention — approved product direction

Latest user direction: offer familiar forms, keep responses private to the team managing them, allow direct staff corrections, and avoid a specialized compliance workflow. No respondent edits or uploads. These contracts do not claim implemented behavior or legal certification.

- **Collection:** authors choose ordinary form questions and validation using the P4 field menu. Do not add an ID/medical/payment classification flow, extra policy questionnaire or model-based content screening as a prerequisite. No respondent file-upload control. Basic input validation, private access and safe rendering remain required.
- **Audience:** the form manager selects public or invited/signed-in respondents. Submission never grants access to other responses. **[ANSWERED]** Form creators/managers/editors can see incoming responses without separate reviewer setup; responses remain private to authorized form collaborators. Publishing a form does not publish its dataset.
- **Notice:** show collecting organization, purpose, storage destination, retention period and a contact before submission. No unrelated reuse or AI processing outside the authorized task and processing policy.
- **Retention — [ANSWERED D4]:** no default 12-month deletion. Keep canonical responses until an authorized organization manager deletes them, with an annual review reminder rather than an expiration date. An organization keeping useful records for four years should not lose them merely because they are old. An optional explicit per-form deletion period may override the organization default when permitted by its policy. This is approved product behavior; it does not override an organization's applicable retention obligations or published notice. Explicit organization closure uses the 30-day window in P4; provider lifecycle, backup/history retention and transient cleanup require technical qualification; do not confuse them with live record retention.
- **Optional timed-deletion enforcement:** store the scoped policy/version, retention anchor and duration; derive eligibility from the chosen event (initially submission time), not login/activity. A bounded scheduled server job must recheck current policy, record identity, access/holds if supported, and concurrent changes before each effect; record idempotent deletion receipts. Show affected counts and warn before a policy change makes existing records eligible. Any recovery/trash window is additional retained time and must be disclosed separately from final purge. Do not expose an automatic-deletion toggle until the selected destination supports and proves it. The manual-retention MVP does not require a universal retention engine.
- **Google and backups:** a Google adapter may remove only our identified responses with explicit authority, never unrelated rows/the whole Sheet. Lost authority produces a blocked/pending result, not success. Provider history, backups, exports and independently downloaded copies are distinct; do not promise immediate erasure everywhere. P5 excludes durable local answer buffers for Google delivery; canonical response retention remains separate from minimal receipt retention.

**Reference check, October 29:** [Tally](https://tally.so/help/submissions-data-retention) makes timed response deletion optional, permits periods from minutes to years and retains incoming submissions indefinitely when disabled; deleted submissions have a separate 90-day trash period. [Google Vault](https://knowledge.workspace.google.com/vault/retention/retain-drive-files-with-vault) supports fixed or indefinite file retention. Vault file policies are not a per-response Sheets deletion adapter. [Airtable](https://support.airtable.com/articles/3982973603-creating-and-managing-data-retention-policies-in-airtable) separates revision/snapshot retention, trash and configurable inactive-base deletion. These examples support separating policy types; they do not establish Coach House implementation or regulatory sufficiency.
- **Corrections:** no respondent editing or edit links. The receipt names an organization contact. Authorized form creators/managers/editors can correct submitted answer cells directly, such as a misspelled name or email. Record who changed which fields and when; enforce revisions without requiring a correction-note workflow. Do not change the published question/version meaning. Google corrections write to Google and surface source changes truthfully.
- **Recovery and audit:** keep minimal access/change/delivery receipts without raw answers in logs. Google response bodies are processed only in flight for immediate delivery; do not retain them in a durable buffer or background delivery queue. Minimal identity/outcome records support reconciliation without storing answers, as defined in P5. Apply the same retention intent to controlled caches/exports; never promise deletion of recipients' copies.

Revocation denies subsequent authorized reads/writes and invalidates controlled caches, subscriptions and asset grants as applicable. It cannot recall content already downloaded or copied by a recipient. Do not expose connected private records or persist signed private URLs publicly. Preserve failed/offline input without promising conflict-free offline editing.

### P4. Simple form authoring, team responses and closure

**[ANSWERED; latest user direction supersedes earlier reviewer/annotation proposals]** The normal flow is **create questions → share → see responses → edit cells**. Reuse and extend the existing builder; do not rebuild a form platform or add a privacy wizard. Match the supplied familiar question-card interaction using Coach House components, not copied branding. The screenshots define behavior and the target type menu, not proof of current implementation.

#### Form field and interaction contract

| Target control | Typed answer / behavior | Existing versus required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Short answer / Paragraph | Bounded string; optional supported validation; paragraph is multiline | Existing short/long answer; preserve existing email/number types and saved values, expose their validation naturally without breaking storage |
| Multiple choice / Dropdown | One stable option ID; dropdown is a compact presentation | Existing single choice; dropdown implemented locally in S08 November1. Single-choice Other implemented locally in S08 November1, with a separate bounded text value; dropdown stays ordinary choice |
| Checkboxes | Unique set of stable option IDs; optional Other text | Other implemented locally in S08 November1; reserved version/question-scoped identity plus separate bounded text, never label-based identity |
| Linear scale / Rating | Integer within configured bounds; endpoints/scale labels or accessible rating symbols | Linear scale and rating implemented locally in S08 November1; shared typed controls/validation, keyboard and numeric alternatives. Hosted/release qualification remains |
| Multiple choice grid | Stable row IDs mapped to one column option ID per answered row | Implemented locally in S08 November1: version-bound membership/required rows; mobile stacked controls; up to20 rows and10 columns. Hosted/release qualification remains |
| Checkbox grid | Stable row IDs mapped to sets of column option IDs | Implemented locally in S08 November1: unique permitted column IDs and required rows; same bounded axes/mobile controls; public answers and staff corrections |
| Date / Time | Valid local calendar date or local time, not an implicitly converted UTC instant | Native pickers and timezone-free validation implemented locally in S08 November1; a distinct scheduled-event contract may separately request a timezone |

Question cards support inline title/options, type selection, Required, duplicate, delete and drag/keyboard reorder. Keep ordinary controls visible and secondary settings in one small menu. Type changes preserve compatible values; incompatible options or published response mappings require an explicit change, never silent data loss. Published versions keep stable question/option/row/column identities; adding or changing a draft does not reinterpret older responses. Duplication generates new identities. No respondent file upload, scoring, conditional workflow engine or payment integration is implied. Author-added decorative images are separate from respondent uploads and may reuse qualified assets without blocking basic form delivery.

**AI and external forms:** AI can draft the same typed form definition a person edits, or suggest using an existing/new Google Form when that is simpler or already fits the organization's workflow. Represent an external form as a useful link/step on the plan. Do not require rebuilding it in Coach House. A linked Google Form is not the same feature as a Coach House form writing to Google Sheets. Do not claim automatic external-form creation, editing, embedding or response sync without a real qualified integration; link-out is sufficient for the suggestion. Native basic forms remain in scope; this option does not silently remove them.

#### Team access and direct corrections

Creating a form grants its creator form management; authorized form editors/managers can view incoming responses and edit answer cells. Make this meaning clear in the existing sharing control; no second mandatory reviewer list or publication setup. View-only plan/public access does not grant private response access. A manager can grant scoped read-only response review when needed. Source restrictions still apply: connecting a Sheet does not give teammates the connector owner's credentials or Google permissions. If the source blocks access, show that boundary and the existing access/connect action rather than silently broadening it.

Correct cells through the canonical response writer with stable response identity, validation, current revision and independent readback. Record actor/time/changed-field identifiers; do not turn this into a mandatory annotation or approval workflow. Technical submission ID, timestamps and published-version linkage are not editable answer cells. Keep a user's unsaved edit when another edit conflicts. For Google, update the canonical cells and do not retain answer copies in platform logs/history. A submission retry must return its existing receipt and never restore the pre-correction answers. Respondents still cannot edit after submission. Export/deletion/sharing remain separately authorized actions within the existing permission system.
