**Notice:** use a concise pre-submit organization/contact, destination and actual retention explanation; disclose enabled AI processing where relevant. No new blanket checkbox or sensitive-data questionnaire. Public sharing never includes the response table automatically.

#### Explicit organization closure

**[ANSWERED: 30 days]** Organization owner requests closure; stop public intake/publication and new AI work immediately. Allow a **30-day restricted export/cancel window**, then purge Coach House-controlled organization content. Export still requires each dataset's authority: closure does not broaden response access. An owner may request earlier permanent deletion with explicit consequences. Canceling a subscription, a payment failure, removing a member, disconnecting Google and deleting one person's login are not organization-closure requests. Closing one organization must not delete a shared user identity or another organization's records. P3/D6 governs refund of unused purchased AI balance; closure never converts included allowance into cash.

**Closure mechanics:** stop admitting new writes, reconcile in-flight canonical effects and revoke organization-scoped runtime/connector credentials. Preserve only the restricted access needed for authorized export and canceling closure; no new submissions or background AI actions. Canceling closure does not silently republish pages or restore revoked grants. Google files remain in their external owner's account; remove Coach House bindings/grants, not entire external files. A separately authorized source deletion must finish or report blocked before disconnecting its credential. At the deadline, export delays do not silently extend retention; show the deadline in advance and report failed cleanup explicitly.

**Lifecycle schedule — proposed engineering defaults, subject to measured qualification before activation:**

| Data class | Retention / deletion rule | Required boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Canonical responses, including staff-corrected answers | Until authorized deletion or the explicit configured retention rule; closure follows the approved 30-day window | No age-based default purge; deleting a placement or archiving a plan does not delete its source |
| Individually deleted Coach House response | Deny access at accepted deletion; target removal from live controlled stores within 24 hours; no new user-facing trash feature in this release | Confirm irreversible intent; include derived excerpts and controlled exports. Pending/failed purge is not completed erasure |
| Google response deletion | Delete only the stable identified response through a qualified authorized adapter | Recheck identity after row movement; no row-number-only delete, whole-file deletion or promise to erase provider history. Revoked access reports blocked |
| Raw response caches / telemetry | No durable answer cache; request-local processing only; exclude answers from logs and traces | Authorized browser view memory is cleared on scope exit/revocation where controlled. Already downloaded copies cannot be recalled |
| Temporary Coach House-generated exports | Expire download authorization and delete temporary artifact within 24 hours | Recheck access at generation and download; background generation cannot expand export rights. User-downloaded copies have separate ownership |
| Minimal intake delivery receipts | Terminal records: 30 days after resolution; unresolved records: at most 90 days from admission, followed by expiry without claiming a successful submission | No answer bodies or unnecessary respondent identity. Admission credentials bind a fixed expiry: a purged receipt never makes an expired operation eligible for a new write. Unknown outcome is not proven absence; never resubmit automatically |
| Controlled backups and object history | Use the verified configured recovery horizon, disclosed separately from live deletion | Inventory database, object storage, PITR, manual exports and log drains. Do not assume database backup includes uploaded files or promise a per-row purge from immutable backups |
| Deletion suppression records | Minimal opaque identifiers until every backup capable of restoring the deleted data has expired or been safely retired | Restore in isolation; replay deletions/revocations before reopening access, subscriptions or AI retrieval. Protect suppression records independently of the database restore point |

The receipt schedule applies to public intake recovery only. It does not shorten canonical run/application history, payment records or security evidence by analogy. Those existing owners must have separately documented purposes and retention; answer content may not be retained as billing evidence. Any exceptional retention obligation needs a named basis, restricted scope and end/review condition; do not invent a universal legal-hold system for MVP.

**Qualification, not a new product choice:** before activation, S07/S08 records actual configured backup/history/log horizons, expiry enforcement, deletion worker ownership and retry/alert behavior, and verifies the proposed time targets. If a target cannot be met, revise the published policy before launch; do not silently advertise it. No production settings were inspected or changed in this pass. Supabase documents plan/configuration-dependent database recovery windows and explicitly excludes Storage API object contents from database backups; source documentation is not evidence of our account settings. [Supabase backup documentation](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups)

**Required proof:** deleting a response removes its derived access; closing one organization leaves another untouched; an owner without dataset access cannot export answers; subscription cancellation does not delete data; an expired receipt cannot admit a duplicate write; uncertain Google outcomes never become successful by expiry; a restored backup cannot resurrect deleted records or revoked public grants; a disconnected Google account does not cause unrelated row/file deletion. Exercise worker failure and retries and independently inspect each controlled store. A documentation check alone passes none of these runtime gates.

### P5. Immediate submission and visible responses

**[ANSWERED D4 — user decision, October 29]** Submit directly to the selected canonical destination. Do not build a durable answer buffer, delayed delivery queue or 24-hour retry service. The buffer was proposed for provider-outage recovery; the user chose immediate submission instead. Coach House storage and Google Sheets remain supported destination choices. A response becomes available to authorized reviewers when its canonical write is confirmed, not after a batch or scheduled delivery.

**Submit contract:** validate the immutable published form version, audience, destination, request limits and answers; establish a stable submission identity and minimal recovery record; perform the destination write during the bounded submission request. Return success only after canonical persistence is confirmed. For Google, verify the destination row/reference through the qualified adapter; for Coach House, confirm the database commit. An idempotency record contains no retained Google response body and does not turn into a delivery queue.

| State / event | Behavior | User-visible truth |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Editing | Keep answers in the current form session; do not expose partial typing as submitted responses | Editable form |
| Submitting | Disable duplicate dispatch, preserve current answers and submission identity; perform direct validated write | Submitting |
| Confirmed write | Return canonical receipt/reference; invalidate the authorized response view and display the new response | Submitted; reviewer sees confirmed data |
| Proven failure before write | Keep answers in the current form; allow deliberate retry after correction or restored access | Not submitted; retry available |
| Timeout / lost connection after dispatch | Keep answers and original identity; query outcome before allowing another write | Checking submission; do not claim failure or success |
| Confirmed prior write on recovery | Return the existing receipt without issuing another write | Submitted once |
| Outcome remains unknown | Retain identity-only reconciliation where permitted; offer status/contact and block blind duplicate submission | Submission unconfirmed |
| Destination unavailable / access revoked / schema mismatch | Fail safely; do not switch storage or accept answers for later background delivery | Not submitted if absence is proven; otherwise unconfirmed |

**No retained answer queue:** Google-bound answers exist only in the respondent's active form state and necessary in-flight processing, then at Google after delivery. Exclude raw answers from server logs, tracing, durable job payloads, dead-letter queues and platform backups. No automatic offline queue, local-storage answer archive or promised draft recovery after the browser closes. Normal browser/server memory during request handling is not a new delivery store. Coach House-selected responses remain canonical platform records under the approved retention policy.

**Visible as submissions arrive:** the private response view uses the same destination adapter and authorization as submission/readback. A confirmed local write triggers authorized view invalidation/subscription updates; Google writes trigger source re-read rather than displaying a second canonical copy. An already-open review view updates without requiring a full page reload, using supported subscriptions or bounded refresh while visible. On reconnect/focus, re-fetch and show stale/unavailable state if the source cannot be read. Respect active filters/pagination: update authorized counts and offer a new-response affordance without jumping the reviewer's selection or overwriting their cell-edit draft. Responses submitted through Coach House and later edits made directly at Google must remain distinguishable. Direct Google changes use the adapter's supported freshness mechanism, not an invented instant-push guarantee.

**Duplicate safety:** double-click, concurrent requests and a lost success response must resolve to one canonical submission. A stable ID alone does not prove remote deduplication: qualify the Google adapter's destination identity, concurrency and reconciliation strategy before enabling it. Do not retry a write whose absence is unproven. Receipt lookup requires an unguessable capability or authenticated scoped access; a submission UUID alone is insufficient. Do not collect an email address just to support recovery. A receipt never grants response editing or access to other submissions.

**Scope:** complete submitted responses appear promptly. This decision does not authorize keystroke capture, live viewing of unfinished applications, respondent editing after submission or autosaving incomplete responses. P4 records approved 30-day organization closure; controlled backup and receipt schedules still require technical qualification. No additional product questionnaire is needed for basic form operation.

### P6. Policy acceptance and remaining decisions

Specification fixtures must cover:

1. An ordinary workspace editor without form-collaborator or response access cannot read a private response table; authorized form creators/editors/managers can.
2. A form creator/editor/manager can view new responses and correct a name/email cell without another reviewer setup; an external form editor cannot enumerate neighboring forms or records.
3. Linking a Sheet neither lends the connector's full identity to teammates nor creates Google grants. Protected cells and unqualified concurrent writes stay unavailable.
4. Dropping a local table offers an explicit imported copy and audience; dropping an existing document preserves its original source permissions.
5. Revocation between preview and apply denies the write and clears authorized cached views. Assignment alone changes no grant.
6. Immediate write confirmation, proven failure, revoked provider access and unknown remote outcomes produce distinct receipts. Lost responses/double-clicks do not duplicate submissions; answers remain in the active form for safe retry. No durable answer queue exists. Already-open authorized review views update from confirmed canonical submissions without a full page reload; filters and edit drafts remain intact.
7. Two simultaneous AI requests compete against one available balance correctly; duplicate payment events/objects grant once; unknown settlement cannot double charge.
8. No AI route, access grant or purchase policy is activated merely by editing this planning document. A retry of the original submission cannot overwrite a later staff correction.

**Policy decisions updated.** Latest user clarification simplifies forms: creators/managers/editors see and correct responses, no respondent uploads/edits, familiar question controls, optional Google Forms link-out, and 30-day explicit organization closure. This supersedes separate reviewer setup and annotation-only correction. Immediate submission and managed OpenAI/owner purchases/no auto-recharge remain approved. Commercial quantities and technical source/retention qualification remain explicit; P3 now records approved allowance/reset/carryover/refund behavior. S01–S11 reconciliation is recorded in section 9; section 15 records the completed consistency/coverage review and remaining commercial/qualification gates. Complete-plan approval remains required before application implementation.

## 9. Implementation dependencies and exit evidence

This is the sole implementation sequence. These are dependencies between reusable capabilities, not a prescribed organizational workflow, six blank-slate rebuilds or an active sprint. Existing foundations remain preserved. D1 fixes capability-based scope. Apply answered D2/D5 direction and the approved P2/P4/P5/P3 permission, reviewer, immediate-submission and managed-AI policies. Resolve only remaining D3/D4/D6 details at their affected boundaries. A gate never silently chooses product policy. The six document-development passes are separate from these implementation gates.

| Gate | Dependencies | Reuse first | Required usable outcome | Exit evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Output contracts | Capability scope under D1; relevant D2–D6 decisions before affected contracts are finalized | Particle registry, source owners, commands/proposals; reference identity/view distinctions | Each enabled output maps to its real reader, renderer, editor, writer, permission, projection and recovery; specify migrations only where needed | Contracts have owners and acceptance cases; invalid types/references rejected; legacy graph fixtures load; source versus placement edit scope defined |
| 2. Manual plan and outputs | Gate 1 for the affected output | Existing frames, measured ELK, projection/navigation, document editors, report compositions and private form builder | Create/edit/reuse/navigate supported outputs; save and reopen; include optional walkthrough through the existing focus path | Click/drag/editor arbitration; exact return location; locked geometry/drafts survive; canonical content reused without duplicate counts; manual writes respect authorization/revisions |
| 3. AI-assisted plan and revision | Gate 2 commands/editors for outputs the AI may propose; approved managed-OpenAI route plus qualified processing/accounting configuration | Authorized retrieval, bounded research, proposal application and credit machinery | Objective produces a visible supported proposal; focused requests preserve unrelated work; only authorized acceptance applies changes | Organization fixtures; source grounding; invalid/stale/denied changes rejected; cancellation/retry recovery; cost/latency traces; conditional judgment capabilities tested if enabled |
| 4. Publication and real intake | Gate 2 form/output contracts; D4 collection rules and D5 audiences; Gate 3 only for AI-specific scenarios | Existing page draft/review/commit/revoke/snapshot reader/editor; form version/answer schemas | Selected branded mobile page and, when forms are selected, a real submission endpoint/store bound to an immutable published form version, with immediate confirmed submission, receipts, authorized form collaborators and an updating editable private response view | Public/private isolation; current authorization; supported asset handling; revoke behavior; submit retry saves once; independent destination readback; no durable answer queue; already-open authorized review updates; two-user conflicts handled |
| 5. Saved data views and composition | Source store/writer and D3 fields; Gate 4 intake only for response-backed adapters; Gates 2/3 as applicable | Existing registered views/editors and canonical source commands; new adapters only where missing | Save/edit/reuse authorized source-backed tables; response views support authorized answer corrections without changing published question meaning; capabilities compose with plans and permitted sharing | Query matches authorized stored data, including empty state/pagination/counts; permitted edits survive reload; unauthorized access denied; concurrency and readback checks pass |
| 6. Release | Required capability gates and cross-capability checks passed | Feature flags, hosted quality checks, migration/recovery and accounting paths | Release the bounded MVP without expanding the future catalog | Current-revision hosted gates, migration/rollback evidence where applicable, provider/funding activation and cross-capability acceptance |

**Intake dependency:** a response-backed table cannot be accepted before the immutable form binding, real submission persistence, response access policy and duplicate-submit recovery exist. UI fixtures may support development but are not evidence that the dataset works. If D3 selects an existing non-response dataset instead, its canonical store/writer is the prerequisite; do not require unrelated intake or silently change D3.

**Cross-cutting rules:** authorization, revisions, collaboration conflict handling and recovery belong in every affected gate, not only the publication/release gate. Independent work may overlap once its inputs are defined; acceptance must respect these dependencies. Reference-specific cases are in section 14; section 10 defines broader proof. Neither is a second phase table.

Implement one complete interaction per slice. Sharing and editable outputs remain part of the MVP. Defer broad automation, native messaging/social publishing, end-user payment collection, connector marketplaces, custom domains and full Accelerator conversion unless explicitly included in the supported capability scope. Existing technology is reused, not rebuilt because a gate names it. Explicit AI credit purchases are approved product scope and belong to S05 accounting; deferring end-user payment collection does not defer that requirement.

### Execution packets — reconciled consolidation pass 6

These packets refine the gates above; they are not a second roadmap, an active sprint or permission to implement. `S01`–`S11` identify bounded work within the existing dependency sequence. “Owner” means the canonical feature/module, not an invented staffing assignment. Use section 15's `F/` and `W/` source abbreviations.

**First implementation packet, only after complete-plan approval and implementation authorization: S01.** It connects existing manual objective/plan/note behavior to the shared capability contract, with save/reload and compatibility proof. It requires no new model, funding decision, public audience or universal storage rewrite. If current code already satisfies a requirement, preserve it and record its evidence rather than reimplementing it.

**Packet entry rule:** inspect only the named owners and relevant changes since this source review; preserve the dirty worktree and other lanes. Record the exact included variants/actions before editing. A registry entry may advertise only its proven reader/editor/writer/projection. Missing product decisions stay in D2–D6; technical details use the existing stack and contracts. No broad audit, new framework or future-catalog build is a prerequisite.



**Packet-sized execution context:** treat this full document as the reference specification, not mandatory repeated reading for every task. At packet entry, load its usable outcome, dependencies, binding contracts, relevant variants/output contracts and acceptance cases, plus the current owner files and checkpoint. Reuse already verified context; follow cross-references only when a dependency or ambiguity requires them. Record the applied specification revision and exact proof in the existing packet checkpoint. Do not create separately edited packet-spec copies or drop security, permission, recovery and accounting dependencies to save context. A completion report states what works, what evidence proves it and what qualification remains.

### Execution policy — fast delivery within fixed contracts

**Apply the deeper-thinking framework once, then by exception.** A senior engineer would identify the smallest complete interaction, canonical owners and actual failure boundaries before adding infrastructure. Start from current schemas, writers, saved records, permissions, traces and existing test evidence—not feature descriptions alone. Use targeted source/graph inspection, typed runtime contracts, bounded integration slices and risk-based verification. Independent proof is canonical readback, denied-access checks, preserved unrelated state, measured behavior and required CI; neither the model's confidence nor a plausible preview is proof. Reassess only when changed evidence, dependencies or failures materially affect a decision.

**Each authorized packet begins with four short lines, derived from this spec:**
- **Outcome:** observable usable behavior within authorized scope.
- **Changes:** existing owners and necessary edits; no speculative framework.
- **Constraints:** applicable contracts, dependencies and approval boundaries.
- **Verification:** smallest sufficient checks and explicit completion condition.

Then execute. Make routine implementation choices autonomously. Resolve technical details when their packet needs them; do not reopen settled product decisions or demand implementation detail for future capabilities first. Build the complete supported path before generalizing variations: create, review where required, apply, edit, reload and recover. This delivery order does not reduce required MVP scope. Start dependency work with the smallest actual integration and expand only for concrete uncertainty.

Use parallel agents only for independent work with clear ownership, stable interfaces and one integration owner; no duplicate reconnaissance or mandatory agent count per phase. Reuse source context and verification until invalidated. Batch Graphify and checkpoint updates according to repository rules. Authorized routine reversible edits need no extra review ceremony; existing owner approval of AI proposals, publication and consequential-action authority remain intact. Do not claim undo/history exists where it has not been implemented.

Stop verification when the packet outcome and applicable gates pass. Continue automatically only into the next explicitly authorized packet; stop at the end of authorized scope. Reopen planning only for a concrete contradiction, blocked dependency, failed contract or missing decision that changes scope or risk. A routine implementation detail is not a permission question. Report outcome, evidence and material unresolved gates briefly. October 30 authorization starts S01 implementation and completion tracking; this policy does not authorize paid evaluation, deployment or external effects. Continue later packets only within explicit authorization.

**Reusable execution prompt:**
> Implement [authorized packet] in the existing Coach House stack. Deliver [its usable outcome] using its registered capabilities and canonical writers. Follow the specified selection, editing, navigation, motion and recovery behavior; keep the interface minimal, focused and consistent with existing tokens and surfaces. Output working UI, state and persistence, plus one concise checkpoint. Begin with Outcome / Changes / Constraints / Verification, then execute. Reuse valid context and evidence. Run the smallest sufficient affected checks; expand only for relevant shared contracts, permissions, money, external effects, failures or required release gates. Stop when completion criteria pass. Continue only through authorized packets. Ask only for a material unresolved decision; preserve required approvals. Do not add scope, arbitrary effects, new frameworks or repeated broad planning.

**Shared packet contract:** A1–A5 owns authority; B1–B5 owns selected dependencies; U1–U12 and V01–V14 own presentation; H/K owns planning/context/tools; L1–L7 owns admission, operation identities, events and recovery; P1–P6 owns policy; O1–O8 owns source output bindings; E1–E8 owns proof. Packet text binds these contracts, not a competing copy of their rules. Type checks validate shape; runtime schemas, authorization, revisions, semantic validation and canonical readback remain independent gates.

**Source versus new work:** source paths below are inspected anchors, not a claim that every listed integration exists. Recheck the affected owner at implementation entry. Preserve current saved records and compatible schemas; any necessary new fields have version/migration/rollback fixtures. Existing functionality closes a requirement with evidence. An empty interface, fixture-only preview or schema-only package does not close the usable outcome.

**Approved scope versus readiness:** manual editing, supported sharing, immediate form intake, private response review, saved editable source views and AI credit purchases are required where named. A failed adapter may remain disabled during development, but silently omitting required functionality is not an MVP release. Public response-backed views depend on separate explicit publication support; private response review is the required default.

This qualifies explicit focus at the time requested, not continuous expansion/overlay tracking.

Canvas objectives now edit responsibility through the external rail; hidden/library objectives retain the inspector selector, with no second selector in the visible objective inspector. Older records require no migration. No invitations, account grants or approver roles are created.
- [x] **Scope and source checks:** this field is accepted only on intrinsic objectives. Source-backed objectives retain their source owner. Ordinary work records reject it: a reused step's assignments must belong to its specific plan membership, not global content. New/reassigned roles are checked against the actor's organization People records inside the existing writer; retained unavailable references can be removed without blocking unrelated edits. Public projection remains explicit and excludes responsibility.
- [x] **Contextual step responsibility:** optional strict plan/proposal `assignments` bind one v1 responsibility value to each distinct member ID. The plan row exposes the shared People selector in an explicit plan-scoped popover, using existing revision-checked commands and proposal review. Another use of the same work record remains unchanged. Assignment edits advance plan revision; accepted proposal contents must match. Detachment removes that membership assignment; undo restores it. AI revisions preserve retained assignments and never assign newly generated work. New scopes and changed roles recheck source access; retained unavailable assignments remain removable. Failed saves retain drafts; concurrent revisions disable stale saves and offer Load latest. No implicit grants, invitations or public disclosure.
- [x] **Objective canvas assignment rail:** intrinsic objectives show one external accountable-person control, up to two contributor initials avatars and +N. The shared People selector moves out of the visible objective inspector. Explicit save retains failed/stale drafts; current names and unavailable references remain source-owned. The measured placement reserves 8px separation plus the full 44px touch target on both pointer types. Handles remain on the card; expansion, placement, focus fallback and resizing include rail bounds without persisting a new card family. Assignment and inspector drafts share one guard without clearing each other.
- [x] **Shared-step canvas assignment rail:** one external entry uses explicit workspace/objective/plan/proposal identity. A sole membership is labeled; multiple memberships require a context choice. Starting a new walkthrough selects its exact context instead of reusing a prior manual choice; an open draft keeps its original scope. Accepted-plan edits remain organization-owner-only; pending proposals allow their author or organization owner. The application supplies one checked actor lookup to canvas and sidebar. Visible steps omit the sidebar selector; hidden/library steps retain it. Existing revision-checked commands update only the selected membership, leaving shared/source content and other uses unchanged. Failed/stale drafts survive; a concurrently removed membership keeps the draft visible until discarded. The 52px rail reservation covers measured placement, inspection, focus and ordinary/image resizing; card handles and persisted sizes remain unchanged. Local fixtures qualify these paths; authenticated and multi-client acceptance remain open.
- [x] **Progress — local implementation:** intrinsic objectives support optional versioned Work/Time/Both/Ongoing configuration through the existing inspector/save/conflict/retry path. Accepted plan members are required by default; explicit `optionalParticleIds` use existing owner-only plan commands and proposal review. The step menu offers canonical work status changes; support widgets remain excluded. One deterministic domain selector feeds objective rows, board/focused previews and plan summaries. Required active work is deduplicated by canonical identity (including repeated source references); parent summaries of counted work, optional work, support records and pending/unselected branches do not inflate totals. Zero required work has no percentage, rounding cannot manufacture 100%, and partial-reader projections label visible-scope counts. Ongoing shows work context without percentages. Time has independent valid UTC start/end values and upcoming/active/elapsed states; Both never averages time with work. One shared presentation clock updates elapsed time without writing status. Existing current/target values remain separate measurements.
- [x] **Outcome confirmation — local implementation:** accepted-plan scope/progress edits and objective status changes enforce organization-owner authority in the existing canonical writer; source-owned objectives retain source authority. Explicit Confirm outcome stamps server-owned actor/time, required-work identities, measurement configuration and outcome text. 100% work/time never completes an objective automatically. Later work scope, time/mode or outcome-text changes show Complete · Review scope; reopening clears the confirmation. Forged receipt edits are rejected, and duplicating an objective resets its completion instead of copying evidence. Public projection stays explicitly reviewed text and excludes live work counts, assignments and confirmation metadata. No migration, grants, execution scheduler or billing/provider change.
- [ ] **Final acceptance:** authenticated source/reload, collaboration and assignment-rail acceptance remain; local fixtures are not live database or release proof.

#### S04. Grounded context with enforced tool disclosure

**Implementation status: In progress — workspace-only disclosure, current-context/source revalidation and versioned evidence coverage with contact-search minimization and saved-result dependency checks implemented locally. Return/reconnect invalidation, visible saved-source coverage, typed saved-result recovery and admission/uncertain-reservation recovery are implemented locally. Fine-grained disclosure, remaining context/tool failure contracts, live authorization qualification and approved public-research boundary remain open.**

- [x] **Private-context research cannot offer public web search.** `research-provider.ts` exposes only `search_workspace` and `read_workspace`, regardless of legacy search-budget configuration. Final round exposes no tools. Calls to unoffered tools stop without dispatch. Generated URL annotations are not admitted as source evidence. Workspace citations and proposal/review/reload remain on their existing path.
- [x] **Capability and quote agree.** `research-v4-workspace` invalidates stale quotes; reservations exclude unavailable search and no longer require its pricing configuration. UI states “Uses workspace sources. Web search is unavailable.” Read-tool descriptions distinguish internal retrieval from disclosure to the configured model. No new processing/provider approval is inferred.
- [x] **Unexpected provider activity remains recoverable.** Unexpected built-in web-search output fails to the existing correlated-run reconciliation path; it cannot produce an accepted proposal or a zero-cost success. This contains application tool exposure; it does not claim control over a provider that violates its request contract.
- [x] **Recheck context and source snapshots locally.** Tool operations reload the actor-authorized target; reject actor/canvas changes and stale target revisions. Before each model dispatch and final result, compare selected-plan members and disclosed records against current exact-ID readers. Missing, revoked, changed or unavailable evidence stops the run without another provider dispatch or successful proposal; already transmitted data cannot be recalled. No permission-blind search/result cache remains. Repeated searches reload current records; profile reads coalesce only inside a fresh check. File extraction alone is cached per run, behind fresh entitlement and exact file metadata checks. Freshness reads use groups of at most four and honor run cancellation/deadline checks between bounded operations. Final application uses exact source IDs too; the existing canonical writer and ledger remain authoritative.
- [x] **Versioned evidence and bounded contact search:** tool responses and persisted citations carry server-produced v1 coverage (`metadata_only`, `excerpt`, `complete`, `unavailable`), extraction scope, checked/read timestamps and disclosed UTF-16 character counts. Complete means the approved record projection or declared extraction scope, never the entire underlying entity. Selected-plan metadata never claims a body read. Citation binding and later search do not upgrade or erase stronger read evidence. Historical citations without coverage remain unknown, not backfilled. Search uses contact names/roles only; contact routes, notes and provenance require a selected authorized read. Tool responses explicitly project allowed fields, excluding reader internals. Duplicate read IDs share one read/budget allocation; upstream 20,000-character clipping is reported. `research-v5-evidence` invalidates older quotes after the tool/prompt contract change.
- [x] **Recheck saved citations before reopening:** saved successful results validate run/target provenance and re-read each cited workspace source through the current actor-authorized exact-ID reader. A missing, changed, unsupported or unavailable source withholds the whole result, including derived prose, rather than merely removing its citation. Application shares the same check; duplicate-request recovery cannot bypass it. Source reads run in groups of at most four. Fixed errors exclude private reader details; historical evidence is not upgraded. This proves cited-source checks only, not complete dependency tracking.
- [x] **Persist and recheck research dependencies:** successful new results retain a server-authored v1 manifest separate from visible citations. It fingerprints the exact initial organization-owned prompt projection, all observed workspace tool records (including uncited search/read inputs), selected-plan members and extracted file details. Reopening, duplicate-request recovery and new application reauthorize those dependencies through current readers; changes to the bounded content projection are detected even when revisions are null or unchanged. File reads repeat authorized extraction and compare the disclosed detail projection. Hashes/IDs are stored, not an extra copy of private input text. At most 256 dependencies are admitted, with source checks in groups of four; overflow cannot become a successful billed proposal. The model cannot author this manifest. Canonical plan validation excludes server metadata from model-output validation.
- [x] **Historical-result policy:** results without a complete dependency manifest remain stored but are withheld on reopen/new application with a concise request to research again. Do not fabricate missing provenance or silently upgrade old runs. Existing already-applied proposal history keeps its idempotent no-op behavior; this returns no saved research prose and performs no new write. Changed initial context conservatively invalidates a result, rather than labeling it current.
- [x] **Invalidate open research on return/reconnect:** returning focus, visible-tab activation and reconnection recheck the saved run through the existing server-authorized dependency reader. Hide derived content and block proposing while checks run or fail; coalesce overlapping events. Retry restores only a valid result. Known objective/plan staleness hides an already-open review immediately. Discard/target changes invalidate late responses; failed application requires a fresh status check. This is event-driven validation, not continuous polling or instantaneous cross-client revocation.
- [x] **Show saved source coverage:** cited sources use compact pills with explicit coverage and keyboard/touch-accessible details. Distinguish source details only, partial text, all available text within the declared scope, unavailable content and historical unknown coverage. Expanded details identify record/text/document-body/selected-plan limits and the original coverage timestamp; they do not imply a fresh read or verified facts. Keep an explicit source link and a no-citations state. Coverage comes from the existing server-authored envelope, never model prose. Current-view invalidation unmounts the review and its source details.
- [x] **Typed saved-result recovery:** a shared allowlist defines source-free failure codes and recovery actions. Failed readers withhold derived content and offer a fresh authorization/status check on the same run; they do not assert permanent revocation. Confirmed missing/changed dependencies, historical manifests, invalid saved output and scope mismatches require discarding the unusable result. Unknown context/ledger exceptions are redacted. UI uses the fixed code contract, retains the run and prompt, hides unusable actions and never regenerates automatically. Duplicate-request read failures retain the existing reservation/run ID, return a typed 409 and do not replay generation or settlement. This covers saved-result reads and shared application dependency validation; other admission/tool/provider failure contracts remain open.
- [x] **Admission and uncertain-reservation recovery:** quote/start actions return fixed source-free errors rather than raw context, configuration, schema or ledger exception text. Invalid requests fail before context access; oversized selected-plan context and changed pricing fail before reservation. Changed pricing requires closing/reopening to review a fresh quote; the stale quote cannot be resubmitted. A failed reservation transport is conservatively uncertain: keep the attempted run ID for status recovery, do not claim/generate/settle/reconcile an unconfirmed reservation, and never enable a second run automatically. Confirmed-run interruptions retain existing reconciliation even if reconciliation itself fails. Confirmed ledger rejection categories are implemented in the following checkpoint; actual lost-response qualification remains open.
- [ ] **Complete saved-result revocation:** qualify source-specific permission/revision behavior, authenticated revocation and external changes while the current tab remains active. Current checks use existing actor-authorized readers; they do not create fine-grained source permissions or instantaneous cross-client invalidation. Already disclosed, applied or downloaded content cannot be recalled by blocking a saved run.
- [x] **Confirmed admission denials:** eight exact, server-returned SQL rejection messages plus `P0001` map to credit shortage, usage limit, busy capacity or disabled AI. Source inspection verifies those branches follow the existing-run check under the admission lock; failed allocation rolls back in the existing transaction. Research shows fixed guidance, preserves the typed request and clears only the rejected attempt's recovery identity. No provider work, automatic retry, claim, settlement or reconciliation occurs for these confirmed denials. Access/revision/identity conflicts, unknown SQL errors, rejected transports and malformed successes remain uncertain; they never clear recovery identity merely because an error looks familiar. Existing non-research consumers retain the same Error messages. No SQL, ledger, permission or pricing changes.

- [x] **Deadline and cancellation stage fences:** the existing 90-second signal is checked before/after cancellation and source-validation reads, during/after provider streaming, and after tools return or fail. Late readers and caught tool aborts cannot start another stage or return a successful proposal. Observed cancellation between stages stops further dispatch and retains measured provider usage. Missing terminal usage/expired work throws into existing correlated recovery; it never fabricates zero spending or automatically regenerates. Eight-tool and final-round bounds remain enforced. This is local stage-boundary proof, not proof that non-abortable I/O stops immediately or live provider cancellation/accounting is qualified.

- [x] **Honest non-selected context coverage:** bounded objective overviews now disclose shown/total/omitted counts for plans, each shown plan's member IDs and pending proposal titles. Existing 4-plan/8-member/4-proposal limits remain; reviewed proposals do not inflate pending counts. Plan IDs/revisions disambiguate titles. These are summaries, never full reads; the model must inspect authorized records or request the relevant plan when omissions matter. Selected-plan context remains complete or rejected. Coverage participates in the existing byte budget and context fingerprint. `research-v6-context-coverage` invalidates older quotes. Existing saved runs whose old context fingerprints differ follow existing stale-result recovery; they are not silently rewritten or regenerated. This qualifies payload construction, not live model adherence or a claim that all organization work was read.

- [x] **Direct authorized reference reads:** `read_workspace` resolves known organization, person, core-document, uploaded-file, Particle, calendar and opportunity references through existing actor-scoped readers. Search is needed only when an ID is unknown. References cannot select arbitrary URLs, paths or new readers. Exact ID/kind matching, document entitlement, four-ID and detail-byte/character limits remain; duplicate IDs share one read. Successful reads retain evidence and dependency fingerprints for subsequent dispatch, reopening and application checks. Missing, denied, mismatched and failed reads return the same source-free error. Previously observed records still reject changes instead of silently rebasing. `research-v7-direct-read` invalidates older quotes; existing saved-result schemas/fingerprints are unchanged by this slice. Local fixtures do not establish live source permissions or processing approval.

Local revalidation does not establish global instantaneous revocation or live provider/database qualification.

- **Gate / dependencies:** gate 3 context prerequisite. Managed OpenAI is approved direction; processing configuration and authorized capped evaluation must be qualified before live calls. Customer pack pricing does not block deterministic context work or an explicitly funded evaluation.

Using them for a specific task requires reviewer authority and the qualified processing policy. Register bounded tool inputs, effect class, timeout/budget and safe result schema; documents cannot grant permission or become tool instructions.
- **Usable outcome:** an authorized user asks a scoped research question and receives a concise cited result with honest unavailable/truncated coverage. Tool payloads obey the approved disclosure boundary.
- **Owners:** `F/workspace-ai/server/knowledge-tools.ts`, `knowledge-records.ts`, `knowledge-file.ts`, `knowledge-docx.ts`, `research-context.ts`, `research-provider.ts`; existing run/credit owners.
- **Work:** expose bounded evidence/revision/coverage metadata through existing readers; minimize fields and deduplicate unchanged reads. Enforce tool argument/effect rules server-side. Replace prompt-only private-query protection with K7's mediated query or isolated approved-public-brief route before claiming containment. Keep external search disabled for scopes that cannot meet that boundary. Preserve current format limits and show unsupported extraction truthfully.
- **Completion:** New UI follows applicable U contracts. K1–K9, H7, E2–E4: relevant-source retrieval, denied/stale/revoked sources, sparse/hostile documents, bounded excerpts and calls, cancellation and budget stops. Inspect actual outbound payloads; qualify approved live processing separately from mocks. No unrelated private data in external queries.
- **Limit:** no vector database, generic memory store, new extraction formats, crawler or provider marketplace. Existing canonical organization knowledge supplies context; persistent memory is not a prerequisite.

#### S05. Objective entry to editable AI plan and focused revision

**Implementation status: In progress — local blank-board admission, run-only preview and atomic objective/proposal saving now join research progress, bounded repair and durable history recovery. Primary-composer objective handoff, bounded copy/item/prerequisite/branch editing, incremental inert previews, revisioned snapshot recovery and private draft autosave are implemented locally. Broader navigation and full transport/accounting/live qualification remain open. S04 live/disclosure gates remain open.**

- [x] **AG-UI progress bridge — initial local implementation:** pins core/client/encoder 1.0.3. After existing origin/auth/context/quote/reservation/claim checks, an opted-in request receives server-issued stream identity and ordered application events. Actual model/tool/validation/settlement stages supply fixed labels. The initial bridge carried no private content; the October31 validated-unsaved-preview checkpoint below adds one explicitly authorized presentation payload. Raw tool arguments/results, internal dependency fingerprints, private errors and reasoning remain excluded. Terminal status follows settlement and authorized readback. Result readiness now carries the durable run ID, immutable saved revision one and applicability (October31 revision checkpoint); it does not apply or accept anything. Confirmed duplicate/admission responses retain JSON behavior and existing ledger authority.
- [x] **Scoped receiver and saved-run recovery — local implementation:** strict version/run/request/actor/organization/canvas/target/revision checks; duplicates ignored, gaps/unknown or malformed events end the subscription. The SDK handles split UTF-8/SSE frames; a 64 KiB total wire cap and 128-frame cap bound parsing, and incomplete framing requires recovery. No general state/JSON patches or untruncate repair. End/gap/interruption triggers one existing authorized saved-run read; there is no stream replay, reorder buffer, regeneration or canonical write. Server source validation controls result exposure. Unmount detaches the client stream; explicit Stop retains existing cancellation semantics. Progress cannot replace drafts, change focus, move the camera or accept a plan.
- [x] **Fresh-session discovery — local implementation:** Previous research lists the latest twelve runs for the authenticated actor and exact organization/canvas/objective/plan. The existing plan ledger remains canonical; SQL returns only ID/date/status, excludes legacy records without explicit actor/canvas identity, checks current membership and restricts the function to the trusted server role. The action derives actor/canvas from authenticated workspace context. Selecting a run uses the existing source-revalidated reader and retains its run ID; no automatic regeneration, proposal acceptance, ledger reservation or content cache. Request drafts survive history inspection. Loading, empty, retry and keyboard interactions use existing menu primitives. New migration `20261030180000_workspace_research_history.sql` requires normal deployment before this discovery works against a hosted database; authenticated cross-device/full-app acceptance remains open.
- [x] **Bounded typed-result repair — local implementation:** one repair may use a remaining reserved round after format, relationship/reference or merged-plan validation failure. It uses fixed source-free feedback, disables tools and preserves the original request/target/provider. The unchanged three-round/90-second/input/output limits apply; a conservative next-round tariff estimate must fit the existing cost/credit reservation. Source freshness and cancellation are checked before disclosure and final validation. A second invalid response fails without another attempt. Refusal, incomplete/failed response, unconfirmed usage, denied context and invalid server dependency metadata never become repair prompts. Measured usage/correlation remains in the existing accounting/recovery path; only a fully validated saved result may be billable. Opt-in progress uses “Refining the plan…”; no draft enters canonical state. Contract version `research-v8-bounded-repair` invalidates old quotes without changing stored-result schemas.

Local Vite fixtures and a measured lazy chunk are not these receipts. Do not enable `WORKSPACE_AI_PROGRESS_STREAM` in production yet. Server lifecycle after network/process interruption still relies on the existing ledger reconciliation; no durable background-worker claim.
- [x] **New-objective entry — local implementation:** Plan with AI on the workspace composer opens a planning request on empty or populated default/named canvases. Strict new-objective targets carry the current graph revision and no objective/plan identity. Server context and SQL independently enforce current organization, actor and canvas scope; missing/deleted/stale canvases fail before provider spending. Generation and preview create no graph records. Add proposal builds server identities and atomically stores the objective, supported members and pending plan through the existing exact-revision writer, with fresh source validation and existing owner/member rules. Durable research origin prevents duplicate application after graph receipts expire; recovery can return a confirmed application receipt without exposing the old generated content. Canvas-scoped history works without a session-storage pointer. Existing saved-target requests and run enums are preserved. - [x] **Primary-composer objective handoff — local implementation:** with planning enabled and edit access, plain objective text opens the existing canvas-scoped planning request with its original text; it does not spend or create graph records. The existing quote and Generate plan action precede generation. Explicit document assistance, create commands and slash search retain their meanings; unavailable planning keeps ordinary library search. Closing restores the latest edited request and keyboard focus; only acknowledged proposal saving clears it. An open preview cannot be replaced by another submission; old save completions cannot clear a newer request. Oversized requests receive a local message without silent truncation. No model-based intent classifier, additional endpoint, provider activation or new persistence authority.
- [x] **Apps SDK editor prerequisite — local implementation:** B1's first existing-editor qualification now supplies the shared control foundation for upcoming editable previews. Actual objective/plan title editing and plan menus use the pinned SDK and existing save owners. This completes the prerequisite slice, not editable run-only previews or the entire UI migration.
- [x] **Bounded editable preview copy — local implementation (October31):** Edit preview exposes plan title, summary and newly generated item title/details using the shared SDK Input and existing multiline primitive. The application server loads the actor-owned immutable ledger result; rejects unknown/duplicate/missing keys, blank titles, oversized text, topology/source/target/kind injection; merges only allowed copy; and reuses source revalidation, semantic graph checks, exact-revision atomic saving, pending owner review and durable run deduplication. Human wording is not a newly verified factual claim. The original model result stays unchanged for provenance.
- [x] **Preview-copy interruption behavior — local implementation (October31):** drafts stay in the current session, bound to the run and exact saved snapshot. Focus/online checks hide content until authorization succeeds, preserve matching drafts and reject a changed snapshot. Failed application keeps the draft; further editing locks after a save attempt so retry uses the same submitted copy. Close/Discard ask before losing changed copy; reload has a native unsaved warning. Explicitly discarding edits keeps saved AI research available. The new-plan opener focuses an open preview rather than bypassing its Close guard. Once added, edited content persists through the normal canonical writer and reload. - [x] **Editable preview prerequisites — local implementation (October31):** each newly generated item exposes an inline Prerequisites disclosure with labeled checkbox rows. Users can add/remove prerequisite links to items already in the preview, including authorized existing references. Review reflects the edited connections. Strict version-two drafts carry only the existing keys, bounded copy and at most eight prerequisites per editable item; version-one copy payloads remain compatible. Shared deterministic topology validation rejects self/unknown/duplicate links, cycles across dependencies and decision branches, unconditional/conditional overlap and more than sixty total connections. Invalid drafts remain editable with an inline error and cannot be saved. The server independently revalidates before the existing exact-revision pending-proposal write; reference content/prerequisites, decision outcomes, sources, identities and removals remain immutable in this editor. - [x] **Add/remove preview items — local implementation (October31):** Add item creates an inline user-authored activity with title, details and prerequisites; it focuses the new title. Remove drops a generated item and its incoming/outgoing editable prerequisite links from the preview. Immediate Undo restores the item, copy and links; another edit clears that undo snapshot. Empty previews stay editable but cannot save without an item (existing explicit plan-member removals remain supported). Strict version-three drafts preserve the exact original editable-key set, list explicit removals and carry separate bounded additions; versions one/two remain compatible. The server assigns canonical IDs and fixes manual additions to the existing note/activity capability without reference IDs or evidence claims. It rejects collisions, forged metadata, dangling links, cycles, over-capacity and removal of fixed references, decisions, branch targets or prerequisites of fixed references. Such protected items show a concise explanation. Existing source authorization still checks the original saved result even if all generated items are replaced. Add proposal uses the existing exact-revision writer and pending owner review, with durable run deduplication. No additional model call, canonical deletion, new output type or persistent private draft cache. Decision-branch editing is covered by the later branch checkpoint.
- [x] **Edit generated decision branches — local implementation (October31):** each generated decision exposes an inline Branches disclosure. Users can rename its outcomes and select/deselect next items from the current preview, including manually added activities and authorized references. An empty destination list explicitly ends that path. Review and saved pending proposals reflect those edits without another model call or selecting an outcome. Strict version-four drafts preserve the exact generated decision/outcome key sets and existing order; versions one–three remain compatible. The server rejects duplicate/blank labels, forged identities/selection fields, unknown/self/duplicate targets, cycles and conditional/unconditional overlap. Existing workspace decision definitions and their proposed branches remain fixed in this editor. Generated branch targets become removable only after explicit disconnection; decisions themselves remain protected. Source authorization, exact revisions, immutable original research, pending owner review and replay protection remain unchanged. This slice edits existing generated outcomes; it does not add/remove decisions or outcomes. In-memory branch edits survive authorized rechecks; disclosures collapse when the editor remounts.
- [x] **Durable snapshot readiness and supersession — local implementation (October31):** a shared strict schema validates saved-run identity, known lifecycle states, credit shape, complete result and the separate already-applied receipt on both server readback and client consumption. Recovery remains an explicit authorized read of the same run, without regeneration or canonical writes. Existing exact draft-base checks and source/permission/revision checks remain authoritative. Unchanged previews, new-tab/modifier clicks, downloads, in-page fragments and mail/telephone links keep their normal behavior. Reload/close retains the native unsaved warning. No database rewrite, automatic retry, permission grant or direct application; source authorization and canonical application checks remain mandatory. This establishes the existing single-result lifecycle, not stream watermark persistence, replay or incremental result revisions.

- [x] **Private draft checkpoints — local implementation (October31):** Save draft explicitly checkpoints valid preview edits on the server without applying them to the board or charging credits. Opening the saved run restores its actor-private checkpoint only after current organization/run/source authorization and semantic validation. No private draft text is stored in browser storage. A bounded row under the original run stores the strict v4 draft, server-derived immutable-base hash, revision and retry identity. Server-only SQL checks membership/run ownership; a run lock serializes first saves, compare-and-swap conflicts and duplicate requests. Conflicts retain local edits and require reopening; uncertain saves retry the identical operation. Discard keeps a revision tombstone, preventing delayed saves from resurrecting discarded text. Closing/navigation discards only newer unsaved edits, preserving the last saved checkpoint. Before an edited proposal is submitted, its exact payload is checkpointed and frozen for retry; a late acknowledgement after a scope switch cannot trigger application. The existing canonical writer and separate owner review remain authoritative. Explicit recovery now also supports unfinished text through the incomplete-draft extension below; it is not keystroke autosave or recovery of uncheckpointed edits. Hosted migration, authenticated Next/multi-client acceptance and closure/content-purge qualification remain open; run retention must not implicitly retain private draft content beyond organization deletion policy.
- [x] **Automatic saved-run recovery — local implementation (October31):** reopening research or returning after reload automatically performs one authorized read of the retained run ID, then loads any actor-private draft checkpoint through the existing source-checked reader. Loading withholds proposal content and application. Invalid browser pointers are removed without a request; unavailable, denied and still-running results retain the existing explicit recovery controls. No polling, generation replay, extra provider spending, automatic application or private browser content cache. Existing run/session/read-version fences reject late responses after a scope change or replacement run. Quote completion cannot erase a recovery error. This restores durable results/checkpoints, not a disconnected provider execution, uncheckpointed keystrokes or event-stream replay.
- [x] **Save before departure — local implementation (October31):** the existing unsaved-preview confirmation offers Save and close or Save and leave for recoverable editable drafts. Departure waits for the same authorized revisioned private-checkpoint acknowledgement; saving never applies a proposal. Conflicted, structurally invalid, unverified or uncertain drafts cannot use this path. Unfinished text is supported by the incomplete-draft extension below. Failed or ambiguous saves keep the editor open; the existing exact-operation retry can confirm the checkpoint, followed by explicit departure without another write. Keep editing cancels departure even if an in-flight save succeeds; late acknowledgements cannot close another plan or navigate to an obsolete destination. Existing discard, keyboard focus and ordinary-link/browser-unload protection remain intact. This adds a usable departure path, not automatic incomplete-draft persistence, router/history interception or hosted qualification.
- [x] **Incomplete private draft recovery — local implementation (October31):** Save draft and Save and close/leave preserve empty plan/item titles, unfinished added items and blank decision outcome labels exactly, including whitespace, through the existing actor-private checkpoint writer. Reopening restores those edits directly into the editor. Recovery validates bounds, immutable item/outcome identities, protected references and complete relationship safety; it never returns an applicable plan. Add proposal and attempted/frozen checkpoints still require the strict application schema and full semantic validation. Correcting the missing copy enables the existing pending-proposal flow and separate owner review. No placeholders are persisted, no private text is cached in browser storage, and no schema/SQL/provider changes are introduced. This is explicit saving, not keystroke autosave, crash recovery of uncheckpointed edits, or permission to persist unsafe connections. Earlier valid-only checkpoint restrictions are superseded for unfinished text only.
- [x] **Bounded stalled-stream recovery — local implementation (October31):** research presentation stops after30 seconds without an accepted new event or120 seconds total. Duplicate events and partial bytes cannot keep it alive. Timers, the SDK subscription and response body are released exactly once; an already-aborted response is not subscribed. Timeout, interrupted closure or producer restart clears the unsaved preview before the existing authorized same-run read. Saved results become editable; still-running and denied results keep explicit recovery. No new generation, automatic polling, cancellation of provider execution, proposal application or accounting change. Restart is qualified through snapshot fallback, not durable replay or persisted resume watermarks.
- [x] **Incremental plan item previews — local implementation (October31):** the opt-in provider path now consumes actual output-text deltas and emits complete typed items before a terminal response exists. A bounded framing reader extracts only closed root-level item objects; unknown fields/types, incomplete values, duplicate/overflow keys and oversized buffers never become executable content. Each emitted batch repeats current context/all-observed-source and cancellation checks; unavailable citations withhold presentation. Transport carries only inert key/type/title/description, with strict identity/sequence validation and explicit clearing. The review panel grows a read-only list marked “Unconfirmed items. Connections pending.” It clears on attempt completion, repair, source loss, failure, interruption, focus invalidation and saved readback. Full previews, editing and Add still require their existing complete validation and saved-result boundaries. Optional presentation exceptions preserve measured usage and normal settlement. No schema/SQL, tariff/limit, provider/model, prompt, tool or canonical-writer changes; no fabricated animation of an already-finished result. This is controlled local proof; live provider latency/capacity and authenticated Next acceptance remain open.
- [x] **Interrupted-run reconnect recovery — local implementation (October31):** network return and visible-tab activation detach old presentation and coalesce one authorized same-run read, including an active stream or pending JSON response. Exact duplicate envelopes are ignored; changed content under an accepted sequence, including an old-identity producer restart, requires snapshot recovery. Resolve exact current payment entry points before edits; no replacement ledger or speculative new billing feature owner.

Introduce the H1 new-objective admission path without a hidden placeholder objective or client-chosen authority. Bind typed results to currently enabled registry commands and source revisions. Keep preview, pending storage, owner approval and canonical apply distinct. Reuse existing run storage/recovery; add only missing fresh-session recovery and safe application boundaries. Scope every event/result; show changes without moving the user's camera unexpectedly.

O1/O2, H1–H10, E1–E4/E7: blank-board and existing-objective cases; result recovery on reload; owner/member proposal boundaries; stale context; cancellation/unknown outcome; duplicate apply; unchanged unrelated content/geometry. Prove AG-UI fragments/gaps/duplicate events, scope changes and reload cannot trigger writes or overwrite drafts. Accounting completion additionally proves concurrent reservations, owner-only purchase, paid-object/event dedupe, independent balance readback, unknown settlement and refund/dispute adjustments. A browser checkout redirect is never proof of granted credits.

#### S06. Selected content to branded mobile publication

**Implementation status: In progress — root/named-canvas source parity and a bounded authenticated guest collaboration path are implemented and locally verified. Guest edits cover canonical page title/introduction and explicitly opted-in selected notes; unrelated source fields remain unavailable. Broader brand/UI qualification and hosted acceptance remain open. S05's remaining live/accounting gates remain open; manual publication has no AI dependency.**

- [x] **Canvas-bound sharing — local implementation (October31):** the actual board Share entry carries its selected canvas into editor loading, review, save, publication and exact retry. Recent pages and available Particles are limited to that canvas. Each page retains an immutable nullable source canvas; null preserves existing main-canvas pages and omitted legacy requests. The server resolves exactly one live canvas through the existing workspace catalog, never silently falling back to main. Review tokens bind canvas identity. The service-only writer locks the board, checks the exact selected graph and rejects stale/missing/ambiguous canvases and cross-canvas page commands, including replay. Public snapshots contain only the existing allowlisted projection; canvas identity/catalog/private source data are not added to public output. Draft changes remain private until explicit republish. Revocation and confirmed-operation retry do not require surviving source content. Same item IDs on different canvases cannot redirect a page. New migration `20261031210000_workspace_page_canvas.sql` preserves existing page/link/order/snapshot identity and replaces the old RPC overload; deploy it before the updated application. No publication feature flag, new guest permission or public asset capability is enabled.
- [x] **Authenticated page guests — bounded local implementation (October31):** Preview → Share privately saves a separately reviewed guest snapshot without publishing a public link or enabling public sharing. People creates recipient-email invitations with View/Edit/Manage; users copy the link, and no email is sent. Acceptance requires the exact verified account; invitation IDs are routing identifiers, never edit bearer credentials. Invitations expire after seven days if unaccepted; accepted grants last until revoked. The guest route `/public/pages/[pageId]` uses the already-reserved `public` namespace and skips organization membership/onboarding resolution. Every read/write/replay checks the current scoped grant in a service-only database command. View sees only the reviewed projection. Edit changes only the canonical page title/introduction with revision checks; these edits remain private until an organization editor explicitly refreshes the guest snapshot or publishes. Manage adds invitations for viewers/editors and removal of its own delegated grants; only actual organization editors can grant Manage or publish. Removing a manager also removes its delegated grants. No organization membership, source rebinding, private responses, private provider data, Particle mutation, AI disclosure or public authority is implied. RLS denies direct invitation/receipt access. Durable receipts record actor, operation hash, action, resulting revision, changed field names and timestamp; unknown writes retain exact explicit retry. Migration `20261031220000_workspace_page_guests.sql` follows the canvas migration and must precede deployment. Neither migration was applied remotely.
- [x] **Guest recovery guards — local implementation (November1):** pending or unconfirmed access requests keep the People panel mounted and lock Share back/page-switch/editor controls; they report outstanding work to the existing workspace window and warn before browser unload. Exact explicit retry remains available. Guest title/introduction drafts retain their original revision when invitation responses contain another editor’s newer page. Only explicit reload or acknowledgment of the guest’s own edit resets that draft; a stale save conflicts without overwriting either editor’s text. No new persistence, permission or publication authority. Type checking, scoped lint and seven controlled browser flows pass, including held/lost invitation acknowledgment, exact retry/no duplicate, competing page revision, discard confirmation, reload and existing publication. Browser fixtures are not authenticated Next or hosted acceptance; broader navigation/release gates remain open.
- [x] **Saved-page discovery/recovery — local implementation (November1):** the workspace sidebar links to `/workspace/shared-pages`, an organization-editor directory independent of surviving canvases or Particles. Server-derived organization scope, existing RLS and strict inputs govern a thin 25-row list with immutable-ID pagination; details use the existing scoped page reader and never fetch source content. Existing public snapshots can be opened, public links revoked with confirmation, and reviewed guest access managed through the existing controls. Pending/unconfirmed writes lock local recovery navigation, warn before browser unload and retain the exact explicit retry. #### S08. Versioned public form intake and private receipt recovery

**Implementation status: In progress — native versioned publication, public/invited submission, exact retry, private response history and cell correction are implemented locally for the intake manager. Existing form collaborators can copy a version-specific response link and enter an isolated signed-in response page without workspace navigation. Primary managers can now create, copy and revoke read-only form-response invitations for external/non-editor accounts; verified recipients explicitly accept on the isolated version link. Authorized collaborators can browse published versions within that canonical form and follow version-specific response links. Primary managers can also explicitly invite external collaborators to correct response cells, with fresh acceptance and existing conflict/exact-retry handling. Authorized collaborators can also discover other permitted published forms in the same organization through the isolated response entry. Accepted external Edit invitations now enable isolated canonical form-definition editing with conflict/retry recovery, without workspace or publishing authority. Accepted external Manage invitations now support one-level View/Correct/Edit delegation with scoped rosters, primary adoption and permanent dependent revocation. Other source-edit surfaces, lifecycle qualification and hosted acceptance remain unfinished. All P4 question types and Other have local manual qualification; AI composition remains separate. Native Sheets intake/correction is deferred for MVP by the November2 scope decision and remains unavailable.**

Confirm canonical write/readback before success; proven failures preserve active-form answers for deliberate retry, unknown outcomes reconcile the original identity first. Provide an already-open private reviewer surface that updates on confirmed submissions through authorized refresh/subscription. Do not wait for S09 to make new submissions inspectable and directly correctable by form collaborators; S09 adds saved query/composition controls.
- **Usable outcome:** build a supported form, publish its immutable version, submit real answers, receive a confirmed receipt, see responses arrive and correct permitted answer cells as a form collaborator with canonical readback.

- **Work:** begin this packet with the actual Google adapter qualification, before building UI that promises its writes or corrections. Prove immediate confirmed writes, stable submission identity, duplicate prevention, lost-response reconciliation, concurrent staff and direct-Sheet edits, row movement/schema drift, protected ranges and execution-identity permissions together, without durable answer copies. A read-before-write or app-only lock is not proof against edits made outside Coach House. Record the provider guarantees and the exact supported conflict strategy; if required correctness cannot be demonstrated, keep that capability disabled and report the precise gap for a scoped decision. Coach House storage and Google Forms link-out remain distinct working options, not evidence that native Sheets intake passed. Implement the chosen Coach House/Google Sheets destination binding under D4. Coach House submissions use transactional persistence; the Google adapter needs authenticated direct write/readback, stable identity, minimal answer-free receipts and uncertain-outcome reconciliation; no queued acceptance or retained answer copy. Define scoped version/submission identity and replay behavior separately for each destination. Connect real public controls, field errors, bounded abuse protection and uncertain-submit retry. Implement a minimal private response list/table with direct permitted answer-cell editing through the canonical source writer; the complete saved-query/table configuration is S09. Extend the existing builder with all P4 requested controls and typed validation; Google Forms link-out is a separate optional alternative, not completion of native intake. Publication revocation and form-version changes cannot silently redirect an in-flight submission.
- **Completion:** New UI follows applicable U contracts. O4/O5, E1/E2/E7: independent database readback; one response on duplicate submit; same ID/different payload conflicts; forbidden fields/oversized answers denied; original version preserved; approved public/invited modes and cross-tenant rules tested; form creator/editor/manager response access works without extra setup; unrelated users are denied; answer-cell corrections persist and survive original-submit retries; revoked binding fails; current-form input remains on failure without promises of browser-close recovery. Independently verify Google destination rows as well as database records, double-click/concurrent/lost-response recovery, and visible updates without full-page reload. Verify neither server logs nor durable tasks retain Google-bound answers.
- **Limit:** extend the existing builder with P4 controls; no replacement form designer, respondent uploads, payments or conditional workflow engine. Preview remains rehearsal and never reports real submission success. Implement retention/correction rules chosen in D4, not invented defaults.
