**Actual owners inspected:** `src/features/organization-forms/lib/{form-schema,form-version,form-answers}.ts` and controlled builder/preview own drafts and pure validation. `server/actions.ts` intentionally has no public intake endpoint. `src/features/google-drive/server/{config,google-api,service,file-content}.ts` owns the existing encrypted connection, selected-file metadata and supported document reads; there is no Sheets write/correction adapter. Its `drive.file` scope is one accepted Sheets API scope, but OAuth scope alone proves neither chosen-file access nor protected-range/edit authority. No credentials, scopes, integrations or provider records were changed.

**Provider evidence and qualification verdict (documentation/source inspection, not a live test):**

| Concern | Documented primitive / exact gap | Gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Append/retry | `values.append` appends after the detected table and can include returned values. Its documented request has no submission-id uniqueness or expected-revision parameter. A lost reply cannot justify appending again | Plain append plus read-before-write is unqualified for duplicate prevention. [Append contract](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/reference/rest/v4/spreadsheets.values/append) |
| Atomicity versus concurrent corrections | `batchUpdate` applies its own valid requests atomically, but Google explicitly allows concurrent collaborators to alter the result. `UpdateCellsRequest` declares rows/fields/location, not an expected answer value/revision | An application revision check followed by an unconditional write cannot promise detection of direct-Sheet edits. No supported conflict strategy is qualified. [Batch contract](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/reference/rest/v4/spreadsheets/batchUpdate), [cell writer](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/reference/rest/v4/spreadsheets/request#UpdateCellsRequest) |
| Stable row identity | Developer metadata can attach identity to a row and locate it through a data filter. Metadata keys are not unique; metadata IDs are. Metadata is not secret storage or an access boundary | A provider-side unique marker in an atomic batch is a candidate for delivery deduplication, not proof of the entire intake/correction contract. Qualify marker collision/allocation, exact row binding, deletion, copied rows, delayed commits and retry expiry before enabling it. Metadata lookup does not itself compare answer revisions. [Metadata](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/guides/metadata), [filtered writes](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/reference/rest/v4/spreadsheets.values/batchUpdateByDataFilter) |
| Acting identity and protected cells | Drive exposes current-user capabilities; Sheets exposes protected-range restrictions separately | Existing file metadata reads do not establish interactive correction authority. Recheck the actual editor's access, formula/identity protection and destination restrictions; never substitute an owner's connection. Narrow manager-authorized public delivery is a separate delegation. [Drive capabilities](https://developers.google.com/workspace/drive/api/guides/manage-sharing), [protected ranges](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/reference/rest/v4/spreadsheets/sheets#ProtectedRange) |

**Counterexamples the real adapter must defeat:** (1) write commits but reply is lost; retry races the original delayed operation; (2) app reads answer A, a person writes B in Sheets, app writes C using its earlier check; (3) row moves between lookup and positional update; (4) source permission or protection changes after preflight; (5) an original submission is retried after an authorized correction. App-only locks, a second read, a successful HTTP response and simulated tests alone do not defeat these schedules. These are analytical cases, not reported provider observations.

**Disposition:** keep native Sheets intake/correction unavailable, preserve it as unfinished scope, and continue the already-approved native form path. Do not silently introduce durable Google answer copies, Apps Script, service-account proxies, protected app-only Sheets or last-write-wins corrections. Those change the architecture or collaboration contract and require a scoped decision if adopted. Google Forms link-out remains a separate optional capability. A future live qualification uses an explicitly selected disposable Sheet and two authorized identities, bounded synthetic answers and independent provider readback; do not probe customer data. Passing live runs cannot establish a guarantee absent from the documented primitive.

Initial combined run had one obsolete custom React Grab marker expectation; corrected to assert its absence per `docs/agent/react-grab-execution-contract.md`, preserving all real Particle identity assertions. One hydrated Chromium test passes at 320px/reduced motion (`/tmp/ch-s08-form-controls-browser-final.log`, job86000 exit0): change type, save/reopen, keyboard selection, focus first invalid control, leap date/local time, clearing, no host submit, discarded rehearsal answers and no horizontal overflow. Initial browser attempt timed out on label text whitespace; the corrected semantic locator passes without increasing timeout. ##### S08 native intake-manager path — November 1

- [x] **Atomic activation:** explicitly reviewing and publishing/sharing a form now binds its exact definition to an immutable native form version in the same page transaction. The preview explains that answers are stored privately in Coach House and that only the form manager can publish and review responses. The later creation-ownership checkpoint establishes management before publication. Old publications without a binding remain previews; a draft save never enables intake. SQL compares the version to the locked canonical form definition. Source deletion or page revocation does not delete response records.
- [x] **Real submission path:** the public/invited renderer reuses the nine existing answer controls. The server validates against the stored version, then the transaction independently rechecks the current page audience, version and respondent authority. Unknown fields, invalid answers, wrong versions and revoked access fail. Same-origin JSON requests have a 256KiB streamed-body limit and ten-second read deadline; valid JSON followed by an unfinished stream is not accepted. Initial serialized per-form admission caps are 60 new responses/minute and 1,000/day across versions; these are conservative engineering limits pending capacity qualification, not measured product allowances. Exact retries do not consume another response slot.
- [x] **Receipt/recovery:** the native response transaction persists answers outside board JSON and returns only ID/version/time to respondents. Same ID and normalized payload returns the original receipt; a different payload conflicts. Original-submit retries never restore answers overwritten by staff corrections. Unknown results freeze the exact active request for deliberate retry; inputs remain on failure, with no browser-close recovery promise. No respondent read/update endpoint, file upload or Google answer buffer was added.
- [x] **Private management:** Share and the source-independent shared-page management route expose Responses. Authorized version discovery includes older and private publications, with keyset pagination. The selected version's private list refreshes every five seconds while visible and not editing; older-page browsing remains stable. Direct answer editing uses a fixed response revision and operation identity, validates the full resulting response, and changes only the selected question. Stale edits stay visible; Reload saved answer updates the base and displays the newer value without overwriting the draft. Saves record actor/time/question/revision metadata. Unknown correction results retry the same operation. Technical fields stay read-only. Page viewers/editors and unrelated organization teammates receive no implicit response access.
- [ ] **Complete P4 collaborator ownership:** first-save creation attribution and canonical source protection are now implemented in the following checkpoint. Historical creators remain unknown; existing intake managers are preserved. Read-only reviewer grants and revocation for existing organization editors are implemented in the later checkpoint. Scoped form Edit and source-inspector affordances are implemented in the later editor checkpoint. Scoped Manage is implemented in the later manager checkpoint; external/non-editor access remains unfinished. The following recovery checkpoint covers published forms whose manager lost organization editing access; unpublished-draft recovery is implemented in the later source-recovery checkpoint. Current canonical managers can edit/remove the source, correct and publish. Explicit Edit collaborators can edit source and correct; View collaborators can read. Each still needs current organization editing authority. Publishing and removal stay with the canonical primary manager. Scoped Manage adds bounded View/Edit delegation, as qualified below. No second mandatory reviewer setup is approved; no broad organization/page grant may substitute. S08/P4 is not complete.
- [ ] **Finish remaining S08 gates:** native retention/closure/deletion lifecycle and receipt-retention qualification, complete field controls, configured destination selection, qualified native Sheets, hosted migration/authenticated Next acceptance, appearance review and release checks. Coach House is the only functioning destination in this slice; it is explicitly disclosed, with no silent Google fallback. Optional source export, saved-table composition and general synchronization are not claimed.

**Ownership and deployment:** `organization-forms` owns native version/answer/response contracts, the server-only intake service and the thin `/api/public/forms/intake` route. `workspace-sharing` owns the existing review, exact-source publication and audience checks. Migration `20261101140000_organization_form_intake.sql` adds service-only, RLS-protected form/version/response/correction stores and wraps the existing page transaction; the superseded helper is not service-callable. Apply it after the S07 asset migrations and before this app revision. No hosted migration or production activation occurred. Runtime reads/writes pass validated RPC contracts; credentials and raw answers are not logged. Anonymous response access is denied even with a known version ID.

##### S08 login deletion is not organization closure — November 1

- [x] **Concrete prerequisite found and fixed:** the existing personal-account deletion route could cascade through an owned organization or explicitly delete it after an Auth foreign-key error. That contradicted P4 and bypassed the approved 30-day closure window. The account preflight now identifies organization ownership as a blocker and lists the organization as retained. The existing dialog displays the blocker and disables deletion. ##### S08 transactional suppression capture and retained local journal — November 2

- [x] **Bounded capture implemented:** migration `20261103090000_organization_suppression_capture.sql` records closure requests, cancellations and explicit native response deletions atomically with canonical receipts. Required source coverage follows P2: existing Coach House records, new/imported workspace tables, linked Sheets and response views after S08. Reuse source adapters where valid; no single scenario or dataset silently replaces this scope. Import is an explicit copy with audience/provenance. Linked Google reads require execution-identity checks; protected/formula/identity fields stay read-only, and writes require qualified concurrency/reconciliation. Hidden columns never substitute for authorization.

O6's general saved-view owner remains a technical mapping task at packet entry; establish one owner under repository boundaries, not one duplicate store per view. Do not invent a duplicate record store to avoid that mapping.

Preserve focus/drafts on refetch/conflict. For responses, permit authorized direct answer-cell correction with actor/time/field metadata; preserve technical identity and published question/version linkage. Reuse the same source in multiple views without copying rows.
- **Completion:** New UI follows applicable U contracts. O6, E1/E2/E7: independently compare query/count results; prove stable pagination, saved settings, source edit propagation, field/access denial and concurrent conflict. Hidden columns are not security. If the source has no approved writable fields, register read-only truthfully; that alone does not satisfy an approved editable-data requirement.
- **Limit:** no arbitrary SQL, formulas, bulk editing, general bidirectional synchronization or public live-query endpoint. A declared Google Sheets source/destination adapter is in scope; that does not authorize a universal sync engine. P2's named source coverage is in scope; do not add other adapters unless required by an approved capability. Formula evaluation and universal spreadsheet editing remain deferred.

A prior named-range/metadata lookup cannot close that race. Metadata-filtered writes locate rows but lack the expected-value condition. This is a counterexample under documented semantics, not a live Google observation. ### Starting, overlapping and closing packets

1. **Start S01 only after the complete plan is approved and implementation authorized.** Its first checkpoint is the usable manual objective/plan/note interaction and shared contract—not an infrastructure-only registry PR declared finished.
2. **Apply settled decisions; qualify unknowns.** S03 applies section 4's approved D2 calculation/completion rules and fixtures; S06/S07 apply approved D5 and permission presets. S08 applies approved immediate submission, familiar form controls, form-collaborator response editing and 30-day closure; source/retention qualification precedes public intake. S05 owns final measured tariff quantities before customer billing; monthly/carryover/refund behavior is approved. Source adapter and processing qualification are engineering gates, not requests to reapprove the chosen presets/provider. See the boundary table below; no policy gap silently blocks unrelated planning.
3. **Overlap only stable interfaces.** Navigation can progress with a fixed membership contract; publication/intake can progress independently of AI; context work can progress independently of animation. Keep one owner for shared registry/schema/writer edits. Do not dispatch multiple agents into the same mutable contract. This describes possible execution, not a request to launch agents now.
4. **Close by behavior and evidence.** Update the one inventory/checkpoint with actual outcomes and unresolved checks, then mirror the document at a usable boundary. Do not call a packet done for file count, elapsed time, screenshots or schema-only success. If existing code already passes, close the requirement with evidence rather than rebuilding it.

### Remaining values and technical readiness boundaries

This table locates remaining qualification and commercial values inside existing packets; it is not another roadmap. Approved behavior stays settled. Runtime proof is produced during implementation; tariff quantities require measurement and final commercial approval before selling usage.

| Boundary | Already decided | Still required | Affected packet / earliest dependency |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Progress | Section 4: equal required-work units, deduplicated scope, explicit outcome completion; Time/Both/Ongoing and zero-work labels | Implement deterministic selectors, transition guards and approved fixtures; no further mode or denominator vote | S03 before status calculations; does not block S01 editing |
| Permissions and sources | View/Edit/Manage; no assignment grants; source constraints; private form-collaborator responses | Map each P2 source to actual fields/writer/execution identity, conflict strategy and saved-view owner; qualify with allow/deny/concurrency evidence | S01 shared contract, S06 grants, S09 adapter; technical mapping rather than another role-policy vote |
| Forms and corrections | Familiar field menu; no respondent uploads/edits; form collaborators see/correct responses; Google Forms link-out option | Extend and qualify the existing P4 field/editor/answer contracts and canonical correction writers; no remaining collection-policy vote | S08 before exposing real public intake; definition/editor work remains reusable |
| Retention | Until deleted; annual review; optional supported timed deletion; no durable Sheets answer buffer | Apply approved 30-day explicit closure/export window; qualify the lifecycle schedule against actual backups, receipts and caches; no default 12-month purge | S07 asset rules and S08 intake before public activation |
| AI usage and commerce | P3: organization-wide monthly allowance/no rollover; included-first; purchased carryover/no active-account expiry; owner-only provisional $10 top-up; failed/canceled runs free; unused purchased balance refunded on closure | Measure included quantity, pack credit quantity and conversion; approve final versioned tariff against costs/value. No BYOK/auto-recharge or unapproved paid evaluation | S05 before customer usage/purchases; S10 reuses the same accounting contract |
| Provider processing | Chosen route and narrow context/tool boundary | Qualify actual model/endpoint/account data controls, supported tools, logs and retention; authorize capped non-production evaluation | S04/S05/S10 before their live evaluation; no silent provider substitution |
| Quality thresholds | E1–E8 and zero tolerance for permission/corruption failures | Measure baseline; freeze numerical quality/cost/latency thresholds and evaluate unseen cases | Qualification work starts with relevant packets; S11 integrates it, never invents thresholds after seeing a candidate |

**Consolidation and decision reconciliation complete at specification level.** The approved usable-slice sequence remains S01 manual planning → S02/S03 navigation/progress → S04/S05 grounded AI/accounting → S06–S08 sharing/assets/forms → S09 editable saved data views → S10 richer compositions → S11 integrated qualification. Stable-interface overlap remains allowed. Section 15 records the consistency/coverage review; measured tariff values and runtime qualification are explicit. Final whole-specification implementation approval is still required; no packages, provider activation, paid evaluation or release follows from this documentation milestone.


## 10. Product behavior and proof

> **[VERIFY]** E1–E8 are required acceptance contracts, not passing results. Section 1 remains the implementation inventory; section 15 owns receipts and decisions. Reuse valid receipts when their code, environment and assertion scope are unchanged. No named organizational workflow defines MVP success.
>
> **[NOTE]** Test only enabled capabilities and their dependencies. Mentioning formulas, expiring links, external actions or retained memory does not add them to MVP. D2–D6 remain explicit boundaries. A capability with an unresolved required policy cannot be advertised as ready.

#### E1. Observable product behavior

The primary output is the useful board or working artifact. Chat supplies requests, necessary clarification and concise status. A generated description of a form, table or plan is not the corresponding functional output.

| Contract | User interaction | Observable acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| O1/O2: objective and plan | Describe an objective in the scoped composer; review the proposed plan; apply permitted changes | The scope and processing route are visible. A validated editable preview appears. Preview, saved proposal and accepted plan are distinguishable. Canonical readback and reload match the accepted change; no fabricated people, dates or evidence. Blank-board generation must work before that entry point is advertised. |
| O1/O2: focused revision | Select a plan/item and request a change | Review identifies additions, updates, removals and affected relationships. Unmentioned records, memberships, completed work, drafts and manual geometry remain unchanged. Owner acceptance changes only approved scope. A member's saved proposal does not become an accepted plan. |
| O1/O8: navigation | Select, expand, edit, traverse and return | Ordinary canvas click selects. Explicit controls expand/edit. A plan-list item focuses its existing placement. Escape closes the active surface and restores focus without losing drafts. Optional walkthrough uses the same navigation and does not write plan state. |
| O3: document | Edit a supported source through its existing editor | Save uses the source's canonical writer and revision. Another authorized reader sees the change. Read-only imports remain read-only; an attractive preview cannot imply write support. |
| O4/O5: form and intake | Edit a definition; publish an allowed form; submit; review privately | Preview submits nothing. Real intake binds the published form version, accepts approved fields, persists one submission and returns its receipt. Form creators/managers/editors see responses and can correct answer cells; unrelated viewers cannot. Definition changes do not reinterpret previous submissions, and submit retries do not undo corrections. |
| O6: data view | Ask for supported data; inspect, save and edit allowed cells | Rows match a canonical query with declared filters, ordering and pagination. Save preserves the view definition. A cell edit calls its registered source writer. Read-only technical fields stay read-only; authorized form collaborators can correct answer cells with conflict handling and change metadata. No response table is claimed complete before real intake exists. |
| O7: share | Select content; preview audience; publish; open mobile link; revoke | The page shows the organization and selected safe projections in explicit reading order. No unselected fields, private assets or edit authority leak. Named-canvas publication reads the intended canvas. After revocation, new authorized checks deny access; already downloaded copies cannot be recalled. |
| All: failure and recovery | Interrupt, lose connection, retry or encounter a conflict | Preserve recoverable drafts, report which operation is uncertain, reconcile its receipt before retrying and never show false success. A fresh reload reads canonical saved state. Unsupported operations remain unavailable with a short explanation and supported alternative where one exists. |

For each enabled output, account for creation, empty state, populated state, edit/save, reload, permission loss and recoverable failure through current applicable evidence. Prove common behavior once at its shared owner, then verify each variant’s actual wiring and unique behavior; do not duplicate the full shared suite for every variant. Distinct adapters, permission boundaries and persistence destinations still require their own evidence. For non-editable projections, prove that mutation controls and write authority are absent. Test relevant connections between outputs; isolated component success does not prove integration.

#### E2. Deterministic invariants

These are binary checks against application state, authorization and tool dispatch. All applicable checks must pass; a good-looking plan or high average model score cannot compensate for a failure.

| Boundary | Required fixture and independent assertion |
| --- | --- |
| Identity and scope | Two organizations, multiple users and named canvases; forged identifiers, revoked membership and stale access. Read/return/write/publication paths recheck authority. Denied results disclose no unauthorized existence or content. |
| Record versus placement | One record in two plans/placements. A canonical content edit updates the shared record; contextual membership and view state stay local. Removing membership does not delete a referenced record. |
| Revisions and concurrency | Two clients edit the same source; change source after preview and before apply. Stale writes conflict visibly; valid independent edits survive. Multi-owner results report each committed/failed effect without claiming global atomicity. |
| Proposal and application | Stage, reject and accept the same proposal. Pending storage may create proposed Particles as H2 specifies; it must not silently change accepted membership. Rejection cannot erase shared records. Duplicate acceptance returns its receipt or an explicit conflict without another effect. |
| Retry and interruption | Disconnect before and after canonical commit, cancel during a tool call, replay same request, reuse its ID with a different payload, exceed receipt retention. Verify effect and settlement counts from the canonical ledger. Unknown outcome requires reconciliation, not blind replay. |
| Typed boundaries | Invalid schema versions/types, temporary or nonexistent IDs, illegal transitions and unsupported adapters. Reject before executable rendering or mutation. TypeScript success alone does not prove runtime validation. |
| Bounds and abuse | Exactly 250/500 graph limits and cap+1, cycles where forbidden, missing endpoints, oversized context, malformed files and exhausted run/tool budgets. Fail without partial graph mutation or draft loss; conditional formula support also rejects arbitrary execution and unbounded expressions. |
| Disclosure and injection | Hostile instructions in documents, retrieved pages, tool results and output markup. Inspect actual provider/tool payloads and rendered DOM. External queries contain only authorized disclosure; unsafe HTML/URLs cannot execute. A prompt saying “keep private” is not enforcement. |
| Publication and intake | Tampered/revoked tokens, stale snapshots, cross-page assets, unpublished fields, extra submission fields and duplicate submits. Read actual public projections and private stored submissions. Expiry is checked only if supported; a public bearer token never grants edit access. |
| Collaboration and view state | Another user's mutation during expand/collapse, traversal and layout. Personal camera/selection/expansion changes do not overwrite shared state. Preserve manual/locked positions; temporary layout restoration cannot undo newer movements. |

Fixtures assert both the intended change and preservation of unrelated state. Expected state comes from fixture authors, canonical source reads and deterministic calculations—not the generating model's account of what it did.

#### E3. AI quality rubric

Evaluate schema validity separately from usefulness. Score each dimension **0: unusable**, **1: needs material correction**, **2: usable with ordinary editing**. “Ordinary editing” changes preference or detail; repairing wrong authority, invented facts, missing essential outputs or a structurally unusable plan is material correction.

| Dimension | A score of 2 requires |
| --- | --- |
| Outcome fit | Addresses the stated objective and constraints with a compact, coherent plan; avoids unrelated work and mandatory invented milestones. |
| Working outputs | Chooses supported capabilities and makes the needed artifact editable/usable; does not substitute prose for an artifact or claim unavailable functions. |
| Dependencies and decisions | Captures necessary order, parallel work and unresolved decisions without manufacturing dependencies or blocking questions. |
| Grounding and uncertainty | Uses authorized relevant sources, distinguishes inference/unknowns from facts, and links evidence to claims. Current permission and source revision remain checkable. |
| Revision fidelity | Performs the requested change, reuses existing work and preserves unrelated accepted work, identities and relationships. |
| Presentation | Shows the result on the board with concise labels and actionable status; no unsolicited essay, redundant summary, unexplained duplicates or needless layers. |
| Tool efficiency | Retrieves enough relevant evidence with bounded targeted calls, avoids repeated unchanged reads and stops when the requested result is ready or a real blocker remains. |

Forbidden tool use, unauthorized disclosure, invented completion of an action, invalid persisted state or silent overwrite is a hard failure regardless of rubric score. For sparse context, an honest useful partial plan can outperform an exhaustive fabricated plan. Do not force citations for proposed ideas; do require support for claims presented as known organizational facts.

**[ANSWERED] Qualification procedure:** establish a baseline on the current supported route, calibrate rubric examples with a product reviewer, then freeze per-capability quality floors and latency/cost ceilings before comparing candidates. Record both absolute usability and regression against baseline. Do not claim release readiness while those thresholds are unset or measured only on prompt-tuning examples. This is a bounded evaluation prerequisite, not a new product-discovery exercise or a demand for another provider.

#### E4. Evaluation set and grading

**Cadence for S04/S05/S10/S11:** routine development uses a small fixed deterministic smoke set with mocked provider/tool outputs for affected parsing, orchestration and rendering behavior. This proves mechanics, not live planning quality. Run broader affected quality/retrieval cases when model/configuration, prompts/skills, tools, retrieval or planning logic changes; perform representative integration qualification before release. Reuse valid qualification for unchanged capabilities. Repeated stochastic and held-out trials below apply to qualification, not every edit or packet. Live evaluation still requires authorized data, route and spending budget. No automatic paid evaluation follows from a code change.

Use a versioned suite organized by capabilities, not a single “golden journey.” Include new objective generation, revision, supported output construction, retrieval, collaboration and sharing where enabled. Vary sparse/rich/conflicting context, existing reusable work, scale, permission role, source format and interruption point. Include different organizational objectives as inputs without turning any one into scope.

Each case records: request; authorized starting state; source revisions; enabled tools and policies; expected invariants; acceptable result properties; forbidden effects; required evidence; and applicable O/H/K/E identifiers. Fix time, locale and tool responses for deterministic cases. Use synthetic or authorized de-identified records for reusable fixtures; do not copy production personal data or credentials into tests/traces.

- Separate development cases from held-out qualification cases. Keep adversarial and regression cases after a failure is fixed. A case added to training/prompt-tuning ceases to be held-out evidence.
- Code graders check schema, permissions, graph invariants, arithmetic, source identity, preservation and final state. Test retrieval against curated relevant sources, including missed evidence and incomplete coverage; finding one valid source does not prove recall.
- Human-calibrated model graders may assist subjective usefulness assessments. They receive only authorized, minimized evidence; their output is neither an access decision nor sole proof of correctness. Spot-check disagreement and failures against human review. Do not ask the generating run to certify itself.
- Repeat stochastic cases with the same inputs/configuration. Record every attempt, including refusals, timeouts and failures. Report per-capability/per-route results and sample sizes; averages cannot hide a failing capability or discarded runs. One successful run is demonstration, not reliability evidence.
- A retry that eventually succeeds still counts its first failure, total latency, calls and cost. Measure first-attempt usability separately from recovery. Parallel agents or extra judgments must show benefit against added cost/latency before adoption.

This follows [OpenAI agent evaluations](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agent-evals) for repeatable datasets and trace inspection, and [Anthropic agent evaluation guidance](https://www.anthropic.com/research/demystifying-evals-for-ai-agents) for grading outcomes, repeated trials and calibrated evaluators. These sources guide the method; Coach House's invariants and qualification thresholds remain product-owned.

#### E5. Interaction and performance measurement

Measure the actual implementation, not a screenshot or model response alone. During development, measure affected interactions and reuse valid shared-surface evidence; representative integrated qualification remains required before release. Straightforward spacing/copy/style changes use focused inspection and existing accessibility/baseline checks, not new tests that merely mirror markup. Add a regression test when changed behavior or a concrete recurring failure warrants it; required repository visual baselines still apply. Use representative mobile and desktop viewports, keyboard-only operation, screen-reader semantics, reduced motion, slow network and production-like builds. Record device, viewport, build mode, dataset size and network conditions. A fixture's speed cannot establish the full app's speed.

| Measurement | Acceptance rule |
| --- | --- |
| Immediate feedback | Instrument user input to visible acknowledgement; section 5 targets ≤100 ms, with visible pending feedback by 400 ms when work continues. Report p50/p95 and failures. Do not relabel a loading shimmer as a useful AI result. |
| AI timing | Record request admission, first useful validated preview, terminal generation, proposal save and canonical apply separately. H7's timeout is an upper work bound, not a usability promise. Benchmark quality alongside latency and cost. |
| Save and recovery | Measure save-to-confirmed-readback and failure-to-recoverable-state. Successful optimistic display does not stop the persistence clock. Count timeouts and unknown outcomes separately from successful latency. |
| Animation | Section 5's 160–240 ms transition target and existing camera behavior remain distinct. Interaction stays interruptible; reduced motion preserves meaning without required movement. Pending animation cannot hide a save failure or move focus unexpectedly. |
| Layout and traversal | Exercise small, typical and near-cap graphs. Expanded bounds include assignments. No overlap makes required controls unreachable; manual/locked geometry and reading order survive navigation. Public mobile layout does not depend on canvas coordinates. |
| Accessibility | Touch targets ≥44×44 CSS px; desktop actions follow the shared 28px minimum and U3 focus/hit-region rules; visible focus, labeled status not color alone, usable keyboard alternatives to dragging, no focus traps and preserved drafts on dismissal. Test status announcements and menus in the actual interaction. |

For latency/cost reports, show sample count, success denominator, failures, cache conditions and provider configuration. Performance optimizations must preserve the same E2 correctness checks. Existing limits remain until measured evidence and a compatible schema/migration change justify changing them.

A bounded usability check asks an intended user to generate, locate, edit, recover and share an enabled output without developer guidance. Observe task completion, assistance, mistaken expectations and lost work; do not require screenshot approval as a substitute. Use different objectives; intake review is tested when intake is enabled. A persistent misunderstanding about save, proposal or publication state is a product defect, not merely a copy preference.

#### E6. Existing test seams and their limits

These source observations were checked October 29; this pass did not execute the suites or establish new passing results.

| Existing seam | Evidence it can provide | What it cannot establish alone |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tests/acceptance/workspace-research-provider.test.ts` | Mocked provider/tool-loop behavior, declared calls, budget handling and invalid evidence rejection | Real provider quality, hosted tool behavior, live disclosure containment or actual billing |
| `tests/acceptance/workspace-sharing-database.test.ts` | Transaction/concurrency behavior against a configured test database | A run without `WORKSPACE_SHARING_TEST_DATABASE_URL` skips this suite; skipped is not passing database evidence |
| `tests/browser/workspace-ai/playwright.config.ts` and `revision.spec.ts` | Vite fixture UI interactions, mocked research responses, revision/reload assertions | Authenticated Next.js app, production RLS, live provider or real deployment acceptance |
| O1–O8/H10/K9 source and test pointers | Existing owners and focused seams to extend rather than duplicate | Source inspection is not execution; reference-pack tests are not Coach House integration proof |

Use existing Vitest acceptance projects, relevant database/RLS suites and browser seams for affected behavior. Add full-app checks only where fixture tests cannot prove the required boundary. A live AI qualification uses authorized non-production data, the approved route and a capped budget; it must not send messages, publish or charge an unapproved funding source as incidental evaluation.

Keep existing CI guardrails. During development, run focused checks; use the complete local quality suite only when explicitly requested or diagnosing a concrete CI failure. When tests change, maintain the existing acceptance manifest. Intended visual changes follow repository baseline rules; never weaken thresholds to make a regression pass. None of these runtime checks is needed merely to validate this documentation edit.

#### E7. Evidence receipt

Store a concise receipt in the existing owner checkpoint, not a new tracking system. Include:

- Requirement IDs and enabled capability/route; code commit plus dirty-patch identifier when relevant; schema, prompt/skill, tool registry, fixture and evaluator versions.
- Environment and test command; date; pass/fail/skip counts and reasons; expected versus observed state; sanitized trace/artifact pointers. Never include credentials, bearer tokens, hidden reasoning or unnecessary personal data.
- For AI runs: actual provider/model/configuration, attempt count, tool calls, usage/cost certainty, tariff version, per-dimension scores and unresolved limitations. Unknown usage is unknown, not zero.
- For effects: independently read canonical IDs/revisions/receipts and verify unrelated-state preservation. Check both allowed and denied identities. The same mocked response supplying both action and verification is not independent persistence evidence.
- Which gates remain: local, hosted CI, review, deployment, live provider and live product acceptance. Reuse receipts while their claims remain valid. Relevant changes to code, tests, schemas, configuration, dependencies, environment or observed failures invalidate affected evidence; unrelated edits alone do not. Mandatory current-revision release checks still rerun as required. Reference the existing receipt instead of copying it; failed, skipped and inconclusive checks cannot become passing evidence through reuse.

#### E8. Release interpretation

Complete-plan approval precedes application implementation; a completed documentation pass does not authorize it. Section 9 remains the sole dependency sequence. An enabled capability is ready only when its output contract, applicable U1–U12 UI rules, required D decisions, applicable deterministic checks, qualified AI behavior where used, integrated interaction and recovery are evidenced. Block the failing capability or repair it; do not average safety failures away or claim the full MVP by silently removing required scope.

Required hosted `quality` and branch-protection checks must pass for the current PR revision. Review, deployment and live acceptance remain separate; passing local tests does not authorize or prove them. Migration/backward-compatibility and rollback/recovery must be verified when a slice changes stored contracts. Non-destructive disabling of an AI route must preserve existing manually editable work.

**Acceptance checkpoint:** product behavior, evaluation method and evidence boundaries are approved. No new runtime pass results or quality baselines are claimed. D3/D4 require source/retention qualification; D6 requires measured tariff quantities; numerical AI qualification thresholds need measured baselines. Section 9 S01–S11 now binds these acceptance IDs to execution packets under the existing dependency order.

## Architecture decision and dependency boundary

**Ownership direction is defined in section 2 A1–A5.** Application-owned capabilities and canonical commands govern agent proposals, registered Particle presentation and replaceable event transport. No second graph database, duplicate record store or arbitrary code-generating UI engine is required.

**Dependency selection is recorded in B1–B5:** adapt json-render core/React 0.21.3 for bounded generated presentation and AG-UI core/client/encoder 1.0.3 for run-scoped transport; defer full CopilotKit 1.83.0 and experimental Jev composition. No dependency was installed. Existing source owners, React Flow/ELK, research and credit infrastructure remain authoritative. D6 separately governs inference, funding and processing; package selection does not activate a provider. Conditional judgment remains optional under section 6.

## 12. Selective reuse: Knowledge Canvas

**Informative provenance only:** the filenames/reuse notes below record the research history. Product behavior is specified in sections 2–8/13 and acceptance in sections 10/14; no implementation requirement may consist solely of “follow the reference”. Do not add a dependency on an external folder. Any future code adaptation must preserve attribution inside the project; no such import is required to read or apply this specification.

> **[COMPLETE]** Reference inventory, algorithm checks and relevance mapping are recorded. **[NEEDS ORGANIZING]** Proposed ports must fit existing Coach House owners. No reference code has been imported into the application.

### What this reference actually supplies

Local reference: `/Users/calebhamernick/Downloads/knowledge-canvas-reference` (`K/` below). Its provenance names Floorplan commit `700645bc6f7934cb871b7f7dc7c49262aeb37746`.

The pack contains 63 files: extracted CSS/tokens, interaction specifications, proposed TypeScript/JSON contracts, synthetic examples, small dependency-free JavaScript algorithms and tests. It is **not a React component package, production server, source parser, collaboration backend, or complete editing system**. Its upstream source was statically inspected; this review did not run the upstream application.

Use it as a selective reference library. Coach House stays the application and data authority. Import/adapt only named pieces through existing feature boundaries. Keep upstream attribution and `K/00-source/LICENSE-floorplan.txt` with copied/adapted upstream material; record the pinned source and local modifications. The pack's code examples and proposed contracts are distinguished from extracted upstream code in `K/00-source/ATTRIBUTION.md`.

Disposition means a planning recommendation unless explicitly marked user-approved. “Port” still requires Coach House types and trust-boundary validation.

### Do not transplant

- Floorplan's SVG/foreignObject renderer, local server, code scanners, global extension object, second composer/inspector, or provider subprocess orchestration. Existing React Flow and Coach House owners cover those boundaries.
- Codebase vocabulary such as calls/RPC/imports as plan relationship types. Preserve dependency, sequence, decision outcome, resource reference and feedback semantics.
- A single canonical `parentId` for every record. One record can participate in multiple plans; containment belongs to a view/group/membership context.
- The reference's permissive dependency-cycle examples. Coach House rejects cyclic plan links; feedback remains explicit and does not become a prerequisite.
- Readable source access as authority to edit. `adapter.mjs` defaults metadata editing on for available records; production capability decisions must come from Coach House authorization.
- `schema-check.mjs` as the production validator. It implements a subset for fixtures. Keep Zod as the runtime/type authority and derive boundary schemas where compatible.
- Raw global CSS tokens, a second size grid, 0.28-opacity actionable text, 36px-only touch targets, arbitrary menu scripts, or new controls just because the reference has them.
- Automatic tours, glossary injection, practice/mastery tracking, code exploration, health dashboards, marketing/signup flows or a plugin marketplace. These are outside the current use case. Any future inclusion needs a specific user need and decision.

## 13. Concrete system rules strengthened by the reference

> **[PROPOSED]** These rules strengthen the target contract; they are not implemented merely by being written. The optional walkthrough requirement is user-approved; its detailed interaction defaults below remain recommendations.

### Identity, projection and evidence

The saved graph is not the rendered graph. Existing records and plan memberships produce view cards; grouping, search, previews and outside-scope connectors are projections.

| Contract | Required fields / invariant |
| --- | --- |
| Record reference | Organization/source owner + stable record ID + kind + source revision; server resolves access |
| Plan membership | Plan ID + record/Particle reference + contextual assignment/order; same source may appear in other plans |
| View card | Scope/placement identity + canonical reference + display role (`record`, `group`, `connector`); no independent authority |
| View connection | Stable key + projected endpoints + semantic kind/direction + supporting raw relationship IDs + provenance |
| Evidence | Source identity/revision + locator + basis (`source`, `authored`, `inferred`) + extraction completeness; claims bind to their evidence |
| View request | Authorized scope + view kind + selected/context references + source/graph revisions + request identity |
| UI result | Discriminated result kind + validated target/configuration + provenance + permitted actions + truthful persistence status |

These are adapter requirements, not a replacement database schema. Preserve current IDs, public URLs and saved content. Build one Zod source of truth for each application boundary and infer TypeScript types; avoid manually maintaining competing JSON Schema and TypeScript definitions.

Group only compatible relationships. Retain all supporting raw links and evidence; do not merge a proposal into accepted work or an inferred connection into an established prerequisite. Counts deduplicate canonical records within their stated scope and exclude unauthorized records **before** projection. A hidden/private record cannot leak through a connector label, count, path, search result or AI context.

A visual route means connection, not causation or execution. Grouped edges cannot be directly deleted as though they were one stored edge: resolve their underlying relationships and preview the exact change. Off-board proxies reveal the authorized source location and do not create extra records, assignments or completion units.

### Typed AI results that show work

Keep the current bounded research generation and semantic validation. Add the shared result envelope around those owners rather than replacing them with the pack's `Answer` format.

- Distinguish existing references from newly proposed items. New items use validated local keys; the application assigns/preserves IDs when committing. Unknown existing references fail closed.
- Bind output to run, organization, selected plan, relevant graph/source revisions and capability version. A graph revision alone does not prove a document, person or permission is unchanged.
- A read-only answer projects authorized existing information. An edit result previews typed commands. Neither may silently execute the other.
- Required affected items remain visible or reachable with an explicit omission indicator. Never truncate away a decision outcome, prerequisite or required review target to meet a card cap.
- Show the plan/table/diff. Default status is zero or one sentence; new generated titles usually 3–5 words; one clarification when necessary. Preserve exact names, facts, dates and user wording. No automatic three-question follow-up panel.
- Stop cancels the identified run. Late results cannot replace newer work or clear another run's pending state. Preserve the prompt, selected context and unsaved manual work.

### Optional guided walkthrough — approved

> **[ANSWERED]** Include optional walkthroughs, per the user's October 28 reply. **[NEEDS BUILD]** Integrate orchestration/return state with existing focus controls. No separate learning system or completion side effect is authorized.

Provide one contextual “Walk through” action for a plan. Direct exploration remains available; no automatic tour or added permanent toolbar.

1. Start from the selected accepted plan or clearly labeled proposal. Capture return scope, camera, selection and keyboard focus. Choose the current plan order/selected route; do not silently decide an unresolved branch.
2. Next/Back focuses an existing visible item using the current camera controller. Show its title, owner where available, and at most one short explanation of purpose or dependency. “Why” reveals sources/details only when requested.
3. An actual connection carries its direction/evidence. A narrative jump is a navigation transition, never a fabricated dependency or permanently saved edge. Alternative paths remain labeled as alternatives.
4. A sequence refers to existing plan membership; it does not turn every document, person or form into a task. Advancing never marks work done, accepts a proposal, changes assignments, submits a form, sends a message, or awards Accelerator completion.
5. Exit restores the prior view/focus. Navigation respects dirty-editor guards. A deleted/inaccessible target gets an explicit unavailable state and safe next/back/exit choices; never show stale restricted content.
6. Bind generated narration to plan and source revisions. A changed plan invalidates affected narration. Cached/session narration is sufficient initially; do not introduce a separately editable “flow” database just to navigate a plan.
7. Keyboard and touch have explicit Next/Back/Exit controls. Reduced motion changes focus immediately with static highlighting. Use a mobile list/stack where the graph is impractical. Displayed `2 of 6` measures navigation only.

### Navigation, layout and motion

Maintain one navigation history across overview, selected plan, item detail and temporary result view. Back returns to the actual prior context, not always the root. Search reveal and walkthrough focus use the same command; no independent camera owners.

Fit considers the live rectangles occupied by Objectives, Opportunities, composer, windows and walkthrough controls. Keep selected content reachable without zooming everything into illegibility. A focused plan projection can reduce visible detail without changing stored memberships or completion.

Use the existing ELK measured-layout pipeline. Include expanded content, assignments and action areas in bounds. A layout identity combines scope, graph/source revision, measurement key and sequence token. Stale success, failure and cleanup are all ignored. Preserve manual/locked positions; preview and accepted-state geometry are distinct. Do not run layout on every streamed token or keystroke.

Apply progressive disclosure to secondary text and metadata; keep titles, status meaning and focus legible. Do not copy the reference's numeric zoom thresholds without checking Coach House geometry. Keep separate visual channels for type, work status, proposal state, selection and focus; green must not mean both “recently changed” and “completed” without another distinguishing cue.

Refresh reconciles IDs and source revisions, preserves drafts and personal view state, and flags stale summaries. A failed analysis is not an empty/healthy result. Authored content is never overwritten by generated descriptions during refresh.

## 14. Reference-derived acceptance checks

Section 9 owns the implementation sequence, reuse mapping and exit evidence. Attach the following cases to those gates and existing suites; they are not additional phases or independently approved features.

The reference reduces design/algorithm work. It does not remove application wiring, security, persistence or validation. No credible percentage/time saving is established from this review.

Additional reference-derived checks to attach to existing suites:

- Two plans reuse one document: unique view keys, shared source identity, independent placement/assignment, correct edit scope.
- Group/reorder/filter/expand: stable projected edge identity, complete authorized provenance, no self-edge artifacts.
- Derived connector/proxy: reveal the actual item; never expose a denied source or increase objective progress.
- Walkthrough: no implicit writes; correct direction/narrative distinction; missing/revoked target recovery; exit restores focus/camera; reduced-motion equivalent.
- AI creates then revises: provisional IDs resolve once; required items are not silently clipped; stale async completion cannot change the current view.
- Existing snapshot reader remains independent of private graph traversal. A new internal projection cannot accidentally become the public payload.

Use focused behavior checks and current fixtures. Do not require screenshot ceremonies or rebuild all 110 candidates before a complete interaction can ship. Required CI, accessibility, authorization and release evidence remain intact.

Keep click-to-select and explicit edit/focus actions. `[REQUIRED]` Inline plan edits use these owners; source content opens its registered editor. |

Organization owner controls accepted-plan changes and proposal review; proposal author privileges remain bounded by existing rules. UI controls are not authority. |

Graph receipts are bounded, not a permanent execution ledger. |
| Acceptance | Create/edit/reorder/reuse/save/reload; retain IDs and unrelated edits; reject stale/unauthorized changes; author cannot approve own proposal without owner authority; navigation cannot mutate progress. Extend existing `workspace-particle-persistence.test.ts` and `workspace-particle-operations.test.ts`; coverage is not claimed passed by this review. |

Response storage is user-selected Coach House or Google Sheets; define canonical ownership and remote-delivery recovery for each adapter. Exact schema/migration is later implementation work. `lib/form-version.ts` and `form-answers.ts` already supply pure version and answer validation. |
| Reader | `[REQUIRED]` Resolve the current published form binding and permitted immutable version on the server. Never trust a supplied organization, version or source ID as authority. Private responses are read only through the authorized dataset adapter in O6. |
| Renderer / editor | `[REQUIRED]` Reuse existing answer controls in an interactive public form; label field errors beside fields and show a receipt only after confirmed persistence. Respondents edit their current unsent answers, not the form definition. |

| Permission | `[REQUIRED]` D4 defines anonymous/member access, form-collaborator response editing, no respondent uploads, retention and correction; D5 defines publication audience. Permission to submit never permits reading others' answers. Server enforcement, RLS, request limits and abuse controls precede exposure. |
| Public projection | `[REQUIRED]` Allowed form fields/instructions and submission acknowledgement only. No response dataset, reviewer notes, private sources or predictable response lookup endpoint is published by association. |

Define whether durable local recovery is permitted for the collected data before storing it on shared devices. Revoked/changed binding rejects new acceptance according to the explicit version policy; no silent submission to a different version. |
| Acceptance | Submit/read back independently; retry produces one response; conflicting replay rejected; invalid/oversized answers rejected; concurrent submissions isolated; revoked access fails; cross-tenant/private reads denied; original version meaning retained after draft edits. Existing pure form tests are starting fixtures, not persistence proof. |
| Remaining | Native storage/RLS, immutable publication, immediate confirmed receipt, canonical corrections and scoped access qualify locally under S08. Remaining: independent restore-safe retention/purge and authenticated/hosted acceptance. Google Sheets immediate intake is deferred for MVP, not complete. D4 policy is answered. |

Authorized form creators/managers/editors can correct answer cells; protect technical IDs/version linkage and record actor/time/changed fields. Corrected answers remain canonical and cannot be overwritten by retrying the original submission. An explicit Google Sheets adapter must preserve its canonical source and permission contract; no automatic dual-master sync, bulk mutation or formula engine. |
| Permission | `[REQUIRED]` Recheck source/field permissions server-side for query and writes. Hidden columns and filtered rows are not an access boundary. Shared-plan access alone does not expose underlying private records. |

| Recovery | `[REQUIRED]` Preserve view settings and unsaved permitted edits; reject stale revisions without silent overwrite. Unknown write outcome requires receipt/readback reconciliation. Restore focus/selection after refetch only for records still accessible. |
| Acceptance | Query/total match independently authorized data; pagination/filtering stable; disallowed fields cannot be written; one edit persists to the source and all references reflect it; concurrent changes conflict safely; empty results differ from denied/unavailable results. Response tests require real O5 storage, not seed-only UI evidence. |
| Remaining | Projects/shared-table/native-response saved views and canonical edits qualify locally; linked Sheets reads qualify with controlled providers. Editable Sheets is deferred for MVP because provider CAS/stable identity/idempotency remains unqualified. Actual read-provider and hosted/full-app acceptance remain. |

### Final planning consistency and coverage review — October 29

**Review scope:** reconciled product requirements, cross-contract dependencies, source/output ownership and acceptance references in this document. Reused the already inspected source inventory; this is not a fresh whole-repository audit, runtime pass or production certification. The original annotated pre-review copies remain in the planning archive; current Desktop/Downloads copies are mirrors, not separate authorities.

**Resolved contradictions:**
- Progress defaults and explicit objective completion now live in section 4 and bind S03/D2; no zero-denominator or automatic time-completion ambiguity.
- P3/D6 distinguish approved monthly/reset/carryover/refund behavior from unmeasured tariff quantities; no old earliest-expiry recommendation overrides included-first consumption.
- P4 form managers/editors can see/correct responses; P2 Manage is qualified by that explicit resource contract. Separate reviewer setup and annotation-only correction are superseded.
- U9's model form catalog follows P4's target menu; the latest November1 checkpoint reports thirteen manual types. Native intake is locally implemented; lifecycle, Sheets and hosted acceptance remain unfinished; manual controls/validation/writes qualify before AI exposes each extension.
- L3 no longer allows pending delivery to appear as a submission receipt or treats answers as uneditable. S08 supplies usable direct response editing; S09 adds saved-query/table composition.
- O1–O8 now each names delivery packets, canonical owners and acceptance links in its existing contract table. No second inventory, separate phase list or new requirement tracker was created.

**Coverage rule:** one requirement has one canonical decision/contract and accountable packet; dependent packets cite it instead of inventing another version. A source feature owns its schema/writer even when several packets integrate it. A capability is complete only with the current create/edit/save/reload/share-where-permitted and recovery evidence; unavailable actions remain explicit. V01–V14/U9 covers presentation bindings, O1–O8 covers source/output paths, S01–S11 covers delivery, E1–E8 covers proof. The future catalog is still deferred, and reference citations are provenance rather than missing external specifications.

**Execution-efficiency reconciliation:** section 9 now defines the four-line packet entry and reusable execution prompt. E1 reuses shared-contract proof with unique-variant/wiring coverage; E4 sets risk-based evaluation cadence; E5 scopes interaction measurements; E7 names evidence invalidators; S11 reuses valid baselines. Required safety and current-revision release gates remain unchanged. No additional phase, tracker or application implementation was authorized.

**Feasibility-review reconciliation:** removed L7's obsolete policy-approval checkpoint. H7 explicitly qualifies combined-output capacity; P3 counts all provider spending toward organization/platform safety limits independently of customer charges; S08 starts with actual Sheets write/correction qualification; B5 requires dependency integration and benefit evidence in the owning packets. Section 9 supplies scoped execution context without a second specification. These are proof obligations, not passed tests or new product scope.

**Remaining before customer activation:** qualify exact source writers/fields/identity/conflict handling, configured backup/receipt/deletion behavior, provider processing/network destinations, measured AI thresholds and tariff quantities. Those are bounded tasks in existing packets, with no claim they passed during planning. Existing source entry points are anchors, not invented implementations. S07 public assets and S09 general saved-view ownership must be confirmed at packet entry before coding; their reader/writer/security obligations are already fixed.

**Approval boundary:** the user approved the six recommended defaults and this planning work, not an already unseen final document or app implementation. Present this reconciled specification for final implementation approval. Exact commercial values remain intentionally unfilled until cost/value evidence supports them; this is a visible launch gate, not an excuse to invent a number or redesign billing. No further broad conceptual expansion or repeated privacy/progress questionnaire is needed.

### Cross-output rules and handoff

- **[ANSWERED] Scope:** support reusable visual planning capabilities, not a named example. D1 is resolved on that basis. Subsequent user decisions answer D2/D5, choose Coach House or Google Sheets response storage, exclude BYOK and defer ChatGPT sign-in. D3/D4 technical qualifications and D6 measured tariff values are tracked above; no unapproved default, provider activation or spending follows from these choices.
- **[REQUIRED] One source of truth:** registration advertises only implemented readers/editors/writers/projections. Unsupported actions return an explicit limitation; no fake Save, Submit, Publish or editable field.
- **[REQUIRED] Composition:** one AI request may propose several output types. Validate each source reference and side effect. A graph commit cannot stand in for a document save, form submission or publication transaction. Record partial outcomes and recovery; never claim whole-request atomicity without an actual transaction.
- **[REQUIRED] Permissions:** discovery, read, edit, propose, approve, publish and submit are separate capabilities. Existing access rules win; the later harness may not broaden them. Recheck on action and resume.
- **[REQUIRED] Evidence:** assess capabilities and their combinations using varied objectives, including sparse context, existing work, conflicts and denied access. No single example defines pass/go/MVP. Acceptance requires persisted/read-back outcomes and meaningful usability, not generated screenshots or valid JSON alone.
- **Lifecycle and tools:** section 6 H1–H10 and K1–K9 bind O1–O8 to run/review/application, recovery, budgets, evidence, tools and disclosure. **Acceptance:** section 10 E1–E8. **Execution:** section 9 S01–S11, beginning with S01 when implementation is authorized. These contracts do not authorize implementation or automatically enable unavailable adapters.
